---
title: Field Types Reference
description: All 27 built-in field types, each with what it holds, its form, its page, its settings, its rules, how lists and tokens read it, and its limits
sidebar:
  order: 3
  label: "Field Types"
---

import { Tabs, TabItem, Aside } from '@astrojs/starlight/components';

Structure has **28 built-in field types**. This page goes through them one at a time, each under the same seven headings, so they can be compared and adjusted one by one. What every field shares is under [Field Settings](#field-settings), [Format and checks](#format-and-checks-v1890), [The Rules box](#the-rules-box-v18910) and [Showing, setting and one per section](#showing-setting-and-one-per-section-v1450). Every `node-reference` setting is in one table under [node-reference](#node-reference).

Structure registers its built-ins in the field-type registry under `moduleId: 'structure'`, and the field editor's **Add Field** menu is built from that registry. The built-ins sit under three headings there, in this order. Types that other modules register join the same menu, labelled with their module id (see [Module-provided field types](#module-provided-field-types)).

| Menu heading | Label in the editor (id) |
|--------------|--------------------------|
| Text & input | Text (`text`), Long Text (`textarea`), List (`list`), Assembled list item (`assembled-list-item`), Number (`number`), Date (`date`), Time (`time`), Date & Time (`datetime`), Timestamp (`timestamp`), Select (`select`, or `multiselect` with **Several can be picked**), Chained Select (`chained-select`), Toggle (`toggle`), Yes / No (`boolean`), Phone (`phone`), URL (`url`), Colour (`colour`), Email (`email`) |
| References | Node Reference (`node-reference`), Connections (`connections`), Entries list (`entries-list`), Assembled list (`assembled-list`) |
| Custom | Secret (`secret`), Field Group (`field-group`), Address (`address`), Name (`name-field`), Hidden (`hidden`), Formula (`formula`) |

Not every type is offered everywhere:

- **A connection type's own fields and the AI tab's fields** don't offer Connections, Entries list, List, Assembled list item, Assembled list or Timestamp.
- **Inside a field group** there is no Connections, Entries list, List, Assembled list item or Assembled list. A Field Group is offered one level deep, only inside a node type's or field template's top-level group (not one kept per section, a connection type's or another module's), and that inner group offers no Field Group, Secret or Formula either (v1.89.27). A connection type's group offers no Timestamp.
- **A link field** (Node Reference) can't be changed into an Entries list or a Connections field: "A link field can't become an Entries list or Connections field. To show cards or a table, use Display."
- **A held field keeps its type.** Once the type has entries, a field that is locked after creating, a Timestamp, an add-only field group or an Assembled list can't change its type or id until an admin uses **Unlock**.

There is no built-in `media` type: a storage module provides it (see [media](#media--provided-by-a-storage-module)). `section-break` is retired (see [Retired: section break](#retired-section-break)).

## At a glance

| Type | id | Holds | Format and checks | Filter | Sort | Set the value | Required | Display choices |
|------|----|-------|-------------------|--------|------|---------------|----------|-----------------|
| Text | `text` | One line of text | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | — |
| Long Text | `textarea` | Text over several lines | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | — |
| Number | `number` | A number | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | Progress bar |
| Email | `email` | Text | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | — |
| URL | `url` | Text | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | — |
| Phone | `phone` | Text (only digits with a display pattern) | ✓ | — | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | — |
| Date | `date` | `YYYY-MM-DD` | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | — |
| Date & Time | `datetime` | `YYYY-MM-DDThh:mm` | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | — |
| Time | `time` | `HH:MM` | — | — | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | — |
| Timestamp | `timestamp` | A moment, recorded by itself | Own box | ✓ | ✓ | — | — | — |
| Select | `select` | One option value, or a link to a record | — | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | Tags/Badges |
| Select (several) | `multiselect` | A list of option values | — | — | — | ✓ | ✓ | Tags/Badges |
| Chained Select | `chained-select` | A list of picked options, as paths | — | — | ✓ | — | ✓ | — |
| Toggle | `toggle` | `true` or `false` | — | — | ✓ | ✓ | Touched | — |
| Yes / No | `boolean` | `true` or `false` | — | — | ✓ | ✓ | Touched | — |
| Address | `address` | An object of address parts | — | — | — | Copy only | ✓ | — |
| Name | `name-field` | An object of name parts | Display order | API only | ✓ | Copy only | ✓ | — |
| Field Group | `field-group` | A list of rows | — | — | — | — | Form only | Table |
| Node Reference | `node-reference` | A record id, or a list of them | — | — | — | Records or a copy | ✓ | Links, Cards, Table |
| Connections | `connections` | Nothing: reads the entry's connections | — | — | — | — | — | — |
| Entries list | `entries-list` | Nothing: shows other records | — | — | — | — | — | Own box: Cards or a table |
| List | `list` | A tree of numbered or bulleted items | — | — | — | — | ✓ | — |
| Assembled list item | `assembled-list-item` | One numbered item with its sub-items | — | — | — | — | ✓ | — |
| Assembled list | `assembled-list` | Records made at each level, and copied or typed wording | — | — | — | — | ✓ | — |
| Colour | `colour` | A colour, such as `#3b82f6` or `rose-500` | — | — | — | ✓ | ✓ | — |
| Hidden | `hidden` | Text | — | — | ✓ | ✓ | Form only | — |
| Secret | `secret` | Text, encrypted | — | — | — | — | Form only | — |
| Formula | `formula` | A worked-out value | Own box | — | ✓ | — | Form only | — |

What the columns mean:

- **Format and checks**: the field editor has the [Format and checks](#format-and-checks-v1890) box for it. "Own box" means the type's own settings box does that job (a Timestamp's **Date format**, a formula's **Decimals**, **Prefix** and **Suffix**).
- **Filter**: the list page can filter by it, once **Filterable** is on in the field's **Display** box. "API only" means a module or API call can filter by it (`dataFilters`), but the list page doesn't offer it.
- **Sort**: the list's table can sort by it, on the server. Other columns sort only among the rows already loaded, or not at all.
- **Set the value**: a value rule can set it (see [Set automatically](#set-automatically-setwhen)). "Copy only" means only by copying another field of the same kind.
- **Required**: ✓ means the form and the server both hold it. "Form only" means the create and edit forms ask for it, but the server doesn't check it, so an API call or another module can save it blank. "Touched" means a switch left untouched counts as blank, and one switched on and off again counts as filled.
- **Display choices**: what the **Display** box's **Display type** offers besides the usual look. A module can add more for any type (`fieldDisplayRegistry`).

## How each type is described

Each type below has these headings, in this order. A heading that doesn't apply says "None".

- **Holds**: what is stored in the entry's data, and what a write must send.
- **On the form**: the input on the create and edit forms, and on the detail page while it is being edited.
- **On the detail page**: how it reads, and its own **Display type** choices.
- **Settings**: the boxes in the field editor that are the type's own. Every field has **Every field**, **Create form**, **Display** and **More**; some also have **Format and checks** and a box named after the type, such as **Select settings**. The shared boxes are described once, under [Field Settings](#field-settings).
- **Rules**: what the field's **Rules** box offers. Unless a type says otherwise, it offers **Visible** or **Hidden** (on the form, the page or both), **In create form** and **On the detail page**, **Required** or **Optional**, **Show a note** and **Collapsible**, and on a node type's field **Change a linked record**. So each type says only whether **Set the value** is offered, what other rules can test about it, and what it can't take.
- **In lists and tokens**: the list page's filter, the table's sort and columns, its merge token `{id}` and the formats a token takes, and what a formula reads.
- **Limits and gaps**: what it doesn't do, or doesn't do yet.

A few types (select, node-reference, connections, formula) end with longer reference sections after these seven, for the detail that doesn't fit in a line.

Three things hold for every type:

- **Blank fields are hidden on the detail page** unless the type's **Show empty fields** is on (Pages tab). A false tick box and 0 count as values.
- **A field-group sub-field** uses the same editor, with fewer settings: no **In create form** or **On the detail page** switches, no **Heading** and no **Display type**. Its Rules box offers **Visible** / **Hidden**, **Required** / **Optional**, **Collapsible**, **Show a note**, **Set the value** (a node type's or field template's group) and **Change a linked record** (a node type's group). See [field-group](#field-group).
- **A field's label** can hold merge tokens, such as `{name}` or `{client->name}`, filled in on the entry's page and in its forms (v1.59.0).

---

## Text Fields

### text

One line of text. The most common field type.

```json
{
  "id": "company_name",
  "type": "text",
  "label": "Company Name",
  "placeholder": "Enter company name",
  "required": true,
  "format": {
    "maxLength": 80,
    "pattern": "^[A-Za-z0-9 ]+$",
    "patternMessage": "Only letters, numbers, and spaces"
  }
}
```

#### Holds

Text. The server applies the field's **Format and checks** to a value a write changes.

#### On the form

A text box. Its **Minimum length**, **Maximum length** and **Pattern** are on the box too, and the form's own check shows the field's message under it and stops the save. With **Suggest values already entered** on, a list of values already used opens under the box as people type (see [Suggest values already entered](#suggest-values-already-entered-v1550)).

#### On the detail page

The text. With **Case** set to UPPER, lower or Title, it shows in that case; it is stored as typed. No **Display type** choices.

#### Settings

- **Format and checks**: **Minimum length**, **Maximum length**, **Pattern** and **Pattern message**, **Case** (As typed, UPPER, lower, Title), and **Message**. The starter templates include **Postcode (4 digits)**.
- **Text settings** (on a node type's or field template's field): **Suggest values already entered**.
- **Create form**: the **Default value** box takes merge tokens, such as `{parent->suburb}`, worked out when the create form opens.

#### Rules

**Set the value** is offered: a fixed text, a copy of another field (text, long text, hidden, email, URL, phone, select, date, date and time, timestamp, time, and a formula through a link or the parent), or a value put together (a merge template or a formula). Other rules can test it with every text test: **Is exactly**, **Is not**, **Contains**, **Begins with**, **Ends with**, **Contains the word**, **Is one of**, **Is blank** and the rest.

#### In lists and tokens

- **Filter**: a search box on the list page, matching anywhere in the text, ignoring capitals.
- **Sort**: yes. **Table column**: yes.
- **Token**: `{company_name}`. `{company_name|label}` gives it in its **Case**; `|initial`, `|initials`, `|initialPlain` and `|initialsPlain` give initials. A formula reads it as text.

#### Limits and gaps

- **Case** only changes how it shows. A stored template (a computed name, slug or description) reads the text as typed unless it asks for `|label`.
- A pattern that could take very long to run is refused when the field is saved (see Pattern safety under [Format and checks](#format-and-checks-v1890)).

### textarea

Long text, over several lines. Its label in the editor is **Long Text**.

```json
{
  "id": "notes",
  "type": "textarea",
  "label": "Notes",
  "placeholder": "Additional details...",
  "size": "large"
}
```

#### Holds

Text, line breaks included.

#### On the form

A text area. **Size** in the **More** box sets its height: Small is 2 rows, Medium (the default) 4, Large 8. Its lengths are on the box; a **Pattern** is checked by the form's own check and the server (a text area has no browser pattern).

#### On the detail page

The text with its line breaks, in its **Case**. No **Display type** choices.

#### Settings

- **Format and checks**: as for [text](#text).
- **Long Text settings** (on a node type's or field template's field): **Suggest values already entered**.
- **More**: **Size**, the only field type that has it.

#### Rules

As for [text](#text): **Set the value** with a fixed text, a copy or a value put together.

#### In lists and tokens

- **Filter**: a search box (matches anywhere). **Sort**: yes. **Table column**: yes, kept to one line.
- **Token**: `{notes}`. As for text.

#### Limits and gaps

- On the detail page the whole text shows; there is no "show more" for a very long one.

### number

A number.

```json
{
  "id": "estimated_hours",
  "type": "number",
  "label": "Estimated Hours",
  "format": { "min": 0, "max": 9999, "step": 0.25, "decimals": 2 }
}
```

#### Holds

A number. A write may send it as numeric text (`"12.5"`); anything that isn't a finite number is refused (`Field "estimated_hours" must be a number`). Its **Minimum**, **Maximum** and **Step** are checked by the form and the server.

#### On the form

A number box, with its minimum, maximum and step.

#### On the detail page

- **Number** (the usual): as **Show as** says: a number (with **Decimals**), money (**Currency**, in the viewer's own format, such as $1,234.50) or a percentage (12.5 shows as 12.5%).
- **Progress bar**: a bar out of **Full at**, else the field's **Maximum**, else 100, with the number beside it.

#### Settings

- **Format and checks**: **Minimum**, **Maximum**, **Step**, **Decimals** (0 to 4), **Show as** (Number, Currency with a **Currency code** such as AUD, or Percent) and **Message**. The starter templates include **Money (AUD)**, **Percent** and **Whole number**.
- **Display**: **Display type** (Number or Progress bar) and, for a bar, **Full at**.

#### Rules

**Set the value** is offered: a number, a copy of another number field (or a formula through a link or the parent), or a value put together, which must come to a number. Other rules can test it with **Greater than**, **Less than**, **Is exactly** and the rest.

#### In lists and tokens

- **Filter**: a from and to pair on the list page, compared as numbers. **Sort**: yes, as numbers. **Table column**: yes, as money or a percentage when it shows so.
- **Token**: `{estimated_hours}`. On a page, a card or a label it reads as it shows (money, a percentage); in a stored template only money reads as shown, and `{estimated_hours|label}` asks for the shown form. A formula reads the plain number.

#### Limits and gaps

- **Decimals** changes only how it shows; the number is stored as typed. Use **Step** to stop more decimal places being typed.
- An older field's `validation` (`min`, `max`) and `numberFormat` (currency) are still read, and are rewritten as `format` when the field is next edited.

### email

An email address.

```json
{
  "id": "contact_email",
  "type": "email",
  "label": "Contact Email",
  "required": true,
  "format": { "domains": ["example.com"] }
}
```

#### Holds

Text. The form and the server check it is an email address (one `@`, something before it, a domain with a dot after it, no spaces, at most 254 characters), and with **Allowed domains** that it is at one of them or under one. A module's own write isn't held to the built-in check.

#### On the form

An email box. With **Suggest values already entered** on, a list of values already used opens as people type.

#### On the detail page

A `mailto:` link.

#### Settings

- **Format and checks**: **Allowed domains**, the text checks (**Minimum length**, **Maximum length**, **Pattern**) and **Message**. The starter template **Email** has no settings of its own: its note is "Must be an email address."
- **Email settings** (on a node type's or field template's field): **Suggest values already entered**.

#### Rules

**Set the value** is offered: a fixed address, a copy of an email, text or hidden field, or a value put together. Other rules can test it with the text tests.

#### In lists and tokens

- **Filter**: a search box (matches anywhere). **Sort**: yes. **Table column**: yes, as a link.
- **Token**: `{contact_email}`, as stored.

#### Limits and gaps

- It checks the address's shape only, not that the address exists.

### url

A web address. Its label in the editor is **URL**.

```json
{
  "id": "website",
  "type": "url",
  "label": "Website",
  "placeholder": "https://example.com"
}
```

#### Holds

Text. The form and the server check it is a web address: `http://` or `https://` and any host, or no scheme and a host with a dot (`example.com`), no spaces, at most 2,048 characters.

#### On the form

A web address box.

#### On the detail page

A link that opens in a new tab, but only when the value starts with `http://` or `https://`. Anything else shows as plain text, so a `javascript:` address never becomes a link.

#### Settings

- **Format and checks**: the text checks and **Message**. The starter template **Web address** adds nothing: "Must be a web address."
- **URL settings** (on a node type's or field template's field): **Suggest values already entered**.

#### Rules

**Set the value** is offered: a fixed address, a copy of a URL, text or hidden field, or a value put together. Other rules can test it with the text tests.

#### In lists and tokens

- **Filter**: a search box. **Sort**: yes. **Table column**: yes, as a link.
- **Token**: `{website}`, as stored.

#### Limits and gaps

- **A value without a scheme isn't a link.** `example.com` passes the check, but the page shows it as text, because only `http://` and `https://` become links. Adding `https://` on save, or linking such a value, isn't done.

### phone

A phone number.

```json
{
  "id": "mobile",
  "type": "phone",
  "label": "Mobile",
  "format": { "template": "builtin:au-mobile", "mask": "#### ### ###", "mustMatchMask": true }
}
```

#### Holds

Text. With a **Display pattern**, the form stores only the digits (and a leading `+`), such as `0412345678`; without one, what was typed. With **Must match the pattern**, the number must have as many digits as the pattern has `#`s: `Must look like #### ### ###`.

#### On the form

A phone box. With a **Display pattern**, what is typed shows through the pattern once typed, and the placeholder is the pattern with zeros. Without one, the placeholder is `0400 000 000`, or `+61 400 000 000` when the older `phoneConfig.format` is `international`.

#### On the detail page

The number through its **Display pattern** (`0412 345 678`), or as typed. Not a `tel:` link.

#### Settings

- **Format and checks**: **Display pattern** (`#` is a digit, anything else shows as written), **Must match the pattern**, the text checks and **Message**. The starter templates are **Australian mobile** (`#### ### ###`), **Australian landline** (`(##) #### ####`) and **International phone** (`+## ### ### ###`).
- **Phone settings** (on a node type's or field template's field): **Suggest values already entered**.
- `phoneConfig` (`format`, `defaultCountryCode`) has no controls. Only `format` is read, for the placeholder, and `defaultCountryCode` is read by nothing.

#### Rules

**Set the value** is offered: a fixed number, a copy of a phone, text or hidden field, or a value put together. Other rules can test it with the text tests.

#### In lists and tokens

- **Filter**: not offered on the list page. **Sort**: yes, as text. **Table column**: yes.
- **Token**: `{mobile}`. On a page, a card or a label it reads through its pattern; in a stored template as stored; `{mobile|label}` asks for the shown form.

#### Limits and gaps

- **No list filter.** Phone isn't one of the types the list page filters by.
- **Not a link.** The page doesn't make the number a `tel:` link.
- A number typed before a pattern was set stays as typed until it is edited.

### Suggest values already entered (v1.55.0)

A `text`, `textarea`, `email`, `url` or `phone` field can offer the values people have already typed, so the same words get used again. Turn on **Suggest values already entered** in the field editor ("As people type, offer the values already entered, the most used first. They can still type anything."). It is stored as `suggestFrom`:

```json
{
  "id": "industry",
  "type": "text",
  "label": "Industry",
  "suggestFrom": { "scope": "section" }
}
```

| Key | Type | Default | What it is |
|-----|------|---------|------------|
| `nodeType` | `string` | this type | **From**: another type to read the values of. Needs `field` |
| `field` | `string` | this field | The text field of that type to read |
| `scope` | `'section' \| 'siblings' \| 'children' \| 'any'` | `'any'` | **Where**, as for [option sources](#options-from-other-records-and-values-already-entered-v1550) |

`{}` suggests from this field on this type, anywhere.

- **On the form.** When the input has focus, a list under it shows up to 8 values that start with what is typed (capitals ignored), the most used first. Up and down move through them, Enter takes one, Escape closes the list, and a click takes one too. Whatever is typed stays: the list never forces a choice, and the server doesn't check the value against it.
- **What counts.** The field's text values on the records the person can read, of the type and its subtypes (those whose field of that id is the same kind of text field), inside the isolation section and the active space, in the scope. Blank values don't count, and a value is trimmed. The values are counted over the 5,000 most recently changed records that match, and at most 200 come back. A secret field is never read.
- **Where it works.** Top-level fields of node types and field templates. A field-group sub-field (`a field inside a group cannot suggest values already entered`) and a connection type's field (`the fields of a connection type cannot suggest values already entered`) are refused.
- **What a save refuses.** `suggestFrom` on another kind of field (`only a text field suggests values already entered`), a key other than the three, a `nodeType` without a `field` (`its suggestions: name the field of that type too`), or a `scope` that isn't one of the four. An admin save also refuses a type that isn't there, and a field that isn't a text field of that type (`… reads "x", which is not a text field (values already entered are read from text fields only)`); an import checks the shape only. A suggestion never reads a reference field, although a select's "Values already entered" can since v1.58.0: the input doesn't ask for one.

---

## Date & Time

### date

A calendar date.

```json
{
  "id": "start_date",
  "type": "date",
  "label": "Start Date",
  "required": true,
  "format": { "earliest": { "today": 0 }, "dateStyle": "long" }
}
```

#### Holds

A date as `YYYY-MM-DD` (`2025-03-15`). A request write that changes it to anything else is refused: `Field "start_date" must be a date (YYYY-MM-DD)`. A module's own write is stored as given, with a warning. **Earliest** and **Latest** are checked by the form and the server.

#### On the form

A typed date box with a calendar beside it (v1.78.0).

#### On the detail page

The date alone, never a time: `13 Nov 2026` by default, or as **Show as** says (`13/11/2026`, `13 November 2026`, or the viewer's browser's own form). A value stored with a time zone shows the viewer's day. No **Display type** choices.

#### Settings

- **Format and checks**: **Earliest** and **Latest** (Any date, A date, Today, or Days from today, where a minus is before today), **Show as** and **Message**. The starter template is **Date only (13 Nov 2026)**.
- **Create form**: **Default value** takes `YYYY-MM-DD`.

#### Rules

**Set the value** is offered: a fixed date (`"2026-03-15"`), a copy of another date field, or a value put together that starts with a date. Other rules can test it with **Is before today**, **Is today**, **Is after today**, **Is within the next … days** and **Is more than … days ago** as well as the usual tests. The forms and the page use the viewer's date; badges and the server's checks use the server's.

#### In lists and tokens

- **Filter**: a from and to pair on the list page, compared as dates. **Sort**: yes, as dates. **Table column**: yes, as a date.
- **Token**: `{start_date}` reads as shown (`13 Nov 2026`) on a page, a card or a label, and as stored (`2025-03-15`) in a stored template. `{start_date|long}` and the other presets give `14 March 2026`, `14 Mar 2026` (`medium`), `14/03/2026` (`dmy`), `03/14/2026` (`mdy`), `2026-03-14` (`iso`), `March 2026` (`monthYear`) or `2026` (`year`). A formula reads `YYYY-MM-DD`, for `age()`, `daysBetween()` and `formatDate()`.

#### Limits and gaps

- **Earliest** or **Latest** from today allows one day either side on the server, because the server's today can be the browser's yesterday or tomorrow.
- Date tests against today can't set a stored value: they aren't offered in **Set the value** or a linked-record rule.

### datetime

A date and a time. Its label in the editor is **Date & Time**.

```json
{
  "id": "appointment",
  "type": "datetime",
  "label": "Appointment",
  "required": true
}
```

#### Holds

A date and time as `YYYY-MM-DDThh:mm` (`2025-03-15T14:30`); seconds and a time zone are allowed. A request write that changes it to another shape is refused: `Field "appointment" must be a date and time (YYYY-MM-DDThh:mm)`.

#### On the form

A date box and a time box, with a calendar for the day (v1.89.3; before, one browser box, which held nothing until its time was filled in too). A date with no time is stored at `00:00`, as a day picked from the calendar is. A time typed before the date waits for it.

#### On the detail page

As the viewer's browser shows a date and time, unless **Show as** picks a style, such as `13 Nov 2026, 2:30 pm`. No **Display type** choices.

#### Settings

- **Format and checks**: **Earliest**, **Latest** (they test the date part), **Show as** (As the viewer's browser shows it, or a date style with the time) and **Message**. The starter template is **Date and time**.
- **Create form**: **Default value** takes `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM`.

#### Rules

**Set the value** is offered: a fixed value, or a copy of another date-and-time field or a Timestamp. It can't be put together (a build sets text, a number or a date only). Other rules can test it with the date tests against today, by its date.

#### In lists and tokens

- **Filter**: a from and to pair, compared as dates. **Sort**: yes, as dates. **Table column**: yes, as a date.
- **Token**: `{appointment}` reads as stored, unless **Show as** is set (then as shown on a page or card). The date presets work (`{appointment|long}`), on its date. A formula counts it by its date.

#### Limits and gaps

- A time without a date can't be stored: the time box waits for a date.
- The date presets show the date only; there is no token format for the time.

### time

A time of day, with no date.

```json
{
  "id": "office_hours_start",
  "type": "time",
  "label": "Office Hours Start"
}
```

#### Holds

`HH:MM` (`09:00`).

#### On the form

The browser's time box.

#### On the detail page

As stored (`09:00`). No **Display type** choices.

#### Settings

- No **Format and checks** box and no settings box of its own.
- **Create form**: **Default value** takes `HH:MM`.

#### Rules

**Set the value** is offered: a fixed time or a copy of another time field. Other rules can test it with the usual tests, compared as text.

#### In lists and tokens

- **Filter**: not offered on the list page. **Sort**: yes, as text. **Table column**: yes.
- **Token**: `{office_hours_start}`, as stored. A formula reads it as text.

#### Limits and gaps

- **No check on the server.** The server doesn't check that a value is a time, so an API call or a module can store any text.
- **No format.** It always shows as `HH:MM`, with no 12-hour option.
- **No list filter**, and no earliest or latest time.

### timestamp

A moment the record (or a field-group row) records by itself: when the form was opened, first typed in, or submitted. Nobody types it. Added in v1.72.0.

```json
{
  "id": "received_at",
  "type": "timestamp",
  "label": "Received",
  "timestampConfig": { "records": "opened", "format": "long" }
}
```

#### Holds

A moment as an ISO date-time (`2026-11-16T03:04:05.000Z`), written once, when the record or row is created, and never changed.

- **The form was submitted** (`records` left out, the default): the server's own clock. Anything a write sends is ignored.
- **The form was opened** (`opened`) and **Someone first typed in the form** (`typed`): the browser's claim. A claim that is malformed, more than 2 minutes ahead or over 24 hours old is replaced by the server's time. A write that comes from no form (the public API, for example) leaves these blank.
- A module's in-process or system write may give its own valid value.
- After the record exists, a write that sends a different value keeps the stored one, without an error.

#### On the form

Not on the create form. On the edit form and the page it shows read-only. In a field group, each row records its own, and shows once the row is saved.

#### On the detail page

In its **Date format**: the viewer's browser's date and time, or one of the date presets (`14 March 2026` and the rest). No **Display type** choices.

#### Settings

- No **Create form** box, and no **Permissions** (nobody types it).
- **Timestamp settings**: **Records when** (The form was opened, Someone first typed in the form, The form was submitted: "Nobody types this. It records once, when the record is created, and never changes. In a field group, each row records its own.") and **Date format** (Date and time, or a date preset).
- No **Format and checks** box: **Date format** does that job.

#### Rules

**Set the value** isn't offered: "a timestamp is stamped by itself." It is never required. Other rules can test it with the date tests against today and the usual tests.

#### In lists and tokens

- **Filter**: a from and to pair, compared as dates. **Sort**: yes, as dates. **Table column**: yes, as a date.
- **Token**: `{received_at}` gives the ISO text; the date presets work (`{received_at|long}`). A formula counts it by its date.
- The built-in **Created** and **Last changed** (`{created_at}`, `{updated_at}`) are shown the same way on the detail page when switched on.

#### Limits and gaps

- **Only new records get one.** A record created before the field was added stays blank for good; there is no backfill.
- **Held once the type has entries.** Its type, id and removal need an admin's **Unlock**, as a locked field does.
- Not offered on a connection type's own fields or the AI tab, and a connection type's save refuses one: `the fields of a connection type cannot be a Timestamp or locked after creating`.
- **Not an independent timestamp.** It is the browser's or the server's clock. Timestamps from an outside authority are a type's History setting: see [Independent timestamps](/modules/structure/usage/#independent-timestamps-v18936).

---

## Selection

### select

One choice from a list of options.

```json
{
  "id": "priority",
  "type": "select",
  "label": "Priority",
  "placeholder": "Select priority...",
  "options": [
    { "value": "low", "label": "Low" },
    { "value": "medium", "label": "Medium" },
    { "value": "high", "label": "High" },
    { "value": "critical", "label": "Critical" }
  ]
}
```

Each option has a `value` (what an entry stores) and a `label` (what people see).

#### Holds

One option's value, as text (`"high"`). The server checks it is one of the field's option values; a field with no options takes any text. With **Allow Other**, a value that is none of the options is the typed Other text: not blank, at most 200 characters (`Field "priority": Other can be at most 200 characters`). With options from other records, a pick can be a link to a record (`node:` and its id) or copied text (see [Options from other records and values already entered](#options-from-other-records-and-values-already-entered-v1550)). Since v1.89.24 the Other choice can have its own label (**Its label** under **Allow Other**, `otherLabel`, up to 40 characters), for example **Notes**: it shows as "Notes…", and its text box and the list filter say it too.

#### On the form

Structure's dropdown (`StructurePicker`, v1.89.7), with a search box when there are more than 7 options, and each option's description as a second line. With **Allow Other**, the list ends with an Other choice and a box to type in. With options from other records, a searchable list instead, with **Clear the choice**.

#### On the detail page

- **Text** (the usual): the option's label as a badge, with an info button when the option has a description, source or link. A value that is no longer an option shows as stored.
- **Tags/Badges**: each pick as a coloured pill, with a colour chosen per option under **Colours** (`displayConfig.pills`).
- A pick of a record shows the record's current name, linked to its page, with its [badge](#the-badge-v1540).

#### Settings

- **Select settings**: **Several can be picked** (on, the field is a [multiselect](#multiselect)), **Options** (each row has its label and value, an **Icon** and a **Colour** button, **More** with Description, Source, Link and Picture, move up and down, and delete; **Add an option** is under the list), **Add options from…** (records of a type, or values already entered; on a node type's or field template's field), **Allow Other** ("Adds an Other choice with a box to type in. What's typed is saved as the answer (up to 200 characters).", with **Its label** and, where it applies, **Other adds a new record**), and **Shown as** (see [Choices as cards, rows or tags](#choices-as-cards-rows-or-tags-v18930)).
- **Create form**: **Default value** takes an option value or label.
- **Display**: **Display type** (Text or Tags/Badges) and **Colours**.
- No **Format and checks** box.

In the admin field editor an option's `value` is worked out from its label (lower case, with dashes) until you change it: "The value is what an entry stores. It's worked out from the label (lower case, with dashes) until you change it. Changing a value doesn't change entries that already chose it." On a write the server checks that the value is one of the declared option values. A field with no options accepts any value.

#### Rules

**Set the value** is offered: one of the option values (a field with no options takes any text), or a copy of a select, text or hidden field. It can't be put together. Other rules can test it with **Is one of** and **Is not one of** (picked from the typed options), **Is blank**, **Is not blank** and the text tests. A type's [badge](#the-badge-v1540) can show a select field's value, with its own text and colour per option (`fromField`).

#### In lists and tokens

- **Filter**: a dropdown on the list page, with **Other (typed in)** when **Allow Other** is on. **Sort**: yes, by the stored value as text. **Table column**: yes, as a badge with the option's label.
- **Token**: `{priority}` gives the stored value (`high`), and `{priority|label}` (v1.43.0) its label (`High`). Build it shows a select by its label unless you pick **Its stored value**. A formula reads the value.

#### Limits and gaps

- **The table sorts by the stored value**, A to Z, not in the options' order. (A link field's Cards or Table ordered **By a field** does use the options' order.)
- **Changing an option's value** doesn't change entries that already chose it; they show the old value as stored until edited.
- **A condition or filter can't pick a record** among options from other records yet (see below).

#### Choices as cards, rows or tags (v1.89.30)

A select, multiselect or chained select can offer its options as cards, rows or tags instead of the dropdown. The setting is on the field's settings, beside its options.

- **Shown as:** **Dropdown** (the usual), **Cards**, **Rows** or **Tags**. A chained select also offers **Cards with sub-options inside**.
  - **Cards:** a grid of cards. Each card shows its picture across the top, or its icon, then its label and a two-line description. **Card size** (small, medium or large) sets how wide they are. Since v1.89.44 a card shows no radio button or checkbox: the card itself is the choice (its border and colour, and a check while picked; from the keyboard, a focus ring).
  - **Rows:** one full-width row per option: a small picture or icon, the label, and the description at the end.
  - **Tags:** pills that show the option's icon (or + when it has none) while off, and a check while on.
  - **Cards with sub-options inside** (chained select): each top-level option is a card listing its sub-options as buttons. One click on a sub-option picks its card and that sub-option; picking under another card clears the first card's picks. A card with no sub-options is picked by clicking it. Levels below the second show under the cards as before.
- **One pick** (a select, a chained select's **Pick one**): clicking the picked choice again clears it, unless the field is required. A multiselect's choices and a **Pick several** level toggle.
- **Each option's row** has an **Icon** button (the icon picker, with None) and, since v1.89.31, a **Colour** button (the app's quick palette, with No colour). The icon shows in the colour on cards, rows and tags, and a picked choice is tinted in it. Its **More** panel has a **Picture** (a web address, `https://` or `http://`, or a path of the app starting with `/`); on a card, a picture shows instead of the icon. Stored as the option's `icon`, `colour` and `image`.
- **Allow Other** adds an Other choice last, with its box under the choices. Options from other records or values already entered show as label-only choices.
- **On the page:** **The value** (the usual) or **The choices, click to change** (top-level fields, when not Dropdown). The detail page then shows the choices with the picked ones marked. When the viewer can change the field in place, a click saves straight away, as an In place save does: value rules and worked-out fields go with it, and a refusal shows under the field. This works whether or not the type turns In place editing on. Otherwise the choices are read-only.
- **Where it shows:** the create and edit forms, In place, Add details, quick create and field-group rows. In a group with the Table layout, cards and rows show as the dropdown (tags stay). Lists, tables, cards, filters, exports and merge fields show the value as before; the stored value never changes shape.
- **Fields from other modules** get the same settings in their **Display** box (stored in `integrations.fields[<key>].choiceDisplay`).
- **Colour shows on** (v1.89.44): **The icon** (the usual: the icon in the option's colour, the choice tinted while picked) or **The background** (each choice washed in its option's colour, more strongly with a border while picked; the icon keeps the text colour). Stored as `choiceDisplay.colourOn: 'icon' | 'background'`.

Stored as the field's `choiceDisplay`:

```json
{ "as": "cards", "size": "medium", "onPage": "choices" }
```

`as` is `dropdown`, `cards`, `rows`, `tags` or `nested-cards` (chained select only); `size` is `small`, `medium` (the default) or `large`; `onPage` is `value` or `choices`; `colourOn` is `icon` (the default) or `background`. The server refuses it on other field types, an unknown key or value, and `onPage` on a field group's sub-field. An option's `icon` is letters, digits and `-`, starting with a letter, at most 64 characters; its `colour` a palette or theme colour name, a hex code, or an oklch(), rgb() or hsl() colour; its `image` at most 2,000 characters, with no `//` start, backslash or control characters.

**Not covered:** uploading a picture (give its address), cards and rows don't show an option's info button (its source and link), and the dropdown list shows no icons or pictures.

#### Other adds a new record, and several Others (v1.89.33)

**Other adds a new record.** On a select or multiselect whose options come from records (say a **Body clue** type), **Allow Other** has a switch **Other adds a new record**. What's typed under Other becomes a new record of that type, named what was typed, and the pick is that record. If the records name several types, **New records are** picks the type.

- On save, the server first looks for a record of that name among the field's records (case and spacing aside; the oldest match wins), so it doesn't make a duplicate. Otherwise it creates the record in the same save, with the type's defaults, placed as a link field's new entry is (under the entry where the hierarchy allows, else in the entry's section). The pick is stored as the source stores it: `node:` and its id, or the name as text. The same name typed twice in one save makes one record.
- Only someone who may create entries of that type sees Other; the server refuses anyone else ("Not permitted to create…").
- The new record is then an ordinary choice for everyone, in the dropdown, cards, rows or tags.
- The switch isn't available (one line says why), and the server refuses it, when: the options don't come from records; the records have an "Only records that…" condition; their scope is beside this entry, under this entry or records this entry links to; they're stored as text with a label of their own; the field is a group's sub-field or a connection type's; or the type opens a section of its own, works out its own name, or needs fields a name doesn't fill.
- Without the switch, a field with options from records still offers no Other.

**Several Others** (multiselect, `otherMultiple`). Up to 20 typed values, each its own answer: a box each with **Add another** and remove (or chips with one box, in Cards, Rows and Tags). Each must be non-blank and at most 200 characters, and duplicates (case and spacing aside) are kept once. With **Other adds a new record**, each typed value makes or matches its own record.

Stored as `otherAddsRecord: true`, `otherRecordType: '<type slug>'` (only when the records name several types) and `otherMultiple: true`. Turning **Allow Other** off clears all three.

**Limits:** at most 50 new records per save, counting a link field's new entries; matching uses the record's name, not a label of its own; a select kept per section can't use it; inside a link field's "new entry" form, Other doesn't add records.

#### One Select, one or several picks (v1.89.41)

The editor has one **Select** (before, Select and Multi Select were offered apart). Turn on **Several can be picked** (at the top of **Select settings**) to let people pick more than one. The stored types stay `select` and `multiselect`, so nothing stored changes.

- Turning it on or off keeps the field's options, sources, Other, display, format and rules. The default value and fixed rule values switch between one value and a list of one.
- Turning it off clears **Several Others** ("Several Others was turned off.") and a default with several picks ("The default value had several picks, so it was cleared."). It's refused while a rule sets several picks ("A rule sets several picks, or none. Change it first."), and the save is refused while entries hold more than one pick: `"Tags": 12 entries have more than one pick. Change them first, or keep Several can be picked on.` (top-level values, field-group rows, inner groups and per-section values are all counted; nothing stored is rewritten). The switch is greyed out while the field is held (see **A held field keeps its type** above).
- It works for top-level fields, field-group sub-fields, field templates and connection types; another module's fields keep their own type.
- **Old values read right:** a Select holding a list of one pick reads as that pick (the form, the page, tables, badges and the **Is** / **Is not** list filters), and a multiselect holding a single value reads as a list of one. A formula or merge-token comparison still sees a list of one as a list until the entry is saved with a new pick.

#### Merge fields in option labels (v1.89.18)

- An option's **Label** can hold merge fields read from the entry, such as `{parent->name}` or `{other_parent->residential_address}`. The Label box has the **Merge fields** button. A Select only (one pick or several), not a Chained Select.
- The entry stores the option's **value**, never the worked-out text, so a renamed person or a changed address never breaks saved entries, rules or filters. A new option's value is made from the label's words with the tokens left out (else `option-N`).
- **Worked out from the entry** on the form's pickers, the detail page and edit in place (Tags/Badges pills too), table cells and entries-list pills, and by `{field|label}` / `{ref->field|label}` in merge templates, stored computed values and badges. A label that comes to nothing shows its words without the tokens, else the value.
- **Shown as written** where no single entry is in view: the list's filter menu, the Conditions dialog's value picker and the admin lists.
- **Checked on save** like a saved template: a label may not read a secret (its own or a linked entry's) or a viewer token.
- **Limits:** a stored value that reads `{field|label}` is right as of the entry's own last save; it isn't redone when only the linked record changes.

#### Options from the entry's own links (v1.89.18)

- **Options from records** has a **Where** of **Records this entry links to** (`scope: 'links'`), with **Link fields** (`linkFields`, 1 to 20): the type's link fields to read. The options are the records those fields hold, in the form including picks not saved yet. Picking the first link field fills in the types from what it links to. Label, store (a link `node:<id>` or text), connect and filter work as for the other places.
- Example: a matter with **Parent** and **Other parent** link fields, and a select *Children live with* reading both, labelled `{residential_address}`: it offers each parent's address and stores the parent.
- **Checked on save:** a pick must be a record held in those link fields as the same save leaves them; anything else is refused ("not one of the records this field offers"). Read rules and the section wall apply as before. Each link field named must be a link field of the type.
- **Limits:** removing a parent doesn't clear a select already saved with them (only changed values are checked); a record typed straight into a link field as new is offered once saved; at most 100 linked records.

#### Descriptions, sources and links on options (v1.53.0)

Every option of a `select`, `multiselect` or `chained-select` can carry rich content, for a choice that needs explaining but isn't enough to be a node type of its own:

```json
{
  "value": "high",
  "label": "High",
  "description": "Needs an answer within a day.\nTell the team lead.",
  "source": "Service guide, section 4",
  "link": "https://example.com/service-guide#4"
}
```

| Key | Type | Limit | What it is |
|-----|------|-------|------------|
| `description` | `string` | 2,000 characters | Plain text, several lines. Never read as HTML or markdown |
| `source` | `string` | 200 characters | A short citation: a document, section or website name |
| `link` | `string` | 2,000 characters | An `http:`, `https:` or `mailto:` address, and nothing else |

- **In the pickers** (a select's dropdown, a multiselect's buttons and every level of a chained select), the description shows as a muted second line under the label, cut to two lines.
- **On the Info tab, the detail page and a page layout's fields cards,** a picked option with any of the three has a small info button beside its label, named "About" and the option's label for screen readers. It opens a popover with the description, then "Source:" and the source (linked when the option has a link too), or the link alone, shown as its host name (or the address, for `mailto:`). Links open in a new tab.
- **Tables, cards' text, merge fields and other text** show the label only.
- **In the field editor,** each option row has **More**, which shows **Description** ("Plain text, shown under the option and in its info button"), **Source** ("A document, section or website name") and **Link** ("https://..."), and a **Picture** (see [Choices as cards, rows or tags](#choices-as-cards-rows-or-tags-v18930)). While it is closed, an option that has any of them shows a small marker. A problem shows under the row, for example "This option's link must start with http:, https: or mailto:."
- **Links are checked twice.** The save checks each link with a URL parse, in the admin form and on the server. A page checks it again before it becomes a link, so an older or imported `javascript:` or `data:` address never becomes one.

**What a save refuses.** A `400` naming the field and the option, from every path that saves fields, the imports included (`Field "priority": the option "High": …`):

- `its description must be text`, `its source must be text` or `its link must be text`
- `its description is longer than 2000 characters`, `its source is longer than 200 characters` or `its link is longer than 2000 characters`
- `its link must be a full web address (https://...) or a mailto: address` (a relative address included)
- `its link must start with http:, https: or mailto:`
- for an option's look (v1.89.30, v1.89.31): `its icon must be an icon name: letters, digits and "-", starting with a letter`, `its colour must be a colour: …` and `its picture must be a web address (https://...) or a path of this app starting with /`

#### Options from other records and values already entered (v1.55.0)

A `select` or `multiselect` can offer more than its typed options: the records of a type, the values people have already typed in a text field, and since v1.58.0 the records already linked in a reference field. The admin adds them with **Add options from…** in the field editor (see [Options from other records](/modules/structure/usage/#options-from-other-records-v1550)) and orders them among the typed options. They are stored apart from `options`, as `optionSources`, so everything that reads `options` reads the typed options as before.

```json
{
  "id": "supplier",
  "type": "select",
  "label": "Supplier",
  "options": [{ "value": "none", "label": "No supplier yet" }],
  "optionSources": [
    { "id": "s1", "kind": "records", "position": 1, "nodeTypes": ["supplier"], "scope": "section",
      "filter": { "field": "approved", "equals": "true" } }
  ]
}
```

The picker shows **No supplier yet**, then the approved suppliers in this entry's section, by name. A pick stores a link to the supplier; the field's entry and the supplier get a connection named "Supplier".

**Records of a type** (`kind: 'records'`):

- **`nodeTypes`**: the types whose records are offered, their subtypes too (1 to 50).
- **`filter`**: a condition on the record, in the [reference filter](#only-some-entries-filter-v1420)'s grammar (**Only records that…**). It is checked when offering and again when saving.
- **`scope`** (**Where**): `'section'` the entry's own isolation section ("In this …", named after the app's isolated primary scope type; no shared type); `'siblings'` the records beside the entry, under the same parent; `'children'` the records under it (none while it is being created); `'any'` anywhere the person can see; and, for a records entry only, `'links'`, the records held in its `linkFields` (see [Options from the entry's own links](#options-from-the-entrys-own-links-v18918)). Whatever the scope, only records the person can read are offered, and never across the [isolation section](#inside-an-isolation-section-v1420): an entry in a section reaches its own section's records and the shared types'.
- **`label`** (**Show them as**): a merge template over the record, `{name}` when absent. It can't read a secret field of the record, at most 500 characters.
- **`store`** (**When picked, store**): `'link'` (the default) stores a link to the record, `node:` and its id, which shows the record's current name and follows a rename. `'text'` stores the label's text at the time of picking: no link and no connection.
- **`connect`** (**Also connect the chosen entries**): for a link, on unless `false`. Each picked record gets a connection named after the field, with the same rules as a [reference field's connections](#connecting-the-chosen-entries-v1460): one connection for two entries, removed when the last pick between them goes, and the existing entries brought in step when it is turned on. All of a field's link sources connect, or none do.
- **`connectType`** (**Connection type**, under the switch): the connection type the links use, as for a [reference field](#connecting-the-chosen-entries-v1460). All of a field's link sources name the same one. Left out on an older field, the links keep the field's automatic type.

**Values already entered** (`kind: 'used-values'`): `nodeType`, `field` (a top-level text field of that type: `text`, `textarea`, `email`, `url` or `phone`, never a secret) and `scope`. Its options are the field's distinct values on the records the person can read, the most used first, up to 200, with a search as they type. A pick stores the text.

**Values already entered from a reference field (v1.58.0).** The `field` can also be a top-level `node-reference` field of the type. The options are then the records that field links to on the type's records in the scope: for example, every person already listed in the **Members** field of a team in this section.

```json
{
  "id": "team_member",
  "type": "select",
  "label": "Team member",
  "options": [],
  "optionSources": [
    { "id": "members", "kind": "used-values", "nodeType": "team", "field": "members", "scope": "section", "store": "link" }
  ]
}
```

- **`store`** (**When picked, store**), required for a reference field: `'link'` stores a link to the record, `node:` and its id, exactly as a records entry's link does, so everything above about links applies (the name shown, the strips and the wall, `{team_member->x}` and the recompute). `'text'` stores the record's name at the time of picking. The field editor writes `'link'` unless the admin picks **Its text**.
- **`connect`** (**Also connect the chosen entries**), for a link: on unless `false`, as for a records entry. A field's link entries all connect, or none do, a records entry's and a values entry's alike.
- **What it offers.** The records the field links to, the most linked first, then A to Z, up to 200; past that the picker says "Showing the first 200. Type to find more." The search matches the start of a record's name (capitals ignored), before the bounds, so a record linked only on older records is still found.
- **Whose records.** The scope (**Where**) applies to the records that hold the links. A record they link to is offered when the person can read it (their own or a shared type's, live, of a type they may read, with the owner's unassigned rows), and never across the entry's [isolation section](#inside-an-isolation-section-v1420). When the reference field's every definition read (on the type and its subtypes) lists allowed types, only records of those types and their subtypes are offered or accepted.
- **The type's subtypes** count when their field of that id is a reference field too; a subtype that has a different kind of field there is left out.
- **The bounds.** On top of the 5,000 most recently changed records, only the first 100 items of each record's list count (for the offer and for the write checks alike), and at most 20,000 (record, linked record) pairs, the most recent records' first.

**On the form.** A field with sources opens a searchable list instead of the dropdown or the buttons: each run of typed options, with their descriptions, and each source as its own group (a records source headed by its types' names, a values source by its field's label), in the admin's order. A records source lists the first 50 records that match, then "Showing the first 50. Type to find more."; the search reads the server's list search. A values source that reads a reference field (v1.58.0) lists the records by name, up to 200, then "Showing the first 200. Type to find more." A select has **Clear the choice**. A multiselect's picks show as badges under the button, each with a remove button.

**What a pick shows.**

- **A link** shows the record's current name, linked to its page, with the record's own [badge](#the-badge-v1540) for the viewer: on the Info tab, the detail page, a page layout's fields cards and the table view's column. A record that is gone, or that the viewer can't read, shows "An entry you can't see", never its id. A card's or list row's field line, which is one line of text, counts the links instead ("2 linked entries").
- **A text pick, or a value already entered,** shows as stored.
- **In merge fields,** `{supplier}` and `{supplier|label}` give the linked record's name (a multiselect, each name), and `{supplier->x}` reads field `x` of the picked record, as a reference does. See [A select's picks of records](/modules/structure/merge-fields/#a-selects-picks-of-records-v1550).
- **Everywhere else,** the value is the stored text: the public API, `getNode`, AI Core's record reads, conditions and list filters see `node:` and the id.

**What a write checks.** Only values the write changes are checked; a value already stored passes.

- **Every write,** a system one included: a select's value must be one text, and a multiselect's a list of texts, at most 100. Each must be something one of the field's sources can give: a typed option, a link (when a source stores links), or other text (when a source stores text or reads a text field's values already entered). Otherwise `Field "supplier" has an invalid option ("…")`.
- **A person's or module's write** (not the system's) also checks what each pick names:
  - **a link** must name a live record the writer can read, of one of a link source's types or a subtype, matching its `filter`, in its `scope`, and inside the entry's isolation section. Otherwise `Field "supplier" picks an entry that was not found`, `Field "supplier" cannot pick "Acme": it is not one of the records this field offers`, or `Field "supplier" can only pick entries in the same isolation section, or of a shared type: "Acme" is outside it`;
  - **a text pick** from a source with the plain `{name}` label must be the name of a record in its scope; with a label of its own, it is taken as the copied text, up to 500 characters;
  - **a value already entered** must be held by that field on a record the writer can read, in the scope;
  - **a link from a reference field's values** (v1.58.0) must name a live record the writer can read, inside the entry's isolation section, that the field links to (among a record's first 100 items) on a record the writer can read, in the scope. A link either a records entry or a values entry offers is accepted. Otherwise the same messages as for a records entry's link;
  - **a name from a reference field's values** (v1.58.0, **Its text**) must be the name of such a record. Otherwise `Field "x" has an invalid option ("…")`.
- **The entries a reference field creates** (`$new`) are checked the same way, as their own creates. A duplicate's changed values are checked, and duplicating or restoring an entry keeps its links to the isolation section.

**Links behave like references.** A link in a top-level select or multiselect is treated as a reference wherever Structure looks after references, even after the field's sources change: when a section record is deleted and what it contains is kept, the links between the kept rows and the rows left in Deleted are removed as references are (and on an assign or a move out of it); a move can't carry a link across the isolation section; duplicating a subtree points a link at the copy of its record; and a merge's backfill carries a link over. A stored value that reads through a link (`{supplier->x}`) is worked out again when the linked record changes.

**Conditions and list filters.** **Is one of** tests the typed options, and **Is blank** and **Is not blank** any pick. A condition or filter can't pick a record yet; in JSON, `{ "field": "supplier", "in": ["node:…"] }` compares the stored text.

**What a save refuses** (a `400` naming the field, `Field "supplier": …`; every path that saves fields, the imports included, checks the shape):

- `optionSources` on a field that isn't a select or multiselect (`only a select or multiselect field takes options from other records`), more than 10 (`it has more than 10 option sources`), or two with one id.
- An entry that isn't an object, has no `id`, an unknown `kind`, a key its kind doesn't take (`option source 1 has an unknown setting "x"`), a `position` that isn't a whole number from 0, or a `scope` that isn't one of its kind's (`'links'` is a records entry's only). A `'links'` entry with no link fields, more than 20, or one named twice; `linkFields` on any other scope.
- A records entry with no type (`option source 1 needs at least one type`), more than 50, a malformed `filter`, a `label` that isn't a template or is longer than 500 characters, a `store` other than `link` or `text`, or a `connect` that isn't `true` or `false` or is `true` with a text store (`only a pick stored as a link can connect the entries`).
- A values entry (v1.58.0) whose `store` isn't `link` or `text` (`option source 1: what a pick stores must be "link" or "text"`), or whose `connect` isn't `true` or `false`, or is `true` without a `store` of `link` (`only a pick stored as a link can connect the entries`).
- Link sources that don't agree on `connect`, a values entry that stores links included (`its records entries that store a link must all connect the entries, or none of them`), or on `connectType` (`… must all use the same connection type`). A `connectType` that isn't a slug, or on an entry that stores no link.
- A typed option whose value starts with `node:` (`the option value "node:x" cannot start with "node:" (that marks a link to a record)`).
- On a field-group sub-field (`a field inside a group cannot take options from other records`) or a connection type's field (`the fields of a connection type cannot take options from other records`).
- **On an admin save** of a node type, a proposal or a field template, also: a type that isn't there (`option source 1 names the type "x", which is not one of the node types`), a values source's field that isn't a text field or a reference field of its type (`option source 1 reads "x", which is not a text field or a field that links to records (values already entered are read from those only)`), a values source on a text field that says what a pick stores or connects (`… which is a text field: only a field that links to records takes what a pick stores, or connects the entries`), a values source on a reference field without a `store` (`… which links to records: say what a pick stores (a link to the record, or its text)`), and a label that reads a secret field of the records it shows (`option source 1 may not show its records with the secret field "x": a label shows in pickers and lists`). An import checks the shape only. So an imported values entry on a reference field with no `store` is taken as a text field's: it offers nothing, and its picks are refused.

### multiselect

Several choices from a list of options. Since v1.89.41 the editor shows it as a **Select** with **Several can be picked** turned on (its type label reads "Select (several)"); before, it was **Multi Select**.

```json
{
  "id": "tags",
  "type": "multiselect",
  "label": "Categories",
  "options": [
    { "value": "litigation", "label": "Litigation" },
    { "value": "property", "label": "Property" },
    { "value": "family", "label": "Family Law" },
    { "value": "commercial", "label": "Commercial" }
  ]
}
```

#### Holds

A list of option values (`["litigation", "family"]`). The server refuses a value that isn't a list (`must be a list`) and, when the field has options, any item that isn't one of them. With **Allow Other**, the list may hold one typed Other text as well, or up to 20 with **Several Others** (`Field "tags" can hold at most 20 values typed in`). With options from other records, at most 100 picks, each a typed option, a link to a record or copied text. An older single value (not a list) is read as a one-item list.

#### On the form

Since v1.89.30 it can show as cards, rows or tags instead (see [Choices as cards, rows or tags](#choices-as-cards-rows-or-tags-v18930)).

A row of toggle buttons, one per option, each with its description under its label (v1.53.0). With **Allow Other**, a box to type an Other answer under the buttons. With options from other records, a searchable list instead, with the picks as badges, each with a remove button. With no options: "No options configured".

#### On the detail page

- **Text** (the usual): each pick as a badge with its label and the option's info button.
- **Tags/Badges**: coloured pills, a colour per option.

#### Settings

As for [select](#select): **Select settings** with **Several can be picked** on (**Options**, **Add options from…**, **Allow Other** with **Several Others**, **Shown as**), **Default value** (an option value or label), and **Display type** with **Colours**.

#### Rules

**Set the value** is offered: a list of option values (`[]` allowed), or a copy of another multiselect field. Other rules can test it with **Is one of**, **Is not one of**, **Is blank** and **Is not blank**.

**"Is one of" over a multiselect (v1.53.0).** A condition's `in` (**Is one of**) holds when **any** of the field's picks is listed, and `notIn` (**Is not one of**) when none is. Before v1.53.0 a value with two or more picks was compared as its picks joined with `,`, so it never matched. A value with one pick, or none, compares as before. This holds for every list value: a multiselect, a multiple reference and a chained select.

<Aside type="caution" title="A behaviour change">
Existing **Visible When**, **Required When**, value-rule and reference-filter conditions that test a multiselect or a multiple reference with **Is one of** or **Is not one of** can start to apply on the next save or render: a field may now show, be required, or be set where it wasn't before. Check those conditions after updating.
</Aside>

#### In lists and tokens

- **Filter**: not offered on the list page. **Sort**: no (a list of values). **Table column**: yes, as the labels.
- **Token**: `{tags}` gives the values joined with ", " (`litigation, family`), and `{tags|label}` the labels. A formula reads the list.

#### Limits and gaps

- **No list filter and no sort.** The list page can't filter by a multiselect, and the table can't sort by it.
- **One Other answer unless Several Others is on.** Without it, a multiselect with **Allow Other** holds at most one typed answer beside its picks (see [Several Others](#other-adds-a-new-record-and-several-others-v18933)).

### chained-select

A tree of options (v1.53.0). One top-level option is picked. An option with sub-options asks a follow-up question, and its sub-options are picked one at a time (**Pick one**) or several at a time (**Pick several**). A picked sub-option can have sub-options of its own, to any depth up to six levels. "Chained" just means nested: there is no separate setting.

```json
{
  "id": "area",
  "type": "chained-select",
  "label": "Area",
  "chainedSelectConfig": {
    "placeholder": "Choose an area...",
    "options": [
      {
        "value": "family",
        "label": "Family",
        "heading": "Who it's about",
        "placeholder": "Choose...",
        "children": [
          {
            "value": "children",
            "label": "Children",
            "pick": "several",
            "heading": "Topics",
            "children": [
              { "value": "schooling", "label": "Schooling" },
              { "value": "health", "label": "Health", "children": [{ "value": "dental", "label": "Dental" }] }
            ]
          }
        ]
      },
      { "value": "work", "label": "Work", "description": "Anything to do with a job." }
    ]
  }
}
```

#### Holds

**The value** is a list of text: the full path of every picked option, its ancestors included, the option values joined with `/`. Family, then Children, then Schooling and Health, then Dental under Health, is stored as:

```json
["family", "family/children", "family/children/schooling", "family/children/health", "family/children/health/dental"]
```

- Each value in a path has `%` written as `%25` and `/` as `%2F`, so an older value such as `n/a` is the segment `n%2Fa`. A slug never needs either.
- Each pick is its own item, so a list filter, a condition and a merge field read a pick at any depth as one value.
- **An older value**, one item per level (`["family", "children"]`), is read as one chain (`["family", "family/children"]`) everywhere: the form, the pages, the conditions and the server. A write that changes it stores it as paths, as long as nothing would be dropped. A one-item value means the same in both shapes. Stored rows aren't migrated.
- **The server checks it** as it checks a select's, and only when the write changes it. It must be a list of text, and with options configured each pick must be one of them, under a picked option, with one pick at the top and one under each **Pick one** option. A field with no options takes any list of text. Each refusal is a `400`: `Field "area" must be a list of picked options`, `Field "area" has an invalid option ("family/pets")`, `Field "area" picks "family/children" without the option it sits under`, or `Field "area" picks more than one option where only one can be picked ("work")`.
- **No default.** A chained select still takes no default value.

#### On the form

Since v1.89.30 it can show as cards, rows, tags or **Cards with sub-options inside** (see [Choices as cards, rows or tags](#choices-as-cards-rows-or-tags-v18930)). As a dropdown: the top level is one dropdown. Under each picked option that has sub-options come its heading and its own picker, indented: a dropdown for **Pick one**, or toggle buttons like a multiselect's for **Pick several**, and so on down the tree. Changing a pick clears what was picked under it, and unticking an option clears everything under it. The pickers show each option's description under its label. A field with no options says "No options configured". If the stored picks include options that have since gone, the form says "Some earlier picks are no longer options. They'll be cleared when you change this."

#### On the detail page

Labels, never the stored values (before v1.53.0 the read-only view showed the values). No **Display type** choices.

- **The detail page, the Info tab and fields cards** show a nested view: each run of single picks on one line ("Family › Children"), the picks with nothing under them as badges, several sub-picks indented under the option they sit under, and each option with rich content with its info button.
- **Tables, card text and other text** show each pick with nothing under it as its labels joined with " › ", and the picks joined with "; ": "Family › Children › Schooling; Family › Children › Health › Dental".
- **A pick that is no longer an option** shows its stored text.

#### Settings

The **Chained Select settings** box holds a **Chained select** section and **Shown as** (see [Choices as cards, rows or tags](#choices-as-cards-rows-or-tags-v18930)). No **Format and checks** box, and no **Default value**.

**chainedSelectConfig:**

| Property | Type | Description |
|----------|------|-------------|
| `placeholder` | `string` | The top-level picker's placeholder ("Select..." when absent) |
| `options` | `ChainedOption[]` | The top-level options. One of them is picked |

**Each option:**

| Property | Type | Description |
|----------|------|-------------|
| `value` | `string` | **Required.** What an entry stores. Unique among its siblings, and it can't hold `/` |
| `label` | `string` | **Required.** What people see. Since v1.59.0 it can hold merge-field tokens, such as `{name}` or `{client->name}`, filled in on the entry's page and in its forms (see below). |
| `description`, `source`, `link` | `string` | Its [rich content](#descriptions-sources-and-links-on-options-v1530) |
| `pick` | `'one' \| 'several'` | How its sub-options are picked. Absent: `'one'` |
| `heading` | `string` | Shown above its sub-options, such as "Type of issue" |
| `placeholder` | `string` | Its sub-options' picker's placeholder, for `'one'` |
| `children` | `ChainedOption[]` | Its sub-options |

At most six levels deep and 1,000 options in all. Since v1.89.30 an option may also have an `icon` and an `image`, and since v1.89.31 a `colour`, shown on cards, rows and tags.

**From another module (v1.89.30).** A chained select can also come from another module (a field source). It works like one made here: the value is the list of picked paths, it gets the same checks, and conditions, merge fields, filters and exports read it the same way. Its options are that module's. If the module stops supplying it, **Make them this type's own** turns it into an ordinary chained select, options included.

**In the field editor,** the field's **Chained select** section has:

- **Placeholder**, for the top picker: "People pick one of these. Give an option sub-options to ask a follow-up question."
- **Options**: "The value is what an entry stores. A new option's value is worked out from its label (lower case, with dashes) until you change it. Changing a value doesn't change entries that already picked it, or anything under it: those picks show as stored until they're changed." An option that was there before you started editing keeps its value exactly as typed.
- **Each option row**: its label and value, **Icon** and **Colour**, **More** (Description, Source, Link, Picture), move up and down, and delete. Deleting an option with sub-options asks first, "Delete Family and its 3 sub-options?": "Entries that picked them keep their picks, shown as stored, until they're changed." A row with no sub-options has **Add sub-options**; at the sixth level it says "Options go at most 6 levels deep, so this one can't have sub-options."
- **Sub-options.** A row with sub-options shows their count ("3 sub-options") and expands and collapses, so a wide tree stays readable. Expanded, it has **How are they picked?** (**Pick one** or **Pick several**), **Heading** ("Optional, e.g. Type of issue"), **Placeholder** for Pick one, the sub-option rows (the same rows, one level in, with a smaller indent past four levels), **Add a sub-option**, and **No sub-options** while the list is empty.
- **Add an option** at the end of the top list. At the limit: "This field holds the most options it can (1000, at every level together)." A problem the save would refuse shows under the editor.

**What a save refuses.** A `400`, `Field "area": ` and the problem, from every path that saves fields, the imports included:

- `option 2 needs a label`, or `the sub-option 1 of "Family" needs a label`
- `the option "Family › Children" needs a value`
- `the option "Family › N/A" has the value "n/a": a value cannot hold "/"`
- `two options at the top have the value "work": give each its own value` (or `… under "Family" …`)
- `the option "Family": how its sub-options are picked must be "one" or "several"`
- `the option "Family": its heading must be text` (or its placeholder), and `its placeholder must be text` for the top one
- `the options go deeper than 6 levels (under "…")`
- `it has more than 1000 options`
- an option's description, source, link, icon, colour or picture, as for a select: `the option "Family › Children": its link must start with http:, https: or mailto:`

**An older config** (before v1.53.0) is `{ levels: [{ label, placeholder? }], options: { root: [...] } }`, each option's sub-options under the first key of its `children` object. It is still read everywhere: the `root` list (or the first key) is the top level; each option's first `children` key holds its sub-options, picked one at a time; each level's label and placeholder become the heading and placeholder of the options above it, and the first level's placeholder the top one; and it is read only as deep as it has levels, as the old picker showed. Blank rows are left out. It is checked only for its shape, its limits and its options' content (a repeated value or a `/` in a value passes), so a type carrying one keeps saving and importing. The field editor rewrites it as a tree, with no `levels`, on its first edit.

#### Rules

**Set the value** isn't offered: "its choices belong to its own tree." Another record's rule can't set it either.

**In a condition** (Visible when, Required when, a value rule's condition, a reference field's **Only entries where…**), a chained select offers **Is one of**, **Is not one of**, **Is blank** and **Is not blank**. **Is one of** and **Is not one of** open a list of every option, indented by level ("Choose options..."), so any option at any depth can be chosen. **Is one of** holds while one of the chosen options is picked, and **Is not one of** while none is. It is stored as paths, `{ "field": "area", "in": ["family/children"] }`, and the hint names the option by its labels: "Area is one of Family › Children". The text tests (written in JSON) compare against the picks' paths joined with `,`.

#### In lists and tokens

- **Filter**: not offered on the list page. **Sort**: yes, by the stored picks as text. **Table column**: yes, as the text shown in tables (above).
- **In a merge template**, `{area|label}` gives that same text, and a bare `{area}` gives the picked option values ("family, children, schooling"). In a formula, `{area}` is the list of picked values, so `last({area})` is the last pick.

#### Limits and gaps

- **No default, no Set the value and no list filter.**
- **The table sorts by the stored paths**, not by the options' order or labels.
- Six levels and 1,000 options at most.

---

## Yes / No

### toggle

An on or off switch.

```json
{
  "id": "is_active",
  "type": "toggle",
  "label": "Active",
  "defaultValue": true
}
```

#### Holds

`true` or `false`. A write that sends anything else is refused (`Field "is_active" must be true or false`). A switch nobody has touched stores nothing.

#### On the form

A switch beside the label.

#### On the detail page

**Yes** or **No**, the same words the cards and the table use. A box nothing was stored in is blank, and is hidden unless the type shows empty fields. No **Display type** choices.

#### Settings

- **Create form**: the default is a switch, "Pre-checked on new entries".
- **Toggle settings** (on a type whose entries live in isolation sections, or a shared type's field kept separately in each section): **Only one per …**, named after the isolated type. See [One per section](#one-per-section-uniqueinscope).
- No **Format and checks** box.

#### Rules

**Set the value** is offered: on or off, or a copy of another toggle or yes/no field. Other rules test it with **Is on** (`{ "field": "is_active", "equals": "true" }`) and **Is off** (`{ "field": "is_active", "notEquals": "true" }`, which an unset box matches too). A field that is one per section can't also be set by a rule.

#### In lists and tokens
