- **Filter**: not offered on the list page. **Sort**: yes. **Table column**: yes, as Yes or No.
- **Token**: `{is_active}` gives `true` or `false` in a merge template, and true or false in a formula.

#### Limits and gaps

- **Required means "touched", not "on".** A switch left untouched counts as blank, so a required one blocks the save until it is switched on (or on and off again). A default avoids that. There is no way to say a switch must be on before saving.
- **No Yes or No in a token.** `{is_active|label}` gives nothing; only the user-account link has a Yes/No format (`{linked|label}`).
- **No list filter.**

### boolean

A yes or no question. Its label in the editor is **Yes / No**. It works exactly as [toggle](#toggle); only the name differs.

```json
{
  "id": "has_dependants",
  "type": "boolean",
  "label": "Has Dependants?"
}
```

<Aside type="tip">
Use `toggle` for on/off states (e.g. "Active", "Enabled") and `boolean` for yes/no questions (e.g. "Has Children?", "Is Australian Citizen?"). Both store a boolean value — the distinction is semantic.
</Aside>

#### Holds

As for [toggle](#toggle): `true` or `false`.

#### On the form

As for toggle: a switch.

#### On the detail page

As for toggle: **Yes** or **No**.

#### Settings

As for toggle: the **Pre-checked on new entries** default, and **Only one per …** in the **Yes / No settings** box where sections apply.

#### Rules

As for toggle: **Set the value**, **Is on** and **Is off**, and one per section.

#### In lists and tokens

As for toggle.

#### Limits and gaps

As for toggle. Having two types for the same thing is a candidate for merging into one.

---

## Composite

### address

An address in parts.

```json
{
  "id": "office_address",
  "type": "address",
  "label": "Office Address",
  "required": true,
  "width": "full",
  "addressConfig": {
    "showStreet2": true,
    "showState": true,
    "showCountry": true
  }
}
```

#### Holds

**Stored value:**

```json
{
  "street1": "123 Pitt Street",
  "street2": "Level 4",
  "city": "Sydney",
  "state": "NSW",
  "postcode": "2000",
  "country": "Australia"
}
```

The server doesn't check the object's keys or its parts.

#### On the form

A stack of boxes:

- Street Address (full width)
- Street Address Line 2 (full width, unless `showStreet2` is `false`)
- City, and State / Region unless `showState` is `false` (two columns)
- Postcode, and Country unless `showCountry` is `false` (two columns)

#### On the detail page

The address on separate lines: the street lines, then the city, state and postcode on one line (`Sydney NSW 2000`), then the country. Blank parts are left out, and every stored part shows, even one whose box `addressConfig` hides. A value of another shape, such as one an import brought in, shows as it is stored. No **Display type** choices.

#### Settings

No settings box of its own, no **Format and checks** and no **Default value** in the editor. `addressConfig` has no controls: set it with the Fields tab's **Import Fields** button, the configuration import or the API.

**addressConfig:**

| Property | Type | Default | Description |
|----------|------|---------|-------------|
| `showStreet2` | `boolean` | `true` | Show the second street address line |
| `showState` | `boolean` | `true` | Show the state/region field |
| `showCountry` | `boolean` | `true` | Show the country field |
| `defaultCountry` | `string` | — | **Reserved — not yet honoured by the built-in renderer.** Declared for a future pre-filled country; the current renderer reads only `showStreet2`, `showState` and `showCountry`, and the Country input starts empty |

#### Rules

**Set the value** is offered as a copy only, of another address field (this entry's, through a link to one entry, or the parent's). A linked-record rule never copies an empty address over a real one (v1.88.4). Other rules can choose it, but a test reads the whole address as one value, not its text: only **Is blank** and **Is not blank** mean anything, and an address whose parts were typed and then cleared counts as not blank.

#### In lists and tokens

- **Filter**: no. **Sort**: no (several parts). **Table column**: yes, on one line.
- Cards, list rows, table cells and connection badges show it on one line, separated by commas: `123 Pitt Street, Level 4, Sydney NSW 2000, Australia`.
- **Token**: `{office_address}` gives the same one-line text. A part, such as `{office_address.city}`, gives just that part, and `{office_address|locality}` (v1.43.0) gives the suburb, state and postcode (`Sydney NSW 2000`). Both work through a reference too (`{client->office_address.postcode}`). A formula reads the one-line text, and `{office_address.city}` one part.

#### Limits and gaps

- **No controls for its parts.** Hiding the second street line, the state or the country needs JSON.
- **`defaultCountry` does nothing**: it is declared but the Country box starts empty.
- **Required counts any object.** An address whose parts were typed and cleared again passes **Required**, on the form and the server.
- **No checks.** There is no postcode or state check, and no address lookup.

### name-field

A person's name in parts. Its label in the editor is **Name**. You type the full name in one box and it fills in the parts for you to check.

**nameConfig:**

| Property | Type | Default | Description |
|----------|------|---------|-------------|
| `showPrefix` | `boolean` | `false` | Show the **Title** box |
| `showMiddle` | `boolean` | `false` | Show the **Middle name(s)** box. Off, any middle names typed in the full name go into the first name |
| `showSuffix` | `boolean` | `false` | Show the **Suffix** box, and pick suffixes out of a typed full name |
| `prefixOptions` | `string[]` | Mr, Mrs, Ms, Miss, Mx, Dr, Prof | The titles the **Title** dropdown offers and a typed full name recognises. Not set: the default list. An empty list (`[]`): the Title box takes any text, and a typed full name still recognises the default titles |
| `suffixOptions` | `string[]` | Jr, Jnr, Sr, Snr, II, III, IV, V, PhD, Esq, KC, QC, OAM | The suffixes a typed full name recognises at its end. The Suffix box always takes any text |
| `displayOrder` | (older) | `'given-first'` | How lists, cards, table cells and connection badges show the name. Since v1.89.0 it is set as **Display order** in **Format and checks** (`format.nameOrder`); an older `displayOrder` is still read and is moved there when the field is next edited |

```json
{
  "id": "full_name",
  "type": "name-field",
  "label": "Full Name",
  "required": true,
  "nameConfig": {
    "showPrefix": true,
    "showMiddle": true,
    "showSuffix": false,
    "prefixOptions": ["Mr", "Mrs", "Ms", "Miss", "Dr", "Prof"],
    "displayOrder": "surname-first"
  }
}
```

#### Holds

```json
{
  "prefix": "Dr",
  "first": "Kim",
  "middle": "James",
  "last": "Schulz"
}
```

The stored keys and their shape are unchanged from earlier versions, so existing names carry over as they are.

A request that saves something other than an object of text parts gets a `400`: `Field "x" must be a name: an object of text parts (prefix, first, middle, last, suffix)`. A module writing in-process gets a warning instead. A stored value left unchanged is never re-checked.

#### On the form

**Entering a name.** The input has a **Full name** box on top and, underneath, a box for each part:

- **Title**, if `showPrefix` is on: a dropdown of the titles, with **None** to clear it, or a text box when `prefixOptions` is an empty list. A stored title that isn't in the list still shows as an option.
- **First name**
- **Middle name(s)**, if `showMiddle` is on
- **Surname**
- **Suffix**, if `showSuffix` is on

Typing in the Full name box fills the boxes underneath as you type. Editing a box rebuilds the Full name text from the boxes. Only the boxes the field shows are written, so a part the field hides keeps whatever is stored.

**How a typed full name is split:**

1. Extra spaces are ignored.
2. A comma means surname first: everything before the first comma is the surname, and the title, given names and suffix follow it. `Smith, Dr Jane Mary` gives the surname Smith, the title Dr, the first name Jane and the middle name Mary. Two cases still read given names first, with the comma ignored: when nothing comes before the comma, and (with the suffix shown) when only suffixes follow it, as in `Jane Smith, Jr`.
3. If the first word is one of the titles, it becomes the title. Case and a trailing full stop don't matter, and the title is saved as the list writes it, so `dr.` saves as `Dr`. With the title hidden, no word is read as a title.
4. With the suffix shown, suffixes at the end (Jr, Sr, II, III, IV, PhD and the like, or the field's `suffixOptions`) become the suffix, saved as the list writes them. A suffix is only taken while a first name and a surname are left, so `Jane Ao` keeps Ao as her surname.
5. The last word left is the surname. Lowercase particles just before it stay with it (van, von, de, da, di, del, della, der, den, du, la, le), so `Ludwig van Beethoven` has the surname `van Beethoven`. A capitalised `Van` counts as a name, not a particle.
6. The first word left is the first name, and any words between it and the surname are the middle names. With middle names hidden, they join the first name instead.
7. A single word is the first name.

The split is a starting point: check the boxes and correct any part by hand.

#### On the detail page

One line, given names first, in the order title, first, middle, surname, suffix: `Dr Jane Q Citizen PhD`. Blank parts are left out. A value of another shape shows as it is stored. No **Display type** choices.

#### Settings

- **Name settings**: **Show title**, **Show middle names** and **Show suffix** switches (`showPrefix`, `showMiddle`, `showSuffix`). With the title shown, **Type any title** (sets `prefixOptions` to an empty list), or else **Titles (comma-separated)** (`prefixOptions`; leave it blank for the default list).
- **Format and checks**: **Display order**, for lists, cards and tables: "The entry page shows given names first." Seven orders, from **Given names first (Dr Jane Smith)** to **Surname and initials (Smith, J.Q.)**. The starter templates are the five **Name:** templates.
- No **Default value** in the editor. `suffixOptions` has no control: set it with **Import Fields**, the configuration import or the API.

#### Rules

**Set the value** is offered as a copy only, of another name field. A linked-record rule never copies an empty name over a real one. Other rules can choose it, but a test reads the whole name as one value, not its text: only **Is blank** and **Is not blank** mean anything.

#### In lists and tokens

- **Cards, list rows, table cells and connection badges.** One line in the field's **Display order**. Surname first puts the surname, a comma, then the title and given names, then a comma and the suffix: `Smith, Jane`, `Smith, Dr Jane`, `Citizen, Dr Jane Q, PhD`. A name with no surname shows given names first either way. The short orders leave out the title and the suffix:
- **First and last name** (v1.45.2) gives `Jane Citizen`. With only one of the two it shows that one, and with neither, given names first.
- **Initial and surname** and **Surname and initial** (v1.45.1) give `J. Citizen` and `Citizen, J.`: the first name's initial, from its first word.
- **Initials and surname** and **Surname and initials** (v1.45.2) give `J.Q. Citizen` and `Citizen, J.Q.`: one initial for each word of the first and middle names.
- With no first or middle name an initial order shows the surname alone, and with no surname, given names first.
- **Merge templates and computed templates.** A token for the whole field, such as `{full_name}`, gives the name given names first, as in `Dr Jane Smith`. A token for one part, such as `{full_name.last}`, gives just that part. This also works through a reference, as in `{client->full_name}`, and since v1.43.0 so does a part, as in `{client->full_name.last}`. `{full_name|surnameFirst}` (v1.43.0) gives the name surname first, as a surname-first list shows it: `Citizen, Dr Jane Q, PhD`. `{full_name|firstLast}` gives `Jane Citizen`, `{full_name|initialSurname}` gives `J. Citizen`, `{full_name|initialsSurname}` gives `J.Q. Citizen`, `{full_name|surnameInitial}` gives `Citizen, J.` and `{full_name|surnameInitials}` gives `Citizen, J.Q.` (v1.45.1 and v1.45.2), each matching its display order. Since v1.47.0 each initial one has a form without full stops, which is a format only, not a display order: `{full_name|initialSurnamePlain}` gives `J Citizen`, `{full_name|initialsSurnamePlain}` gives `JQ Citizen`, `{full_name|surnameInitialPlain}` gives `Citizen, J` and `{full_name|surnameInitialsPlain}` gives `Citizen, JQ`. For the initials alone (v1.47.0), `{full_name|initial}` gives `J.` and `{full_name|initialPlain}` gives `J`, from the first name, and `{full_name|initials}` gives `J.Q.` and `{full_name|initialsPlain}` gives `JQ`, one for each word of the first and middle names. On a part they work on its text, as in `{full_name.first|initial}`. See [Formats](/modules/structure/merge-fields/#formats-v1430). Build it offers **Full name**, **Full name, surname first**, **First and last name (Jane Smith)**, each short format with and without full stops (such as **Surname and initial (Smith, J.)** and **Surname and initial (Smith, J)**), an **Initials** group (**First initial (K.)**, **First initial (K)**, **Initials (K.A.)**, **Initials (KA)**), and a **Parts** group (**Title**, **First name**, **Middle name**, **Surname** and **Suffix**).
- **Formulas.** A `{full_name}` token reads as the same text, so text functions and comparisons work on it. A part token such as `{full_name.first}` reads one part.

**Sorting and search.**

- **Sorting.** A list sorted by a name field (`dataSortField`) sorts by surname, then first name, then middle names, ignoring case. A blank part sorts after a filled one, and a blank name sorts last in both directions. The server recognises a name field from the type's own, inherited or template fields, whatever `dataSortType` says. `displayOrder` changes only how the name is shown, not how it sorts.
- **Search.** Keyword search finds a name by any part: title, first, middle, surname or suffix, and the start of a word ("love" finds Lovelace). The parts are already in the search index, so no setup is needed.
- **Data filters.** A text filter on a name field (through the API's `dataFilters` or a module; the list page doesn't offer one) matches its parts:
  - **contains** matches the whole name, so "Ada" and "Ada Lovelace" both match;
  - **equals**, **starts with** and **in** match when any part does;
  - **not equals** and **not in** match when no part does;
  - **is empty** is true when every part is blank.

A table sorted in the browser (not by the server) orders names by surname, then first name.

#### Limits and gaps

- **Required counts any object**, as for an address.
- **The detail page always shows given names first**, whatever the display order.
- **The formats without full stops aren't display orders.** `{full_name|initialSurnamePlain}` and the other three work in tokens, but **Display order** offers only the forms with full stops.
- **No list filter on the list page**, although the API's data filters match its parts.
- `suffixOptions` has no control in the editor.

### field-group

Rows of sub-fields that people add and remove, such as the people on a matter.

```json
{
  "id": "key_people",
  "type": "field-group",
  "label": "Key People",
  "width": "full",
  "fields": [
    {
      "id": "role",
      "type": "select",
      "label": "Role",
      "options": [
        { "value": "Applicant", "label": "Applicant" },
        { "value": "Respondent", "label": "Respondent" },
        { "value": "Solicitor", "label": "Solicitor" }
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "person",
      "type": "node-reference",
      "label": "Person",
      "nodeReferenceConfig": {
        "allowedTypes": ["person"]
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "notes",
      "type": "text",
      "label": "Notes"
    }
  ]
}
```

#### Rows on the page, and who and when (v1.89.33)

A top-level field group's settings have an **On the page** box with two switches. For example, a **Changeover notes** log: staff add a note from the changeover's page while its other details stay as they are, and each note shows who wrote it and when.

**Add rows on the page.** The detail page shows, with the group's rows, a small form with the row's fields. Its button saves that one row straight away: no edit mode, and the entry's other fields aren't sent.

- **Make it look nice:** **Title** and **Intro** (plain text or merge fields read on the entry), **Button label** (blank shows "Add"), **Where the form sits** (above or below the rows), **Row order** (oldest or newest first; display only), **Show the last N rows** with "Show all", **Frame** (card or plain), and a **Preview**. The form lays out the fields with their own **Width** and **New line** settings (the group's rows in the forms now do too, when the group is vertical), and selects keep their **Shown as**.
- After adding, the form clears and says "Added."; the entry is read again, so cells a rule or formula fills show.
- It shows only to someone who could change the group in place, on a live, assigned entry. A group locked after creating takes no new rows; an append-only one still does.
- The server adds the row to what is stored at that moment (the row is read inside the write, and the write retries if the entry changed), so two people adding at once never overwrite each other. Every edit check applies, as an edit of the group would: permissions, locks, append-only, Required, row value rules, linked-record rules, the section wall and link filters. At most 1,000 rows per group when adding.
- Stored as `groupConfig.addOnPage: { on, title? (up to 80), intro? (up to 200), buttonLabel? (up to 40), position?: 'above' | 'below', order?: 'oldest' | 'newest', show?: 1 to 100, frame?: 'card' | 'plain' }`. Switching it off keeps the settings.
- Not for a group inside a group, a group kept per section, another module's group or a connection type's group (the box says why).
- Other modules can add a row in process with `structureService.appendFieldGroupRow(id, fieldId, userId, row, options)` (updateNode's options), or over HTTP with `POST /api/v1/structure/nodes/:id/fields/:fieldId/rows` and `{ row }`.

**Record who and when** (`groupConfig.rowStamps: true`). The server stamps each row: who added it and when, and who last changed it and when. Each row shows "Added by Kim · 18 Nov 2026, 10:32", and "Changed by …" when it has been edited ("Added automatically" for a module's or the system's write).

- Stored on the row as `$stamp: { addedAt, addedBy, changedAt?, changedBy? }` (ISO times and user ids). `$stamp` is reserved: no sub-field may have that id.
- Only the server writes it. Anything a form or API call sends under `$stamp` is dropped before any check, on every write path; a sent stamp only helps recognise a stored row that moved, and must match that row's stamp exactly.
- A row whose cells changed gets `changedAt` / `changedBy` and keeps its `addedAt`; Timestamps and worked-out cells don't count as a change. An unchanged row keeps its stamp. Append-only and locked-cell checks ignore the stamp.
- Turning it on stamps only rows added or changed from then on. Turning it off adds nothing; stamps already stored stay.
- Merge fields and formulas can't read it (`{log[0].$stamp}` gives nothing). It goes out with the entry's data through the read API and the `structure.node.updated` payload.

**Limits:**
- **Names:** someone who isn't an admin sees their own name and "someone else" on other people's rows, because only admins can read the app's list of users.
- **Matching rows:** rows are matched by place. If an older client strips the stamps and inserts a row at the top, a change can be put on the wrong row.

#### A group inside a group (v1.89.27)

A sub-field of a field group can itself be a **Field Group**, one level deep. For example, **Changeover notes** rows can each hold **Observations**, with one row per child: Child (a link), Behaviours (a Select with **Several can be picked**) and Notes (Long Text). It is offered in a node type's or field template's top-level group, not in one kept per section, another module's or a connection type's.

- **Holds:** `data[outer][i][inner][j][sub]`. Inner rows are plain objects, matched to the stored rows by their place, as outer rows are. There can be at most 100 inner rows per outer row and 1,000 per group.
- **Settings:** the inner group has the same editor, with its own layout, Add button, **Rows can be added but not changed or removed** and lock settings. An inner group can't hold another group, a secret, a formula or worked-out field, a field set by a rule, a Connections field, an Entries list or a list field. Its links can't create, connect or write back. Every save refuses these (for example `a group inside a group can't hold a secret`), and `a group inside a group is not kept here` on a connection type.
- **Locks:** in an append-only outer group, a saved outer row is frozen whole, inner rows included, so add the observations in the same save as the note. When the inner group holds its rows (append-only, locked, or with a Timestamp or locked cell), saved outer rows keep their places: they can't be removed or moved.
- **Rules:** Visible / Hidden, Required / Optional, Collapsible and notes read the inner row, then the outer row, then the entry; an inner cell hides an outer cell of the same id. **Set the value** isn't offered inside an inner group ("this group is inside another group.").
- **Tokens:** `{outer[0].inner[1].sub}`, `{outer[0].inner.sub}` (the first inner row) and `[key=value]` selectors at either level. A formula sees the nested lists. The merge field lists show inner sub-fields as `{outer[0].inner.sub}`, with **This row**, then the outer row, then the entry's fields.
- **Not covered:** deeper nesting; inner groups in a per-section group, a module's group or a connection type; linked-record rules, list filters, sorting, option sources and `getFieldChoices` for inner fields; reading through an inner link (`{o[0].i[0].ref->x}`).

#### Holds

```json
[
  { "role": "Applicant", "person": "node_abc123", "notes": "" },
  { "role": "Respondent", "person": "node_def456", "notes": "Self-represented" }
]
```

**The value must be a list of rows (v1.38.0).** Each row is a plain object (or `null`). A request write that sends anything else — a single row object, or a list holding entries that are not rows — is refused with **400** (`must be a list of rows`), because encryption, the field locks and secret stripping all work row by row. A module writing in-process is not refused: a single row object is wrapped as a one-row list and non-row entries are dropped, with a server warning. A value already stored in the old single-object form is still sealed and stripped correctly, and is only re-checked when a write changes it.

Each new or changed row is checked cell by cell, by each sub-field's rules (its type, its **Format and checks** and its **Required**). A row as stored, wherever it now sits, isn't checked again.

An entry's **History** lists a group's changes row by row (a row's `$stamp` isn't a change), and a type's independent timestamps can be set to stamp each version that adds a row to chosen groups (see [History and Check integrity](/modules/structure/usage/#history-and-check-integrity-v18934)).

#### On the form

The rows, each with its sub-fields and a remove button, and an **Add** button (named "Add" and the field's label) that adds a row with each sub-field's default. **Field alignment** sets how the sub-fields sit in a row: **Vertical**, **Horizontal** (side by side as there is room) or **Table** (one line per row, edited in place). With **Rows can be added but not changed or removed**, saved rows are read-only: "Rows already saved can't be changed or removed. New rows can be added."

#### On the detail page

- **Rows** (the usual): each row's sub-fields through the read-only renderer, so a `secret` sub-field is masked with a reveal toggle (v1.38.0), and a blank cell or row is left out. On a narrow card the rows go to one column.
- **Table**: one line per row.

#### Settings

- **Field Group settings**: the sub-fields, edited with the same editor as top-level fields (any type but Connections, Entries list, List, Assembled list item and Assembled list; a Field Group one level deep, see [A group inside a group](#a-group-inside-a-group-v18927)); **Field alignment**; **Add button** (Left, Centre, Right or Full width); on a node type's or field template's group, **Rows can be added but not changed or removed** ("Once saved, a row stays as it is, for everyone, admins included. New rows go at the end."); and on its top-level group, the **On the page** box (see [Rows on the page](#rows-on-the-page-and-who-and-when-v18933)).
- **Display**: **Display type** (Rows or Table).
- No **Format and checks** and no **Default value**.
- A sub-field has the **Every field**, **Create form**, **Format and checks**, **Display** and **More** boxes, with **Required**, **Permissions**, **Can't be changed after creating**, a merge template, and the rules listed under **Rules** below. It has no **In create form** or **On the detail page** switch, no **Heading** or **Display type**, no one per section, no options from other records, no Other that adds records and no suggestions.

#### Rules

**Set the value** isn't offered: "a field group holds rows of its own fields." Other rules can't test the group itself. A sub-field's rules offer **Visible** / **Hidden**, **Required** / **Optional**, **Collapsible** / **Not collapsible**, **Show a note** (since v1.89.19) and **Set the value** (since v1.89.21); since v1.89.23 they offer **Change a linked record** too: a rule through a group's link sub-field (**Field to set** lists it as "People › Person") runs for each row, on that row's linked records, reading the row's cells over the entry's fields. **When it first applies** fires for a record newly linked in any row, or a row whose tests have just become true, judged across the whole group, so moving, inserting or deleting rows never fires it twice. A sub-field's Rules box lists the rules started from it. **Set the value on a sub-field** works as on a top-level field, row by row: each row's rule reads that row's cells over the entry's fields (a cell wins over an entry field of the same id). **Copy from** lists the row's own cells under **This row**, then the entry's fields. The forms apply it live, and the server applies and checks it on every save. A row the entry already had keeps an **Only set it once** value, and a cell changed by hand stays; a new row starts from the cell's default. In an append-only or locked group only **Suggest it** is offered; it isn't offered in a group inside a group, a per-section group, or on a connection type's fields, and a sub-field rule can't test through a link. A sub-field's **Visible when** works row by row: it is evaluated against the row over the entry's data, so it can test a field in the same row or a top-level field.

#### In lists and tokens

- **Filter**: no. **Sort**: no. **Table column**: yes, as text.
- **Token**: rows are read with `{key_people[role=Applicant].person}` (the first row that matches), `{key_people[0].notes}` (a row by number) or `{key_people.notes}` (the first row). See [Merge Templates](/modules/structure/merge-fields/#repeater-filtered-lookup). A formula reads a group as its list of rows, so `sum(pluck({lines}, 'amount'))` adds up a `lines` group's amounts.

<Aside type="tip">
Field groups are powerful when combined with [merge templates](/modules/structure/merge-fields/). You can pull values from specific repeater rows using filtered lookups like `{key_people[role=Applicant].person->surname}`.
</Aside>

#### Limits and gaps

- **Required on the group is form only.** The form asks for at least one row; the server doesn't.
- **A required sub-field is checked by the server only.** The form doesn't stop the save for a blank required cell; the server refuses it (`Field "key_people.person" is required`) for a row the save adds or changes. A sub-field with **Visible when** is never required on the server.
- **One level of groups inside groups.** A group inside a group can't hold another; every save refuses one (see [A group inside a group](#a-group-inside-a-group-v18927)).
- **A held group keeps its rows' places.** With a locked or Timestamp cell, or add-only rows, saved rows can't be removed, moved or put between.
- **`showOnCreate` and `showOnDetail` on a sub-field** are accepted but do nothing.

---

## Links and lists

### node-reference

A link to one or more other records. Its label in the editor is **Node Reference**; the screens call it a link field.

<Tabs>
  <TabItem label="Single reference">
```json
{
  "id": "assigned_client",
  "type": "node-reference",
  "label": "Client",
  "required": true,
  "nodeReferenceConfig": {
    "allowedTypes": ["client"]
  }
}
```

Stores a single node ID string: `"clu1abc..."`.
  </TabItem>
  <TabItem label="Multi reference">
```json
{
  "id": "related_matters",
  "type": "node-reference",
  "label": "Related Matters",
  "nodeReferenceConfig": {
    "allowedTypes": ["matter"],
    "multiple": true
  }
}
```

Stores an array of node ID strings: `["clu1abc...", "clu2def..."]`.
  </TabItem>
  <TabItem label="Create, edit, delete, limit">
```json
{
  "id": "people",
  "type": "node-reference",
  "label": "People",
  "nodeReferenceConfig": {
    "allowedTypes": ["person"],
    "excludeTypes": ["child"],
    "multiple": true,
    "maxItems": 10,
    "allowCreate": true,
    "createTypes": ["person"],
    "allowEdit": true,
    "allowDelete": true
  }
}
```

Up to 10 people, any person but a child. The picker can make a new person, and each chosen person has **Edit** and **Delete** buttons beside it.
  </TabItem>
</Tabs>

#### Holds

A record's id (letters, digits, `-` and `_`, at most 100 characters), or with **Allow multiple references** a list of ids. An empty string marks an unset slot. A new record made from the picker is sent as `{ "$new": { … } }` and stored as its id once it is made. See [Value checks](#value-checks-v1380) for what a write must send.

#### On the form

A searchable record picker (`NodeSelector`) that offers only what the field may hold: its allowed types less its excluded ones, the entries that match **Only entries where…**, inside the entry's [isolation section](#inside-an-isolation-section-v1420). With several entries, each is its own row, and **Add** adds a row until **Maximum number of entries**. **Create new** (with **Allow creating new entries here**) makes a new record in the same save; **Edit** and **Delete** sit beside each chosen entry when allowed. While a value rule adds records, they show read-only under the field: "Added by a rule when saved".

#### On the detail page

- **Links** (the usual): each entry's name, linked to its page, with its [badge](#the-badge-v1540), separated by commas. While an entry loads, or when it can't be read, its id shows in its place. An entry whose type the browser doesn't know shows its name without a link.
- **Cards** and **Table**: only what is picked in this field, with **Show only entries where**, **Order**, **Number of entries**, **Pagination**, **Allow adding** and the card or table settings an Entries list has. Choosing more than one display gives a toggle on the page. See [Cards or a Table](#cards-or-a-table-which-entries-in-what-order-v1892).

#### Settings

- **Node Reference settings**: **Allowed node types**, **Excluded node types**, **Allow creating new entries here** (with **Types a new entry can be**), **Allow editing the chosen entries here**, **Allow deleting the chosen entries here**, **Allow multiple references** (with **Maximum number of entries**), **Leave out records picked in** (below), **Also connect the chosen entries** (with **Connection type**; a node type's, field template's or their groups' fields only), **Only entries where…** and, once a type is isolated, **Global types it may point at**. In a group inside a group there is no **Allow creating new entries here** or **Also connect the chosen entries**.
- **Create form**: **Default value** takes a record id.
- **Display**: **Display type** (any of Links, Cards and Table) and the list settings.
- No **Format and checks** box.

**Leave out records picked in (v1.89.29).** Name other link fields of the entry (up to 10; for a field group's link sub-field, its row's other link cells come first, then the entry's fields). This field's picker then never offers a record those fields hold, as the form stands (unsaved picks too), in every picker: the forms, rows, edit in place, Add details and the entry-form slot. A record this field already holds stays shown. The server refuses a new pick of such a record (`Field "x" cannot reference an entry already picked in "…"`), a Set the value rule skips it, and `getFieldChoices` leaves it out. It works one way: set it on both fields to keep them apart in both directions. Stored as `nodeReferenceConfig.excludePickedIn`.

**nodeReferenceConfig.** Every setting a reference field has, in one place. Each key is optional, and leaving a key out is the same as its default. They all work on a top-level field and on a [field-group](#field-group) sub-field, and they all round-trip through the configuration export and import. Structure reads no other key here.

| Property | Type | Default | Description |
|----------|------|---------|-------------|
| `allowedTypes` | `string[]` | any type | The node types (slugs) the field may point at. A listed type admits its subtypes. Leave it out, or give `[]`, for any type. When it is set, each id a request write **adds** must be a readable entry of one of these types or a subtype (see [Value checks](#value-checks-v1380)). Admin control: **Allowed node types** |
| `excludeTypes` | `string[]` | none | v1.42.1+. Node types the field may **not** point at, with their subtypes. `allowedTypes: ['person']` admits subtypes such as `child`; `excludeTypes: ['child']` takes them out again. The picker leaves them out, and the server refuses a non-system write that names one, checking every id the field holds. Admin control: **Excluded node types** |
| `multiple` | `boolean` | `false` | Hold a list of entries instead of one. Admin control: **Allow multiple references** |
| `maxItems` | whole number, 1 to 1000 | no limit | v1.45.0+. With `multiple: true`, the most entries the field may hold. The form stops offering **Add** at the limit, and the server refuses a write that changes the value to more entries, for every caller. On a field that isn't `multiple` the value is still checked, but it limits nothing. `null` counts as not set. See [Edit, Delete and a limit](#edit-delete-and-a-limit-v1450). Admin control: **Maximum number of entries**, shown once **Allow multiple references** is on (leave it blank for no limit) |
| `filter` | condition | none | v1.42.0+. A condition on the **target** entry, in the [`visibleWhen`](#visibility--behaviour) grammar. The picker lists only matching entries, and the server refuses a write that names one that doesn't match. See [Only some entries](#only-some-entries-filter-v1420). Admin control: **Only entries where…** |
| `allowGlobalTypes` | `string[]` | every shared type | v1.42.0+. From inside an [isolation section](#inside-an-isolation-section-v1420), which shared types the field may point at. `[]` = none, `['*']` = every one. No effect on an entry in no section. Admin control: **Global types it may point at**, shown once a type is isolated |
| `allowCreate` | `boolean` | `false` | v1.42.2+. Lets the user create a new entry from the picker. The picker's **Create new** popup asks for the new entry's name (or, when its type computes its name, the fields the name is made from) and its required fields. Since v1.45.0 it leaves out a field set not to show when creating, unless the create needs it, starts the entry with its type's create defaults and applies its type's value rules (see [`setWhen`](#set-automatically-setwhen)). The new entry shows as "Jane Smith (new)" and is created when the form is saved, in the same save as the entry it belongs to. It counts as filled for a required field. It is placed inside the host's isolation section, so a new Matter's new Person lands inside that Matter. Over the API, the value is `{ "$new": { "nodeType": "person", "name": "Jane Smith", "data": { … } } }` (`data` optional), at most 50 per write; without `allowCreate` such a value is refused (`400`). Admin control: **Allow creating new entries here** |
| `createTypes` | `string[]` | the allowed types | v1.42.2+. With `allowCreate`, the types a new entry may be, with their subtypes. Left out, or `[]`: `allowedTypes` less `excludeTypes`. Admin control: **Types a new entry can be**, shown once **Allow creating new entries here** is on |
| `allowEdit` | `boolean` | `false` | v1.45.0+. An **Edit** button (a pencil) beside each chosen entry opens that entry's own form in place. See [Edit, Delete and a limit](#edit-delete-and-a-limit-v1450). Admin control: **Allow editing the chosen entries here** |
| `allowDelete` | `boolean` | `false` | v1.45.0+. A **Delete** button (a bin) beside each chosen entry deletes the entry itself, after the usual delete question, and takes it out of the field. See [Edit, Delete and a limit](#edit-delete-and-a-limit-v1450). Admin control: **Allow deleting the chosen entries here** (help: "Deletes the entry itself, after asking.") |
| `connect` | `boolean` | `false` | v1.46.0+. Also connect each chosen entry, with a connection type Structure makes for the field and names after it, so the link shows on both entries' **Connections** tab. One connection per pair, kept while a reference between the two remains. Works on a node type's fields and a field template's; a connection type's fields refuse `true`. See [Connecting the chosen entries](#connecting-the-chosen-entries-v1460). Admin control: **Also connect the chosen entries** (hint: "Adds a connection between the two entries.") |
| `connectType` | `string` | none | v1.71.0+. With `connect`, the connection type it uses, a slug. Several fields can share one. An app-level type can only use an app-level connection type. Left out on a field saved before v1.71.0, which keeps an automatic type. Admin control: **Connection type** (one that fits, or **Create a new one…**) |
| `syncTargetField` | `string` | none | v1.42.1+. The id of a `boolean` or `toggle` field on the target type. When an entry is added to this field, the server sets that field to `true` on the entry; when it is removed, it sets it back to `false`, unless another entry still references it through a field with the same `syncTargetField`. It happens in the same save, so the two can't disagree, and it covers entries created from the picker. Make the target field `editableBy: 'system'` so it can only change this way. Deleting the host clears its flags, archiving keeps them, and restoring sets them again. A shared/global entry is never flagged. Since v1.45.0 the target entry's own value rules are applied in the same write, and the named field may not be [one per section](#one-per-section-uniqueinscope) on any type the reference can point at (see below). There is no admin control yet; set it through the JSON import |
| `excludePickedIn` | `string[]` | none | v1.89.29+. Up to 10 ids of other link fields the field can see (never its own). Its picker never offers a record they hold, and the server refuses a new pick of one. See **Leave out records picked in** above. Admin control: **Leave out records picked in** |

**What a save refuses.** Every save of a field set checks these keys: node types, field templates, connection types, node-type proposals, the configuration import and a module's `importNodeTypes`, at the top level and in field-group rows (a sub-field is named `group.sub`). Each is a `400` naming the field; the configuration import answers `422` naming the item, and its practice run shows the same refusal.

- `Field "x" allowEdit must be true or false`, and the same for `allowDelete` (v1.45.0)
- `Field "x" maxItems must be a whole number from 1 to 1000` (v1.45.0)
- `Field "x" connect must be true or false` (v1.46.0), and `Field "x" connectType must be a connection type slug`
- `Field "x" excludePickedIn must be a list of at most 10 field ids`, `Field "x" cannot leave out the records picked in itself`, and on the admin saves `Field "x" leaves out the records picked in "y", which is not a link field it can see` (v1.89.29)
- `Field "x": the reference fields of a connection type cannot connect the entries they pick (connect)` (v1.46.0), for `connect: true` on a connection type's field or sub-field. `connect: false` is accepted there, and means off.
- `Field "<flag>" of "<target type>" cannot be one per section (uniqueInScope) while field "<reference field>" of "<host type>" keeps it on for every entry it picks (syncTargetField)` (v1.45.0). The write-back keeps the flag on for every entry the reference picks, and one per section keeps it on for one entry per section, so the two can't both hold. It is refused from either side: a reference that gains the `syncTargetField` or an allowed type, a field that becomes one per section, or a type that gains a parent or a field template. With no `allowedTypes`, every type counts. On some save paths the message starts with `Node type "slug": `, `Field template "slug": ` or `Proposal "slug": `. A pair stored by hand, past every save, writes nothing to that field (the server logs it).

#### Rules

**Set the value** is offered (v1.89.10): **Records picked**, or **Copy from another field** (another link field of this entry, **The parent** itself, or a link field of a linked record or of the parent). A link to several records adds the rule's records unless **Replace them** is on. It runs on the server only, **On every save** or **Only set it once**; with **Suggest it** (v1.89.22) the form fills in the rule's records for people to change. See [Set the value on a link field](#set-the-value-on-a-link-field-v18910).

Other rules can test it, and read through it: picking a link field in the Conditions dialog opens **The record it holds**, **Linked to**, **Viewer** and the fields of the records it picks, such as **Parent › Status**. **The record it holds** (v1.89.16) tests the link itself: **Is exactly** or **Is not** another link field (the same record, whether either side holds an id, a list of one, or a record choice's `node:` value), or **Is empty** / **Is not empty**. For example, on a *Receiving parent* field: if *Delivering parent* is exactly *Parent*, set the value by copying *Other parent* (with **Replace them**), else if it is exactly *Other parent*, copy *Parent*. **Picked in** tests whether another type's link field picks this record. Another record's rule can set it, unless it connects the chosen entries or keeps a field in step (`syncTargetField`).

#### In lists and tokens

- **Filter**: no. **Sort**: no (ids). **Table column**: yes, as the entries' names.
- **Token**: `{assigned_client}` gives the id, and `{assigned_client->name}` reads a field of the entry it points at (see [Node reference dereference](/modules/structure/merge-fields/#node-reference-dereference)), up to three links deep (`{a->b->c}`, v1.81.0). Since v1.43.0 a token through a **multiple** reference reads that field on every entry, leaving blank ones out: a merge template joins the values with ", ", and a formula gets a list. Build it offers a reference's fields under **Which of its fields**: the entry's name, reference number, stage, status, description and slug, then its type's fields. A formula reads the records with `nodes({assigned_client})`.

#### Limits and gaps

- **No list filter or sort.** The list page can't filter by a link field, so a card button that goes to "a list of records" opens a section of the entry's page instead.
- **Not per section.** A link field can't keep a separate value in each section yet.
- **`syncTargetField` has no control** in the editor: set it with the JSON import.
- **Cards and Table read 100 picks.** Past that, the counts say "100+".
- **A rule's records aren't set on the form.** The form shows what the rule will do; the server sets them on save.
- **Values that aren't ids** from a module's in-process write are dropped with a warning rather than refused.

#### Only some entries: `filter` (v1.42.0)

`nodeReferenceConfig.filter` is a condition on the entry being pointed at, written like a [`visibleWhen`](#visibility--behaviour) condition. A Matter whose people and children are one `person` type can offer only the children in one field and only the adults in another:

```json
{
  "id": "focus_children",
  "type": "node-reference",
  "label": "Focus children",
  "nodeReferenceConfig": {
    "allowedTypes": ["person"],
    "multiple": true,
    "filter": { "field": "person_type", "equals": "child" }
  }
}
```

`other_parents` would use `{ "field": "person_type", "notEquals": "child" }`. The multi-rule form (`logic` with `rules`) works too.

- **What it reads.** The target's data fields, and its built-ins `name`, `description`, `slug`, `stage` and `status` (`active` or `archived`). If the type has its own data field called `stage` or `status`, that field wins. A secret value is never read. Since v1.50.0 it can also read `linked`, whether the target is linked to a user account, on a target type whose entries can be linked; the builder offers it as "Linked to a user account". See [The link as a condition](#the-link-as-a-condition-v1500).
- **The picker** pushes what it can to the server as data filters, then re-checks every entry it fetched. The text tests added in v1.52.0 (**Does not contain**, **Begins with** and the rest) are never pushed: they are only checked on the fetched entries.
- **The server** refuses a write when any id in the field, stored or new, names an entry that doesn't match: a `400` saying the entry does not match the filter on this field. It binds request writes and in-process module writes; system callers (`isSystem: true`) are exempt.
- **One evaluator.** The picker and the server run the same generated condition code, so they can't disagree about a match.
- **In the admin field editor** the control is **Only entries where…**, the same condition builder as Visible When, offering the target types' fields. A field-group sub-field has it too.

#### Inside an isolation section (v1.42.0)

When the app has an [isolated type](/modules/structure/configuration/#the-hard-wall-around-sections-v1420) (a Matter, say), each of its records is a section, and a reference may not cross from one section into another:

- An entry in a section may point only at entries of the same section, or of a shared type the field's `allowGlobalTypes` admits.
- An entry in no section may point only at entries in no section, or of a shared type.
- Every id the write carries is checked, stored and new, in top-level fields and field-group sub-fields. A breach is a `400` naming the field.
- The picker lists only what the entry may point at: on an edit the host is the entry, on a create its parent. A new top-level record of an isolated type opens a new section, so its picker offers only shared types.
- Request writes and in-process module writes are bound; system callers are exempt.

The admin field editor shows **Global types it may point at** once a type is isolated.

#### Edit, Delete and a limit (v1.45.0)

`allowEdit`, `allowDelete` and `maxItems` change what the field offers on a form: the create form, the edit form, the detail page in edit mode, and the Add Connection dialog. The read-only view still shows each chosen entry's name as a link.

- **Edit** (a pencil) and **Delete** (a bin) sit beside each chosen entry: an entry the field holds, not a new entry still to be made ("Jane Smith (new)") and not an empty slot. They work on a single field and on each row of a multiple one.
- **Edit** opens the chosen entry's own form in a sheet over the one you are in, and saves it there. That form follows the entry's own type: its fields, its [show and hide settings](#showing-setting-and-one-per-section-v1450) and its value rules.
- **Delete** asks the same question every delete asks (including whether to keep the records inside it), deletes the entry, and then takes it out of the field. The delete happens at once; taking the entry out of the field is saved with the form it sits in, as any other change there. The entry goes to **Deleted**, where it can be restored. Removing an entry from the field without deleting it works as before: clear its slot.
- **Who sees the buttons.** Only someone who may do it, as on the entry's own detail page: the entry's owner, with its type's **Edit** (or **Delete**) permission. A type whose permission is admin-only needs an admin. Anyone else sees no button. An entry in Deleted shows none.
- **An unassigned entry** can't be edited until it is in a section, so its **Edit** button is greyed out, and hovering it says "Not in a Matter yet. Assign it to edit it." (the isolated type's own name in place of Matter). It can still be deleted.
- **The limit.** Once a multiple field holds `maxItems` rows, the **Add** button is replaced by "You can choose up to 10 entries." ("entry" for 1). The server counts the field's non-empty ids plus new-entry placeholders, and refuses a write that changes the value to more: `400` `Field "people" takes at most 10 entries` (`group.sub` for a sub-field). It binds every caller: request writes, in-process writes and system callers. A list stored earlier that is already over the limit stays as it is until a write changes it.
- Each chosen entry with Edit or Delete reads its own record, through the same cache the picker uses.

#### Cards or a Table: which entries, in what order (v1.89.2)

A reference field's **Display** can be **Links** (the usual names), **Badges**, **Items**, **Cards** or a **Table**. Choosing more than one gives a toggle on the page, which opens on the first one chosen.

- **Badges (v1.108.0):** each picked entry's name as a tag, linked to the entry. A tag takes the colour of the entry's own badge when its type gives it one.
- **Items (v1.108.0):** each picked entry as a row: its type's icon, its name with the type's name under it, and its own badge at the end. The row is a link to the entry.
- **Cards and Table** list only what is picked in this field on this entry, with the settings below.

**Allow adding (v1.111.0)** is one switch for the whole Display, under the display types: it puts an **Add** on the page however the field shows. Cards and a Table have theirs as before (it used to sit in their own settings); Links, Badges and Items get the same Add beside the toggle, over the field's row. It picks an entry into this field, or makes a new one where its types can be made, for someone who may edit the entry, and a field with an Add shows on the page while it is still empty. Since v1.113.0, when the field is the first one under its section's heading, the Add sits at the right of the heading's row for Links, Badges and Items too, as it does for Cards and a Table.

**Open in a side panel (v1.113.0)** is a switch beside it. On, a click on a linked entry (a link, a badge, an item, a card or a row of the table) opens that entry beside the page it was clicked on: a panel with its page's sections as a reader sees them (a tab each when it has more than one, then its side column), **Open page** to go to the entry itself, and **Back** when a link inside the panel went on to another entry. Nothing is edited in the panel. A click with Ctrl, Cmd or Shift held, or the middle button, still opens the entry's own page in a new tab. It works on a record's page; anywhere else the link goes to the entry's page as before.

**Arrange (v1.115.0)** shows while the field has Badges or Items among its display types (and for a choice field shown as Tags/Badges). **Arrange** is **In a line** (they wrap), **Grid** (with **Columns**, 2 to 6; two on a phone) or **One per line**; **Gap** is Small, Medium or Large. With nothing picked, badges sit in a line close together and items one under another, as they always have. In a grid a badge keeps its own width at the start of its column, and an item fills its column.

Links, Badges and Items read each entry's name alone, so they take an **Order** and nothing else: **As picked**, **Last picked first**, **By name, in number order** or **By name, A to Z**.

With Cards or a Table, the **Display** box has these settings:

| Setting | Stored as | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| **Number of entries** | `displayConfig.pageSize` | How many show. On a table with paging on, this is the rows per page. |
| **Pagination** | `displayConfig.paginate` | Off: a table shows the first **Number of entries** rows, with **Show all**. |
| **Order** | `displayConfig.order` | **As picked** (the default; nothing is stored), **Last picked first**, **By name, in number order** (the numbers in a name are read as numbers: "Order 2" before "Order 10"), **By name, A to Z** (plain text: "Order 10" before "Order 2"; v1.108.0), or **By a field**. |
| **Icon or initials on each card** | `displayConfig.cardIcon` | Cards only (v1.108.0). On unless switched off: a card then shows its title, lines and buttons with no icon or initials beside them. An Entries list's cards have the same switch (`entriesConfig.cardIcon`). |
| **Show only entries where** | `displayConfig.where` | A condition on the linked entries. Entries that don't match aren't shown, but the field still holds them. |

A stored `null` for `order` or `where` counts as not set.

**Order.**

- **By a field** offers the name and the date, number, text and select fields of the types the link can point at, their subtypes included.
- Then choose **Newest/highest first** or **Oldest/lowest first**.
- A select sorts by the order of its options. A date sorts as a date and a number as a number.
- Blank values always go last. Entries with the same value keep the order they were picked in.

**Show only entries where** uses the same editor and fields as the link's **Only entries where…** filter, including **Try it**. Like that filter, it tests the linked entries' own fields and built-ins (name, stage, status and so on). It can't test who is looking or a field through another link. A blank condition isn't stored.

The page works these out in a fixed order:

1. It reads the field's entries: up to 100 of them. With any order but **As picked**, it reads the last 100, so the latest picks are among them.
2. It keeps the ones that match the condition.
3. It puts them in order.
4. It shows the first **Number of entries** of them.

Cards end with a line such as "Showing 1 of 3." and a **Show all** button that shows the rest in place, up to 24 cards. **Show fewer** goes back. The total counts only the entries that match. When the field holds more than the 100 read, the total says so: "Showing 1 of 100+."

A table applies the condition and the order before its own tabs, search and paging. A column the viewer sorts by still wins. Its count says "100+" in the same way, and with **Pagination** off it ends with "Showing 5 of 12." and **Show all**.

These are a link field display's own. An **Entries list** shows cards and tables as it always has, with no **Show all**.

**Add.**

- **Allow adding** puts one Add on the list while the field has room. A field that takes one entry has no room once it holds one. To keep adding but show one, turn on **Allow multiple references** and set **Number of entries** to 1.
- When the display is the first field under a section's heading (its own section included), its Add sits in the heading's row, for cards as well as a table.
- A section's own **Add** and **Filter** buttons (Section settings, Buttons) act on the section's first **Entries list**. A section with none uses its first link field whose first display is **Cards** or **Table** (one that opens on **Links** doesn't count). The target is the same whether the page is being read or edited.
- While the page is being edited, a link field shows its picker, so the section's buttons for it wait until the edit ends.
- The section's **Add** offers the types the field may create and, while the field has room, **An existing entry**.
- There is still one Add per list: when the section has an Add button, the list shows none of its own.

**Switching to several entries.** A field that took one entry and is switched to **Allow multiple references** keeps the entry it holds. The editor shows it as the first row, and adding another keeps it.

**What a save refuses.** The shape and secret checks run on every save of a field set: type saves, field templates, imports, the configuration transfer and proposals. Each refusal is a `400` naming the field.

- `only a link field's cards or table can choose which entries show, and their order`
- `which entries show must be a condition`, and `which entries show: …` with the condition's own problem
- `which entries show can't depend on who is looking`
- `which entries show can only test the linked entries' own fields (not …)`
- `its order is as picked, last picked first, or by a field`
- `its order can only use the linked entries' own fields`
- `its order is newest or highest first (desc: true), else oldest or lowest first`
- `its display reads the secret field "…", which is never shown`. This covers a condition or an order that reads a secret field of a type the link can point at, or of one of its subtypes. When the link allows any type, a secret on any type counts.
  - A field turning secret later is caught too. When a save or an import makes such a field secret, on the linked type or a subtype, it is refused, naming the type whose display reads it.
- `its display is ordered by "…", which is not a field of the entries it links to`. The order's field must be a field of a type the link can point at, or of one of its subtypes, or the name.
  - It is checked on type saves, field templates and proposals, and after `importNodeTypes` has written every type.
  - Only an order the save brings in is checked, so a field that has since gone away never blocks an unrelated save.
  - The type being saved is read as the save leaves it, so a link to its own type can be ordered by a field the same save adds.
  - It is skipped for a link that allows any type, and for a type that doesn't exist yet.
  - The configuration transfer writes a file one item at a time, so it checks the shape and secrets alone.

An **Entries list** doesn't take `where` or `order`. Its children and "link to this one" sources page on the server, so it is refused there.

```json
{
  "id": "placements",
  "type": "node-reference",
  "label": "Placements",
  "nodeReferenceConfig": { "allowedTypes": ["placement"], "multiple": true, "allowCreate": true },
  "displayConfig": {
    "types": ["cards"],
    "allowAdd": true,
    "pageSize": 1,
    "order": { "field": "start_date", "desc": true },
    "where": { "field": "status", "equals": "current" }
  }
}
```

This holds every placement but shows only the newest current one, with "Showing 1 of 3." and **Show all**.

The JSON keys are also listed under [Field and type settings added in v1.89.2](/modules/structure/json-reference/#field-and-type-settings-added-in-v1892).

#### Connecting the chosen entries (v1.46.0)

Since v1.55.0 a `select` or `multiselect` whose records sources store links connects its picks by the same rules, unless they say `connect: false` (see [Options from other records](#options-from-other-records-and-values-already-entered-v1550)).

With `nodeReferenceConfig.connect: true`, a reference field also **connects** each entry it picks. The link shows on both entries' **Connections** tab and in graph reads, named after the field: a Matter's **People** field gives each chosen person a "People" connection from the Matter. The setting is off until an admin turns it on, so apps and modules that don't use it see no change.

```json
{
  "id": "people",
  "type": "node-reference",
  "label": "People",
  "nodeReferenceConfig": {
    "allowedTypes": ["person"],
    "multiple": true,
    "connect": true
  }
}
```

**Where it works.** On a node type's reference fields, field-group sub-fields included, and on the fields a type gets from an attached field template or inherits from a parent type: what counts is the type's effective field set. A connection type's own fields refuse `connect: true` (see [What a save refuses](#node-reference)). In the field editor the switch is **Also connect the chosen entries**, after **Maximum number of entries**, with the hint "Adds a connection between the two entries." It is on a node type's fields and a field template's, not on a connection type's fields or the **AI** tab's. Turning it off in the editor removes the key rather than storing `false`.

**The connection type is chosen (v1.71.0).** With the switch on, **Connection type** asks for one, or **Create a new one…**, and stores it as `nodeReferenceConfig.connectType`. Several fields can share one type, keeping one connection per pair. An admin save refuses a field newly turned on with no type. Changing the type moves the existing connections to it.

**Any connection type can be chosen (v1.123.0).** The list offers every connection type that is on. Since v1.125.2 the ones that already join the field's types come first, under **Already joins these types**, and the rest under **Other connection types**: one relationship between two types is one connection type, whichever of the two the field is on. Since v1.125.3 each one shows the types it joins under its name, with an arrow for its direction (↔ two-way, → one-way). Choosing one that does not take them yet says what saving adds ("Saving adds Order to its From types."), and the admin's save adds them; nothing is taken off. **Create a new one…** and **Edit** open the relationship's settings in a dialog, the same block as the Connection Types editor: the types at each end, **Two-way**, **One per pair** and [how many at each end](/modules/structure/configuration/#how-many-at-each-end-v11230). An in-process save and an import are still refused when the type does not fit.

**One relationship from both types (v1.123.0).** A connection type fits either way round, one-way or two-way: its From and To say which record is which end. So a field on each of the two types can choose the same connection type. Whichever page is saved first, the two records get one connection, stored from the type's From end.

**Older fields keep an automatic type.** A field saved before there was a choice (connect on, no `connectType`) shows **Made automatically (old)** until a type is chosen. Structure makes that type when a type is saved with the field on (a create, an update, the configuration import, `importNodeTypes`, a proposal's approval, or an edit to a field template the type attaches). A stored config, an import and a module's own config are read as they are.

- Its name and plural are the field's label, and they follow the label when it changes.
- From is the type that declares the field (its subtypes pass); To is the field's `allowedTypes`, or `["*"]` when it has none. These follow the field too.
- It is one-way (`bidirectional: false`), one per pair (`unique: true`), with no maximum.
- It is owned like the type: an app-level type gets an app-level connection type, a personal type one of its owner's.
- An inherited field connects with its parent type's connection type. A field-template field gets one for each type that attaches the template.
- Turning the switch off, or deleting the field, leaves the connection type where it is.

Its slug, its `moduleId`, the admin badge and the rules for deleting it are on [Configuration](/modules/structure/configuration/#connection-types-made-for-reference-fields-v1460).

**The connections.**

- **One per relationship (v1.124.0).** Two entries get one connection from their fields for each connection type those fields connect them with, however many fields share the type, on either entry. A reference connects the two with its field's connection type, unless an active connection of that same type already joins them, one added by hand included. Then none is made. A connection of another type between the two is a different relationship and never counts. (Before v1.124.0 it was one per pair: any connection of any type stopped the field making its own.)
- **A multiple field** connects to each entry chosen in it.
- **The last reference.** A field's connection is removed when no field that connects with its connection type, on either entry, still names the other. When the field that made it lets go but another field with the same type still holds the pair, the connection stays and now names that field. When a field's connection type is changed, its connection is changed in place to the new type and direction (`structure.connection.updated` fires), unless that type already has one between the two.
- **A connection added by hand** is never removed and never taken over by a field.
- **Every write that changes the field** keeps them in step: create, update, bulk create, a new entry made in the field (`{ "$new": … }`, once it is created), a duplicate's copies (a copied field-made link stays field-made), a merge (the canonical's references after the backfill connect; the duplicate's field-made links stay with the duplicate, because a merge doesn't re-point references), and a restore from Deleted. A restore reads the entry's own references, and each field-made link it revives, again after it has committed: a failure there is logged and never fails the restore. A delete sweeps the links, as for any connection, and a delete that keeps the contents keeps the kept entries' links. Value rules and one per section never write a reference, so they never change a link.
- **In the same transaction.** The links are written in the write's own transaction, so a write that is refused leaves no link behind. (A restore's are written just after it, as above.) After the commit, `structure.connection.created`, `structure.connection.updated` and `structure.connection.deleted` fire, as for links made by hand, and the formulas of both ends are recomputed. A bulk create with `skipHooks: true` fires none of these hooks.
- **Unassigned entries** get their links too. Assigning one to a section keeps them, filed into that section.
- **A refused link is skipped.** When the connection rules refuse a link, it is left out, a warning is logged, and the entry's save still succeeds. A request's references have already passed the [section wall](#inside-an-isolation-section-v1420), so this happens only when an admin has changed the field's connection type (narrowed its `allowGlobalTypes`, changed its From or To types, or given it a maximum), or when a system write references across two sections.
- **The owner's side.** A field-made connection belongs to the owner of the entry whose field it is. A reference to an entry of a shared type connects to it from the owner's side, and the other entry's fields count towards keeping the link only when it is the owner's entry too.

**Changed through the field.** A field-made connection can't be deleted or archived by hand, and a request can't change its metadata. Since v1.125.0 its **own fields** (a role, say) are filled in like any connection's, in a Connections field of its connection type or with `PATCH /connections/:id` and `data`. Before v1.125.0 it couldn't be edited at all. The refusal is a `400` naming the field and the entry that holds the reference, for example `This connection comes from the "People" field of "Smith & Jones". Change that field to remove it.` On the Connections tab it shows a lock with that note instead of the remove button. Which callers are refused is on [Integration](/modules/structure/integration/#reference-field-connections-v1460).

**Turning it on or off.** The existing entries follow the switch. After a save that turns it on or off, the admin page brings their connections in step at once, and the type's **Update entries** does it again later. See [Connecting the chosen entries](/modules/structure/usage/#connecting-the-chosen-entries-v1460) on Usage.

#### Value checks (v1.38.0)

The value must be a node id — letters, digits, `-` and `_`, at most 100 characters — or a list of them; an empty string marks an unset slot. Anything else in a request write is refused with **400** (`must be a node id`). When `allowedTypes` is set, a request write — create, update, duplicate, a connection's fields, or the public API — is also checked against it: each id the write **adds** must be an existing entry (the caller's own, or one of a shared type) whose type is one of `allowedTypes` or a subtype of one, otherwise the write is refused with 400 naming the field. Ids already stored in the field are not looked up again, and one write may add at most 5,000 distinct targets. A field with no `allowedTypes` is not type-checked. Both rules also apply to a `node-reference` sub-field inside field-group rows. A module writing in-process is not refused by these two rules (the `filter` and section checks above do bind it, unless it is a system caller). A value that is not a node id is dropped from the write with a server warning (from a list, only the entries that are not ids are dropped), and `allowedTypes` is not checked. Unlike `secret`, a `node-reference` field's `required` flag is enforced by the server too.

### connections

*(v1.56.0.)* Lists the entry's connections of the connection types you choose, and lets people add, edit and remove them right there. It is a connection, not a reference: a parent's record, say, can list its connection to a child, with the connection's own **Role** field saying "mother", and the same link shows on both records' **Connections** tab. The field's menu label is **Connections**, under **References**.

- **It stores nothing.** The field has no value of its own. What it shows is read live from the entry's connections, so it always agrees with the **Connections** tab, and every connection of those types shows in it, however it was made.
- **Its relationships are connection types.** A field can offer one (always the same relationship) or several (adding a connection then asks which).
- **Adding makes a connection, editing changes the connection's own fields, and removing deletes the connection** from both records, after asking. Nothing is written until the form or page is saved.

#### Holds

Nothing. Its rows are read live from the entry's connections, so it always agrees with the **Connections** tab. A write that gives it a value is refused, for every caller.

#### On the form

The connections of its relationships, with **Add**, **Edit** and **Remove** while the form or page is being edited. Nothing is written until the save. See [Adding, editing and removing connections](#adding-editing-and-removing-connections).

#### On the detail page

**The rows.** Each row shows:

- the other record's name, linked to its page, with its [badge](#the-badge-v1540) beside it ("An entry you can't see" when the reader can't read it, "…" while it loads);
- the relationship's name in brackets, always;
- then, after " — ", each shown field that has a value, as "Label: value", joined with commas. A select shows its option's label, a yes/no field Yes or No, and anything else its one-line format.

With **Show each as** set, its text replaces the row's text (still linked, with the badge). In it, a select's `{role|label}` reads the option's label; a secret field and a field that links to records are never read, and nothing is read through a link. When it comes out blank, the default row shows. With no connections the field says "No connections yet." while it is being edited, and "—" otherwise. It reads the 200 most recent connections of its relationships; past that it adds "Showing the first 200 connections."

No **Display type** choices.

#### Settings

**Connections settings**: **Relationships**, **This record is**, **Connected to**, **Only records that…**, **Where**, **Show each as**, **Show these connection fields**, **Allow adding**, **Allow editing**, **Allow removing** and **At most**. No **Required**, **Default value** or **Format and checks**.

**connectionsConfig.** Every key but `connectionTypes` is optional, and leaving one out is the same as its default. They round-trip through the configuration export and import.

| Property | Type | Default | Description |
|----------|------|---------|-------------|
| `connectionTypes` | `string[]` | required | 1 to 20 connection type slugs, each once: the relationships the field shows and offers. Admin control: **Relationships** |
| `direction` | `"either"`, `"from"` or `"to"` | `"either"` | Which end of each connection this entry is. `"either"` shows every connection of these types; `"from"` or `"to"` shows only the ones where the entry is that end, and a new connection puts it there. Admin control: **This record is** |
| `otherTypes` | `string[]` | any type the relationship allows | The node types the other record may be, with their subtypes, at most 50. The picker offers these, narrowed to the relationship's own end types. Admin control: **Connected to** |
| `filter` | condition | none | A condition on the other record, in the [reference filter](#only-some-entries-filter-v1420) grammar. The picker offers only the records that match. Admin control: **Only records that…** |
| `scope` | `"section"` or `"any"` | `"section"` | Where the picker looks once the app has an [isolated type](#inside-an-isolation-section-v1420). `"section"`: records in this entry's section only. `"any"`: those, plus the shared types the relationship allows (its `allowGlobalTypes`). A connection never crosses an isolation section either way. Admin control: **Where** |
| `label` | `string` | the default row | How each row shows: a merge template of up to 500 characters over the connection's fields, plus `{other}` (the other record's name) and `{type}` (the relationship's name). It can't read a secret field. Admin control: **Show each as** |
| `showFields` | `string[]` | every field that isn't secret | The connection fields each row shows, in this order, at most 50. Left out: every field but a secret, a section break, a field that links to records and a field group. A secret field or a field that links to records is refused. Admin control: **Show these connection fields** |
| `allowAdd` | `boolean` | `true` | The **Add** button. Admin control: **Allow adding** |
| `allowEdit` | `boolean` | `true` | An **Edit** button (a pencil) on each row, for the connection's own fields. Admin control: **Allow editing** |
| `allowRemove` | `boolean` | `true` | A **Remove** button (a bin) on each row: it deletes the connection, after asking. Admin control: **Allow removing** |
| `maxItems` | whole number, 1 to 1000 | no limit | At most this many connections. At the limit, the **Add** button is replaced by "At most 3." It is checked in the browser only: for a limit the server holds, give the connection type a **Most per entry** (`maxConnections`). Admin control: **At most** |

```json
{
  "id": "how_connected",
  "type": "connections",
  "label": "How they're connected",
  "connectionsConfig": {
    "connectionTypes": ["related"],
    "showFields": ["role"]
  }
}
```

Each of the entry's **Related** connections shows as a row such as "Sam (Related) — Role: mother". A worked example with every key is in [the JSON reference](/modules/structure/json-reference/#connections-fields-v1560).

#### Rules

**Required** and **Set the value** aren't offered: "this field shows other records and holds no value." Other rules can't test it, but it can have a **Visible when** of its own.

#### In lists and tokens

No filter, sort or table column. `{how_connected}` works out blank; a [formula](#formula) reads an entry's connections with `connections('related')`.

#### Limits and gaps

**At most** is checked in the browser only; for a limit the server holds, give the connection type a **Most per entry**. The full list is under [What a Connections field can't do](#what-a-connections-field-cant-do).

#### Adding, editing and removing connections

While a form or the detail page is being edited:

- **Add** opens a small form in place:
  1. **Connected to**, a record picker ("Search for a record..."). It offers the field's types narrowed to the relationship's end types (both ends' for **Either end**), with their subtypes, never the entry itself, inside the section wall and the **Where** setting, and only records that match **Only records that…**. When the field's types and the relationship's share none, it says "No type of record can be connected with this relationship."
  2. **Relationship** ("Pick a relationship"), only when the field offers more than one. Changing it starts the connection's fields again from their defaults.
  3. The relationship's own fields, with their defaults, required marks and checks, as on the **Add Connection** dialog. A field locked for this person (`editableBy`) shows read-only.

  Its **Add** button is greyed out until a record and a relationship are chosen and every required field is filled in. Pressing Enter in one of its inputs adds the connection without saving the whole form. **Cancel** closes it.
- **Already connected.** When the two records already have a connection of that relationship, or one is already added in this edit, the form edits that one instead of making a second. It says "Already connected: saving changes that connection's fields." and its button says **Save**. For a two-way relationship (`bidirectional`, the default) that counts either way round, as the server reads a two-way type; for a one-way one, only with the same ends. When the connection was made by a reference field, or **Allow editing** is off, it says "Already connected. This connection can't be changed here." and there is nothing to save.
- **Edit** (a pencil, shown when the relationship has fields) opens the same form for that connection's fields. To change the other record or the relationship, remove the row and add another.
- **Remove** (a bin) asks "Remove this connection?", "It's deleted from both records when you save.", with **Remove**. Removing a row added in this edit just drops it, without asking.
- **What waits for the save.** A row says **Added when you save**, **Changed when you save** or **Removed when you save**, and a changed or removed row has an **Undo** button. Under the rows, "These changes are made when you save." (on a new record, "These connections are made once the record is created.").
- **Connections made by a reference field** show like any other, but have no **Edit** or **Remove** button: change the field that made them (see [Connecting the chosen entries](#connecting-the-chosen-entries-v1460)).
- **Which end a new connection makes this record.** With **This record is** set to one end, that end. With **Either end**, the "from" end, unless the relationship takes this record's type only at its "to" end and the other record's type at its "from" end.

**When the changes are made.** The changes are kept with the form and made when it saves, through the same connection API the **Connections** tab uses. So the relationship's own rules (its **From** and **To** types, **One per pair**, **Most per entry**, the section wall) and the connection fields' checks all apply, and the usual connection hooks fire.
