- **On the detail page** they are part of the edit: **Save changes** saves the entry's fields and then makes the connection changes, **Cancel** drops them with the rest, and leaving with them unsaved asks first, as for any change.
- **On the create form** the record is created first, then its connections, in the order they were added. A connection that fails doesn't undo the record: a message says "The record was created, but this connection wasn't made:" and why.
- **On the edit form** the connection changes are made first. If one fails, the form stays open and nothing else is saved: "Not saved. This connection change couldn't be made: …".
- **A change that fails** stays waiting, with "Not saved: …" and the reason under its row, and a message lists each one ("Sam (Related): …"), for example "1 connection change wasn't saved. It's still there to try again." The changes that went through are done; save again to try the rest.
- **Safe against other people's edits.** An edit sends only the values that were changed, written over the connection's fields as they are at the save, so it never writes back what someone else changed meanwhile. If the record's connections can't be read at the save, nothing is written: "Couldn't read this record's connections, so nothing was saved (…)". A change to a connection that has gone since, or isn't this record's, is skipped. An add that matches an existing connection by the time of the save edits that one.

#### What a Connections field can't do

- **Hold a value.** It takes no default and can't be required (the **Required** switch and **Required When** aren't offered), set by a value rule, one per section, or given options from other records or suggestions. A write that gives it a value is refused for every caller: `400` `Field "how_connected" shows the connections of its entry and holds no value: add, change or remove the connections themselves`. Changing a field to this type takes those settings off.
- **Be tested or read by other settings.** **Visible When**, **Required When**, a value rule's condition and a badge rule can't test it, and a value rule can't copy it. It can have a **Visible When** of its own.
- **Sit anywhere but the top level** of a node type or a field template. It can't be a field-group sub-field, or one of a connection type's own fields, including through a field template the connection type attaches. The **AI** tab doesn't offer it. A field template carries it to every type that attaches it, and a subtype inherits it.
- **Show as text.** `{how_connected}` in a merge template works out blank, and the **Insert field...** picker, [Build it](/modules/structure/usage/#build-it-v1430) and the merge-field schema leave it out. A [formula](#formula) reads an entry's connections with `connections('related')`. It isn't offered as a table column or a card field, and a card's or list row's field line skips it.
- **Limit on the server.** **At most** is a browser limit; the connection type's **Most per entry** is the one the server holds.

**What a save refuses.** Every save of a field set checks the shape: node types, field templates, connection types, proposals and the configuration import (which answers `422` naming the item). The admin saves of node types, proposals and field templates also check what the field names; an import checks the shape only, because a relationship it names may come later in the file. Each is a `400`, most starting `Field "how_connected": `:

- `pick at least one relationship (a connection type)`, `it names one relationship twice`, `it offers more than 20 relationships` and `its connections settings have an unknown setting "x"`; a `direction` or `scope` that isn't one of its values; a `label` that isn't a merge template or is over 500 characters; an allow switch that isn't `true` or `false`.
- `"At most" must be a whole number from 1 to 1000`.
- `it shows connections and holds no value, so it cannot be required`, and the same for a default, a merge template or formula, value rules, one per section, options from other records and suggestions.
- `a Connections field cannot be inside a group` (named `group.field`), `a connection type's own fields cannot include a Connections field`, and, on a connection type that attaches one, `Field template "x" has a Connections field, which a connection type cannot take`.
- `its Visible When tests "how_connected", which shows connections and holds no value`, and the same for **Required When**, a value rule's condition (`its value rule 1 tests …`) and a badge rule (`Badge, rule 1: its condition tests …`).
- On the admin saves: `"x" is not one of the connection types`, `"x" is not one of the node types`, `"x" is not a field of its relationships`, `"x" is a secret field, which is never shown`, `"x" links to records, which a row does not show` and `how it shows each connection reads the secret field "x"`.

The type form and the **Field Templates** page check the same before they save, with the same words.

### entries-list

A list of other records shown inside a section: this entry's children, a link field's picks, entries that link to this one, or the entries connected to it (v1.69.0). Its label in the editor is **Entries list**.

```json
{
  "id": "open_tasks",
  "type": "entries-list",
  "label": "Tasks",
  "entriesConfig": {
    "source": { "referencedBy": { "type": "task", "field": "project" } },
    "display": "table",
    "columns": [{ "id": "name", "label": "Name", "template": "{name}" }],
    "filterTabsField": "status",
    "allowAdd": true
  }
}
```

#### Holds

Nothing. A write that gives it a value is refused: `Field "open_tasks" shows other records and holds no value`.

#### On the form

Not on the create or edit form. On the detail page, **Allow adding** puts one **Add** on the list for someone who may add. A section's own **Add** and **Filter** buttons act on its first Entries list.

#### On the detail page

The list, as **Show as** says:

- **Cards**: each entry's **Card title** and up to two lines, **Each card is** (a third, two thirds, the full width, Auto, or sharing the width with a **Card min width**), and up to 6 **Buttons on each card**, each going to a section of the entry's page or a list of records, optionally with a count.
- **A table**: **Columns** (each a merge template or a formula), **Pills** for a select column's options, **Filter tabs** (All, then one per option of a select field), **Rows per page** (1 to 50, 10 by default), a search box (on unless switched off), and a picture from **Photo from** or initials.
- **Show the field's name over the list** (on unless switched off).

#### Settings

**Entries list settings**: **Lists** (This entry's children, with **Only these types**; The entries a link field picks, with **Link field**; Entries of another type that link to this one, with **Linked by**; Entries connected to this one, with **Connection type**), **Show as** (Cards or A table) and the card or table settings above, and **Allow adding**. No **Required**, **Default value** or **Format and checks**. The JSON keys are under [`entriesConfig`](/modules/structure/json-reference/#the-entries-list-field-entriesconfig-v1690).

#### Rules

**Required** and **Set the value** aren't offered: "this field shows other records and holds no value." Other rules can't test it.

#### In lists and tokens

No filter, sort or table column. No token: the merge-field pickers leave it out, and a template reads it as blank.

#### Limits and gaps

- **Where the paging happens.** Children and links-here lists page, sort and search on the server. Link-field and connection lists page in the browser, within their first 100 entries.
- **No "which entries" or order.** Only a link field's own Cards or Table take **Show only entries where** and **Order**; an Entries list doesn't, because its server-paged sources can't.
- **Only on a node type or field template**, at the top level: not in a field group, a connection type or the AI tab.
- **A card button can't open a filtered list** yet; a list button opens the chosen section of the entry's page.

### list

Wording typed here in numbered or bulleted items, sub-items and headings (v1.79.0). Use it for a list you type out yourself. Its label in the editor is **List**. Before v1.90.0 it was **Numbered list** (`numbered-list`): see [Bullets, the new names and what was removed](#bullets-the-new-names-and-what-was-removed-v1900). For wording an [Assembled list](#assembled-list) copies, use an [Assembled list item](#assembled-list-item).

```json
{
  "id": "orders",
  "type": "list",
  "label": "Orders",
  "numberedConfig": {
    "levels": [
      { "style": "decimal", "suffix": "." },
      { "style": "lower-alpha", "prefix": "(", "suffix": ")" },
      { "style": "disc", "suffix": "" }
    ]
  }
}
```

#### Holds

A tree of items: `{ "items": [{ "id", "text", "children": [ … ] }] }`, and an item may also have `kind`, `numbered`, `number` and `sequence` (see [below](#headings-items-with-no-number-duplicates-and-numbering-runs-v18940)). Each item has an id that never changes. Numbers are worked out from where an item sits and are never stored. At most 5,000 characters an item, 300 items in a list (sub-items included) and 5 levels (an item goes as deep as the field has levels). The server checks the shape and the limits for every caller, and that the merge fields in the wording are allowed.

#### On the form

The items, each with **Edit wording**, **Add sub-item**, **Indent**, **Outdent**, **Move up**, **Move down**, **Duplicate**, **Take its number off** and **Remove**, and **Add item**, **Add heading** and **Add subheading** at the end. The wording box has **Merge fields** and **Insert cross reference**, which writes `[[ref:itemId]]`. An item at a bulleted level has no **Number** box.

#### On the detail page

The items, numbered or bulleted by the field's levels, with merge fields filled in live. The top level shows real numbers (since v1.90.1; before, a `#` placeholder unless **Number here** was on). No **Display type** choices.

#### Settings

**List settings**: **Levels** (up to 5, each a **Before**, a **Number** style and an **After**, the same row every list's numbering is set with since v1.116.0, where it said Style and Ending; a bulleted level has the style alone; the styles are 1, 2, 3 / a, b, c / A, B, C / i, ii, iii / I, II, III and the bullets •, ◦, ▪ and None), **Numbering sequences** (up to 6, numbered styles only) and **Also offer the fields of** (an optional extra type for **Merge fields**, `tokenType`). No **Default value** or **Format and checks**.

#### Rules

**Set the value** isn't offered: "a list of wording is typed or copied in." Other rules can't test it, and a linked-record rule can't set it.

#### In lists and tokens

No filter, sort or table column. No token: the pickers leave it out, and a template reads it as blank.

#### Limits and gaps

- **Only on a node type or field template**, at the top level: not in a field group, a connection type or the AI tab.
- **Required on the server reads an empty list as blank**; the form checks only that the field was filled at all.
- Duplicating a record gives its items new ids.
- **It doesn't go into an Assembled list** (v1.90.0). A level's **Copy from** takes an [Assembled list item](#assembled-list-item).

#### Tables (v1.118.0)

An item can hold a table under its words. It works in a List, an Assembled list item and an Assembled list: on a record's own words, on a sub-item, and on a kept item's sub-items. A heading, a subheading and a note hold none.

- **Add table** (the table button on the item's row) puts a table of two columns and one row under the item and opens it. The item's words are its lead-in or caption, such as "Table 2.1—Modifications of the Rules".
- **Columns and rows:** each column has a **Heading** box and a menu (add a column before or after, move left or right, remove). Each row has a menu (add a row above or below, move up or down, remove). **Add row** and **Add column** sit under the table. A table with every heading blank shows no heading row.
- **Paste a copied table into any box.** A table copied from a web page, Word or a spreadsheet goes in from the box it is pasted into, and the table grows to take it. Pasted into a **Heading** box, its first row becomes the headings. Bold and italics come with it from a web page or Word. What is copied is read as text and never put on the page as it came. A single cell, or words with no table in them, paste as words.
- **A cell is words as written,** with `**bold**` and `*italic*` as in an item. It takes no merge fields and no cross references: a brace or a bracket in a cell shows as typed.
- **Numbers in a table are typed.** An "Item" column is a column like any other, so numbers with gaps (4, 5, 7) or inserted ones (24A) stay as written.
- **The table is edited while the item is open** (the pen, then **Done**), with the item's words. Closed, the row shows it as it reads.
- **Where it shows:** under its item's words on the record's page, in an Assembled list's lines, in a frozen set (as it read when frozen) and in the export's print view. **Copy** gives a ruled table in its formatted copy, which Word keeps as a table, and a row a line with a tab between the cells in its text copy. The export's text has the same rows.
- **It travels with its item:** Duplicate, a duplicated record, Copy from a library and an Assembled list's copy all keep it. A change to the table counts as a change to the wording, so **Update from the original** is offered for it.
- **Remove table** sits under the table while it is open.

Stored on the item as `table`: its `columns` (each an `id` and a `label`) and its `rows` (each an `id` and `cells`, one text for each column, in column order).

```json
{
  "id": "i1",
  "text": "Table 2.1—Modifications of the Rules",
  "table": {
    "columns": [{ "id": "c1", "label": "Item" }, { "id": "c2", "label": "Provision" }],
    "rows": [{ "id": "r1", "cells": ["4", "Rule 1.05"] }]
  }
}
```

The server checks a table on every write: 1 to 12 columns, up to 300 rows, 2,000 characters a cell, 60,000 characters a table, 50 tables a list, and a cell for every column in every row. Not there yet: merged cells, column widths, a table on a note, and merge fields or cross references inside a cell.

#### Bullets, the new names and what was removed (v1.90.0)

- **New names:** **Numbered list** is now **List** (`list`), and **List item** is now **Assembled list item** (`assembled-list-item`). The migration `1.90.0__list-type-ids.sql` rewrites the stored field types in node types, field templates and connection types, field-group sub-fields included. The server also reads the old ids as the new ones, and a config import converts them. **Add Field** never offers the old ids.
- **Bulleted levels:** a level's style can be **•** (`disc`), **◦** (`circle`), **▪** (`square`) or **None** (`none`, no mark). Nothing at a bulleted level counts. **Before**, **After** and typed numbers don't apply there, so picking a bullet clears Before and Ending. A top-level item put in a numbering sequence still takes its run's number.
- **Cross references** point only at numbered items. **Insert cross reference** leaves bulleted items out, and one that points at a bulleted item shows "[cross reference to a bulleted item]". A reference to a sub-item can show its number alone (v1.100.0): **Show** asks for "3(b)" or "(b)", the second written `[[ref:<id>:number]]`. In an Assembled list's finished lines, a reference to a line shown is a link to it. With words selected in the text (v1.104.0), **Insert cross reference** opens searching for them, and the pick takes their place. When the selection names no item whole but holds a number that does ("section 61C(1)"), it searches for the number and replaces the number alone, so "section" stays. A selection longer than a line is not searched for.
- **Cross references to linked records (v1.120.0).** In a List, or an Assembled list of its own wording, **Insert cross reference** also offers the numbered items of the records the entry links to, where an Assembled list made them: a section picked in a link field, with its subsections and their sub-items, each under the record's name. The list's own items then sit under **This list**. The reference shows the number as the record's own list cites it, as last saved: "62G", "62G(2)", "62G(3)(a)". It is written the same way, `[[ref:<id>]]`, and **Show** offers the same shorter readings.
  - **Linked means saved.** A record just picked in a link field is offered once the entry is saved. An entry not saved yet offers its own items only.
  - **Unlinked means missing.** Take the record out of the link field and a reference to it shows "[cross reference missing]", with the usual warning.
  - **Every link of the entry counts:** its link fields, those in a field group, and a select's linked records. Up to 50 linked records are looked at, and 600 records numbered (the linked ones and what they hold).
  - **Read as the reader may read them.** A linked record that is not the reader's to read gives no number.
  - **Freeze** keeps the numbers as they read then.
  - **It opens the record (v1.120.1).** On the record's page the reference is a link: a click opens the linked record in the side panel, as its badge does, and with Ctrl or Cmd on its own page in a new tab. Where there is no side panel it is plain words.
  - **The name of its list (v1.120.1).** **Show** also offers the name of the list the item is in (its top record's): "Family Law Act 1975". It is written `[[ref:<id>:list]]` and goes in as a reference of its own, beside the number: "section 62G(2) of the Family Law Act 1975". A reader who may not read that record sees the number in its place.
  - **Not for an Assembled list item** (a record's own words), or a list built from records: those point within their own list.
- **Longer Before and After:** Before takes up to 20 characters and After up to 8.
- **Removed: Items from records** (`itemRecords`), with **Add from a record…**, **Add the records in** and the **Picked record** merge fields. A setting that newly adds it is refused ("items from records are no longer offered"). One already stored is dropped on the next save. Items picked from records before v1.90.0 still show, with their records' values. The editor turns them into ordinary wording on the next edit, and a write can't add a new one.

#### Subheadings, bold and italics, typed numbers and Before (v1.89.50)

These work in a List, an Assembled list item and an Assembled list.

- **Add subheading:** an italic line between items, such as "General considerations". Like a heading it takes no number, holds no sub-items and doesn't count. Stored as `kind: 'subheading'`.
- **Bold and italics:** select words in the wording box and press **B** (Bold) or **I** (Italic); select everything to style the whole item. Stored in the text as `**bold**` and `*italic*` (`\*` for an asterisk). A star inside a merge field, a formula, a record token or a cross reference is never read as styling, and a mark left open shows as typed. **Copy** gives bold and italics in its formatted copy and plain words in its text copy; Freeze and the export's print view show them.
- **Typed numbers:** an item's **Number** box (empty by default, showing the worked-out number as a hint) gives it its own number, such as `2A`, `60CC` or `ca`. It shows inside the level's Before and After, cross references show it, and it doesn't move the worked-out count along: after (a), (b) and a typed (ca), the next item is (c). Stored as `number` (1–20 characters; not on a heading, subheading or an item with no number). It doesn't apply at a bulleted level. An Assembled list's copy keeps it.
- **Before:** each numbered level has **Before** as well as **After**, so Before `(` and After `)` give (1), (a), (i). Stored as `levels[].prefix` (up to 20 characters since v1.90.0).

#### Headings, items with no number, duplicates and numbering runs (v1.89.40)

These work in a List, an Assembled list item and an Assembled list.

- **Add heading:** a bold line between items, such as "Independent Children's Lawyer" or "THE COURT NOTES THAT:". It takes no number, holds no sub-items, doesn't count and doesn't restart the numbering (orders 1 to 4, the heading, then 5). Stored as an item with `kind: 'heading'`.
- **Take its number off / Give it a number:** an item with no number doesn't count, and its text lines up with the items beside it, like "(“the children”)." under a. and b. Stored as `numbered: false`. Cross references can't point at a heading or an item with no number; an existing one shows "[cross reference to an item with no number]".
- **Duplicate:** copies the item and its sub-items right below, with new ids (cross references inside keep their targets).
- **Numbering runs:** **Numbering sequences** (a **Name**, then **Before**, **Number** and **After**) work in a List and in an Assembled list's wording: notations can run A, B while orders run 1, 2. Each top-level item picks its run.
- **Real numbers at the top** (List, v1.90.1): a List always numbers its top level, so **Number here** is gone. An old `numberedConfig.topLevel` is ignored and dropped on the field's next save.
- **Merge fields** in the wording box: the same levelled picker the admin builder uses, with only what people can use. The entry's fields at the top, linked records as levels, Built-in a level down, then **Also offer the fields of** (an optional extra type, which replaces "Merge fields come from"), and **Formats and text…** last. Secret fields, hidden fields and types the person can't read are never offered.
- **Editing a copied item (v1.89.45):** in an Assembled list, **Edit** *(the record)* (the pen) on an item copied from another record's Assembled list item opens that record's form at its list. When you save, a copy you haven't changed here takes the new wording, and a copy you have changed keeps yours, with **Update from the original** offered (on copies at any level). **Edit the wording here** changes just this copy. If you can't edit the record, the pen edits the wording here instead. Frozen sets work as before.
- **Copy and Freeze** show headings bold where the numbers start, and items with no number where their neighbours' text starts.

Settings: `numberedConfig.sequences`. The server checks the item keys on every write (headings hold no sub-items). Items from records, added here in v1.89.40, were removed in v1.90.0 (see [above](#bullets-the-new-names-and-what-was-removed-v1900)).

### assembled-list-item

One numbered item with its sub-items, such as a single clause or subsection, plus any heading or subheading around it (v1.89.50). It is the wording an [Assembled list](#assembled-list) copies. Its label in the editor is **Assembled list item**. Before v1.90.0 it was **List item** (`list-item`). It holds the same shape as a [List](#list), `{ "items": [ … ] }`, plus, since v1.91.0, its own number: `{ "items": [ … ], "number": "IA", "auto": true }` (both optional; `auto` is only ever `true`). It has at most one top-level item that isn't a heading or subheading (the main item). The server refuses a second, and the form says "An Assembled list item holds one main item."

```json
{
  "id": "text",
  "type": "assembled-list-item",
  "label": "Text",
  "numberedConfig": {
    "numberField": "number",
    "levels": [
      { "style": "decimal", "prefix": "(", "suffix": ")" },
      { "style": "lower-alpha", "prefix": "(", "suffix": ")" },
      { "style": "lower-roman", "prefix": "(", "suffix": ")" }
    ]
  }
}
```

- **On the form:** the List editor. Once the main item exists, **Add sub-item**, **Add heading** and **Add subheading** stay, and a second top-level item isn't offered.
- **Its number (v1.91.0):** with **Number field** set to **Its own** (the default, no `numberField`), the number is part of the list item: the main item's **Number** box writes the value's `number`, and other fields read it as `{field.number}` (merge fields, names, descriptions and formulas; the merge field picker offers it as **Its number**). In an Assembled list it can be automatic (see [Automatic or typed numbers](#numbers-insert-and-names-v1940)). A value from before v1.91.0 with the number on its main item reads as its number, and moves to `number` on its next save. With **Number field** set to a text or number field of the type, the main item shows that field's value ("Number from *(the field)*"), as before. With no number it shows `#`.
- **Settings:** **Assembled list item settings**: **Number field** (**Its own**, or a field), **Levels** (with Before and After, or bullets) and **Also offer the fields of**, as for a List. Numbering sequences don't apply.
- **In an Assembled list:** a level's **Copy from** names a type and its Assembled list item. On a level with no **Makes**, a copy brings the headings, the main item (with the record's Number field value as its number, refreshed by **Update from**) and its sub-items. On a level with **Makes**, the copy is a new record, with this list copied into the new record's list item. That value keeps a link to the original, `source: { node, field, mark }`, which **Update from the original** reads. See [Copy from a library](#copy-from-a-library-v1940).
- Everywhere else it behaves like a List: no filter, sort, table column or token.

### assembled-list

A finished list in levels, numbered as one and able to be frozen (v1.79.0; built around levels since v1.90.0, simplified in v1.94.0). Its label in the editor is **Assembled list**, under **References**. Its levels either make records of a type, all edited from the top record, or hold wording copied from Assembled list items or typed here. Not sure which to use? For a list you type out yourself, use a [List](#list). For a list put together from clauses kept in other records, or from records inside the entry such as Parts and Sections, use an Assembled list.

```json
{
  "id": "contents",
  "type": "assembled-list",
  "label": "Contents",
  "assembledConfig": {
    "levels": [
      { "style": "upper-roman", "prefix": "Part", "suffix": "—", "makes": "part", "indent": 0 },
      { "style": "decimal", "suffix": "", "makes": "section", "numbers": "typed", "indent": 0 },
      { "style": "decimal", "prefix": "(", "suffix": ")", "makes": "subsection", "include": ["items", "note"], "indent": 1 },
      { "style": "lower-alpha", "prefix": "(", "suffix": ")", "indent": 2 },
      { "style": "lower-roman", "prefix": "(", "suffix": ")", "indent": 3 }
    ],
    "copy": true,
    "freeze": true
  }
}
```

Here the entry holds Parts, each Part holds Sections, and each Section holds Subsections, whose sub-items number (a) and (i). A Part's heading line reads "Part VII—Children".

#### Holds

`{ "entries": [ … ], "frozen"?, "freezeLog"?, "leftOut"? }`.

- **With no level that makes records,** `entries` holds the wording: items copied from Assembled list items (each keeping `source: { node, field, item, mark }`) and items typed here. At most 1,000 items, 5 levels deep and 6 sequences.
- **When the first level makes records,** the records are the items. `entries` then holds only the headings, subheadings and notes among the top's records: `{ id, text, kind, before? }` (`kind` is `heading`, `subheading` or `note`), where `before` is the id of the record it sits before.
- `leftOut` (v1.89.47) lists the copied items someone removed from the set (`{ node, field, item }`, at most 1,000), so they aren't offered again as new. Freeze and Unfreeze keep it.
- `frozen` (the finished text, with who and when) and `freezeLog` are written by the server alone: a value sent with them has them replaced by what is stored. A frozen set can't be changed on any write path, and the editor can't save its records.

#### On the form

- **With no level that makes records:** **Copy from** *(the first level's library)*... picks a library record and copies its Assembled list item in at the end. **Add item** adds wording of its own, and **Add heading** and **Add subheading** add lines between items. On an item, **Copy from** *(the next level's library)* **under it...** copies a record's item in under that item. Each item can be edited, moved, indented, duplicated, put in a sequence, or updated with **Update from the original** when the original has changed ("Any changes made to it in this set are lost."). Editing here never changes the original.
- **When the first level makes records:** the records editor (see [Records at each level](#records-at-each-level-v1940)).

#### On the detail page

The set, numbered by position across the whole set, with any sequences' own runs (A-1, A-2). Cross references show the current number, and "[cross reference missing]" when their target isn't in the set or in a record the entry links to ([v1.120.0](#list)); merge fields fill in live and are flagged when blank. **Copy** gives plain text and HTML with real numbers and indents. **Freeze** (for someone who may edit) stores the finished text; **Unfreeze** (an admin, recorded) opens it again. The freeze log lists who did each and when. **Copy** and **Freeze** show only when switched on in the settings. No **Display type** choices.

When the first level makes records, the page shows the records live, in order: each record at a heading level as its heading line, each record at the list item level as a numbered paragraph with its sub-items, and the headings, subheadings and notes in their places, each at its level's indent. Spaces typed before a line's words are left out (v1.98.1). Someone who can edit sees "Some records have changed in the library. Edit to update." when a copied record's original has changed. The field always shows there, with or without a heading at its top: its records are read live, so it never counts as an empty field (fixed in v1.97.0; before, a list with nothing stored in the field itself was hidden outside edit mode).

#### Levels (v1.94.0)

An Assembled list is one list of levels (`levels`, up to 8). In its settings each level is a card of three rows:

1. **Before** (`prefix`) · **Number** (`style`, as for a [List](#list): numbered, bulleted or None) · **Suffix** (`suffixStyle`: **A, B, C**, the default, or **a, b, c**) · **After** (`suffix`). A Before that ends in a letter or digit gets a space before the number, so `Part` reads "Part I"; `(` stays tight, as in "(1)".
2. **Makes** (`makes`): a node type, or none. Each item at this level is then a record of that type. The levels that make records come first, so **Makes** shows only on the first level and on a level after one that makes records; clearing it clears it on the levels after too. · **Copy from** (`copyFrom: { type, field }`): a library type and its Assembled list item, whose records can be copied in at this level (see [Copy from a library](#copy-from-a-library-v1940)).
3. **Indent** (`indent`): how far the level's lines sit in, **None** or 1 to 7; left unset, a level sits one step in from the level before it (v1.98.1; before, by its place among all the levels, so sub-items jumped far in once the levels above were pulled back). Sub-items past the last level keep stepping in the same way. · **Can include** (`include`): which of **Heading**, **Subheading**, *(the level's type)* (`items`) and **Note** can be added at this level; all four when unset. · **Numbers** (`numbers`): **Automatic** or **Typed** (`"typed"`), how a new record's number starts. · **Suffixes** (`suffixes`): **Automatic**, which gives each record an **Insert** button, or **Typed** (`"typed"`). **Suffix**, **Numbers** and **Suffixes** show on levels that make records. · **Starts again** (`continues`, v1.95.0; on a level that makes records, past the first): **In each record above** (the default: a Part's Divisions count from 1 in each Part) or **Never** (`true`: the level's automatic numbers run on through the whole list, such as sections across Parts).

**The last level that makes records is the list item level:** its records are numbered paragraphs. Records at the made levels above it show as heading lines. Once a level makes records, every card is a level that makes them, and **Add level** asks for the new level's type (**Pick a type**) before adding its card after the last one.

**Add above (v1.106.0).** Each level card has **Add above**: it puts a new level above that one (Chapters over Parts) and the levels from there move down one. On a level that makes records it asks for the new level's type first.

- **A type of its own, or the same type as an Optional level (v1.110.0).** A record's level is the first level below its holder's that makes its type. With a Chapter type of its own, the records already in a list read as they did. With the same type as the level below (one type for Chapters, Parts and Divisions), **Add above** puts the new level in as **Optional**, and they read as they did too.
- **Optional (v1.110.0)** is a switch on a level whose type a later level makes too. An optional level can be left out: a record of that type sits at the next level down unless it was added as one of the optional level's. So the top of the list offers both **+ Chapter** and **+ Part**, a Chapter knows it is a Chapter (its item keeps the level's id, `at`), and a Part with no Chapter over it is still a Part. Switched off, every record of that type at that place is one of that level's, so the ones already there read a level up (a Part as a Chapter, a Division as a Part); switching it on again puts them back.
- **A record's own page reads from the level it is really at (v1.110.0):** a Part's own part of the list starts at Division and a Chapter's at Part, whichever level first makes their type.
- **A record's level, picked on its row (v1.112.0).** Where more than one level under a record's holder makes its type, its row in the list's editor starts with a small picker of those levels, named by each level's Before ("Chapter", "Part", "Division"; "Level 3" where a level's Before has no letters). Picking one puts the record at that level, with its number and its name re-read on the save; what it holds stays in it and reads from the level it now sits under. So a Part that should have been a Chapter is one pick, not a delete. Since v1.115.1 the picker needs nothing saved first: a level with no id of its own goes by its place among the levels ("L2"), and the next save of the type keeps that. Every record added in the editor says the level it was made at, so a level put in above it later never changes what it is.
- To put existing records under the new level, add a record of it in the list's editor and use **Move to…** on each, or **Demote** (below).
- **Names that follow numbers (v1.115.1).** A type that works its own name out from its number (`Item {number}`) is named again after a save has given every record its number, so a record put first, or one taken out, renames the records after it. Before, each took the name the next one still had, was refused it, and kept its old name. Two records whose names really come out the same still keep what they had, and the server logs it once. Names left behind by a renumbering before v1.115.1 are put right by **Update existing entries** (offered when the type's name template is changed and saved), which since v1.116.2 tries a name again once the entry that held it has its own.
- If the list has no anchor level, new records at the top are of the new top level only.

**Sub-items** (`subItems`, v1.100.0) are part of their record, never records of their own, and are set on the card of the level whose records have them: one row for each step down, with **Before**, **Number**, **After**, **Indent** and **Remove**, and **Sub-items under these** to add a step, up to four.

- **The last level** has sub-items always. With no rows it reads "Numbered a., i." and **Change** puts those two rows on the card to edit, for example to `(` and `)` for (a) and (i).
- **A level above it** has none until **Add sub-items** puts a row on its card. Its records then read as their heading line with the sub-items under it, such as "5 Definitions", then (a) and (b).
- **Stored on the level:** each row is `{ style, prefix?, suffix, indent? }`. A list saved before v1.100.0, with levels kept after the last one that makes records, still reads the same way: those levels number the last level's sub-items until it has rows of its own, and its card shows them as its rows. Since v1.116.1 that is one rule in one place: such levels are read as the last level's Sub-items everywhere, and the next save of the type stores them that way (the levels then end at the last one that makes records). (v1.97.0 to v1.98.1 showed each as a card of its own; v1.99.0 put them on the last level's card only.)

**Headings are not a level.** There are two kinds, and where each is kept differs:

- **A heading, subheading or note among the records** (**+ Heading**, **+ Subheading**, **+ Note** at a place) is a line connected to nothing. It is kept in the record the list is on (at the top), or in the record it sits inside, never in the records beside it. Set **Can include** on the level whose records it sits among. A note's row has **Bold** and **Italic** for the words selected (v1.100.2), so a note can open in bold: "**UPON APPLICATION MADE TO THE COURT** by Ms Smith". Stored in its text as `**bold**` and `*italic*`.
- **A record's own heading** (**+ Its heading**, under a record of the list item level, v1.100.1) is kept in that record, with its number and words, and shows above them wherever the record shows. It is offered where the level's **Can include** has *Heading*, and can be made a subheading.

So a set of court orders has one level that makes the orders, with its **Sub-items** for (a) and (i): "THE COURT ORDERS THAT:" is a heading at the top, kept in the record the list is on, and "Section 67ZBD order" is the first order's own heading, kept in that order. The levels that make records come first, so a first level set to **No records** leaves **Makes** off the levels after it.

A level whose type has required fields with no default or value rule says so on its card (v1.94.3): the list fills in only a record's number and words, so such a record can't be added from it.

The list also has **Copy** and **Freeze**, and **Numbering sequences** when no level makes records.

**Items kept in the list (v1.101.0).** In a list built from records, the same setting is named **Items kept in the list**: each one is a kind of numbered item that is not a record, such as a set of orders' Notations (A., B.). Give it a **Name**, a **Start**, a style and an **Ending**. **Add level** offers it too, as **No record (kept in the list)** in place of a type.

**A kept item's sub-items, merge fields and cross references (v1.114.0).** A kept item is written the way a record's paragraph is: click its words for **Bold**, **Italic**, **Merge fields** and **Insert cross reference**, and add sub-items under it (and sub-items under those). Its merge fields read the record the list is on (a set of orders' own fields), and its cross references reach any record's item in the list ("Orders 1 and 3 above"). Each kind has its own **Sub-items** rows under it in the settings, one for each step down, with a Before, a number style, an After and an Indent: I. then i. then 1., say. With none set they are numbered a., i. Sub-items are kept with their item in the list's own record, as the item is.

**One editor for every kind of words (v1.116.0).** A plain **Note** is written in the same editor as a record's paragraph and a kept item: click its words for **Bold**, **Italic**, **Merge fields** and **Insert cross reference** (it had a box with Bold and Italic of its own). It takes no number and no sub-items. A note's merge fields, and a kept item's, read the record whose list holds it: a note at the top reads the record the list is on, and a note inside a Part reads that Part. A heading and a subheading stay one line of words as typed.

- **Adding one:** its name is a button at the top of the list, beside the first level's records and **+ Heading** ("+ Notation"). It is a line of words with its number, with **Bold** and **Italic**, and can be moved among the top's records and headings.
- **Numbering:** each kind counts on its own through the list, in order, apart from the records' numbers.
- **Where it is kept:** in the record the list is on, with its headings and notes (a note with a `sequence`). No record is made. One whose kind is later removed from the settings reads as a note.
- **Not yet:** sub-items, a typed number, and cross references to one.

#### Anchor level (v1.96.0)

Below the level cards, **Anchor level** picks one level that makes records as the list's anchor (`anchor: true` on that level): the unit everything hangs off, such as an Act's sections. "What is below it is named and referred to from it. The levels above it can be skipped."

- **The levels above it are optional groupings.** At the top, and under a record of any level above the anchor, the add buttons offer every level down to the anchor that the place's type may hold: a Section at the top, or straight under a Part or a Division.
- **A record below it is named from its anchor record,** when its type gives no name of its own: the anchor record's number (no Before or After), then each level's marker down to it, such as "60CA(1)". When the anchor record's number changes, the next save renames what is below it.
- **A cross reference reads in full from the anchor record:** "60CA(1)(a)", not "1(a)"; one to the anchor record itself reads "60CA". In the editor, **Insert cross reference** offers every numbered item of every record in the list, not only the record's own.
- **Or its number alone (v1.100.0):** when the picked item's own number reads differently from its reference, **Show** asks which to put in: "61C(1)" or "(1)", "61C(1)(a)" or "(a)". The number alone is written `[[ref:<id>:number]]`, so "Subsection (1) has effect" follows the subsection if it is renumbered.
- **Demote and Promote (v1.109.0):** a record's row has **Demote** where the record just before it in its place may hold its type: it goes into that record, last, a level down, with everything it holds (a Part put in the Part before it becomes that Part's Division, its sections still in it). **Promote** is the other way: it comes out of the record it is in and sits right after it. The record is the same record either way, so nothing is deleted or made again, and its automatic number follows its new place. Neither shows for a type that only one level makes (no record of its own type can hold it).
- **Or from inside its section (v1.108.0):** for an item below the anchor level, **Show** offers a third reading, the reference without its anchor record's number: "8.01(3)(b)", "(3)(b)" or "(b)". It is written `[[ref:<id>:within]]`. It is offered only where it reads differently from the other two (for a paragraph itself it is the same as its number alone).
- **Or from any part of it down (v1.109.0):** for a deeper item, **Show** offers every reading, longest first: "8.01(3)(b)(i)", "(3)(b)(i)", "(b)(i)" and "(i)". An in-between one is written by how many parts it keeps, `[[ref:<id>:2]]` for "(b)(i)" (2 to 9). A list with no anchor level offers its sub-items the same way: "3(a)(i)", "(a)(i)", "(i)".
- **A link (v1.100.0):** on the record's page, a cross reference to a line shown in the same list is a link that brings that line into view.
- **The level's own numbers are as its card sets them:** automatic or typed, and starting again or running on.
- **On a made record's own page (v1.119.4),** a reference reads as its top record's page reads it. One to an item on the page is worked out with the record itself above what it holds ("62G(2)" on section 62G's page; it read "2" before). One to an item elsewhere in the list, such as another section, reads its number from the top record's list, as last saved (it read "[cross reference missing]" before). On the own page of a record below the anchor level, the anchor record still can't be seen, so references to items on the page read from that record down. Names are still kept from the top record's list on every save.

#### Records at each level (v1.94.0)

When the first level has **Makes**, the list is built from records. The top record holds records of the first level's type, each of those holds records of the next level's type, and so on: each is made inside the record above. Everything is edited from the top record.

- **Heading line:** Before, the number and its suffix, After, then the record's words: "Part VII—Children". A space goes before the words unless the marker ends in a space or a dash.
- **Which records:** only the made types the reader can read, active, in the reader's primary scope, in the order they're arranged. A record uses the level at its depth when that level makes its type, else the first level that makes its type.
- **On the form, one row per record:** a drag handle, the level's Before, its number box and the level's After ("Part [VII] —"), **Automatic number** (and **Insert**, when the level's Suffixes is Automatic), then its words edited in place (**Edit wording**), with bold, italic, **Merge fields** and **Insert cross reference**; at the list item level also **Add sub-item**. The words' own line shows no second number and none of a list's item buttons (v1.94.1); a sub-item keeps its own. Then **Edit details** (the record's own form over the list, for its other fields; on a saved record you may edit, once the list's changes are saved), **Move up** and **Move down**, **Demote** and **Promote** (v1.109.0, below), **Move to…** (any record that may hold it, or **The top**) and **Remove** ("When you save, it goes to Deleted with what it holds."). A record that holds others has a chevron (v1.97.0): **Hide what it holds** folds its contents away, and they are not drawn until **Show what it holds**; **Show all** and **Hide all** are above the list. A list of more than 60 records opens with the records of its anchor level folded (with no anchor level: of the level above its paragraphs), so a long list stays light.
- **Add buttons, always showing:** under each record, and at the top, buttons for what the next level can include: **+ *(the level's Before, when it has words, such as Division; else its type)***, **Copy from *(its library)***, **+ Heading**, **+ Subheading** and **+ Note**. Save the entry first: a new entry has nothing inside it yet. Since v1.94.3, where the levels after a record's own make its own type (optional groupings, such as Division and Subdivision under a Part), the first level after them that makes another type is offered too: a Section can go straight under a Part or a Division. Each is offered only where the place's type may hold it (its allowed children), and a button's tooltip says which record it adds to.
- **Its heading (v1.100.1):** under a record of the list item level, **+ Its heading** puts a heading above the record's number and words, kept in the record itself (its Assembled list item), not in the list around it. It shows above the record's row, with **Make it a subheading** and **Remove**. One for each record, where the level's **Can include** has *Heading*. **+ Note** beside it adds a line with no number under the record's words.
- **Saving:** **Save changes** and **Discard** appear with a count of changes. The changes are checked as a whole first, and if one is refused nothing is saved ("Nothing was saved: …"). They are then written one by one, through the same create, edit, move, reorder and delete as everywhere else, with the same history, rules and audit. A change refused while writing stops the save there ("Saved 1 of 2 changes, then stopped: …"). Unsaved changes are kept while the editor is away (v1.94.3: the form around it saved or closed first) and are there when it shows again, until **Save changes**, **Discard** or a reload of the page. Enter in a number or heading box never submits the form around the list. The entry's own **Save changes** saves the list too (v1.104.0): on the detail page and in the edit form, the list's changes are saved first, then the entry. If the list can't be saved it says why, and the edit stays open with the entry not saved yet. The list's own **Save changes** still saves the list alone, and **Cancel** on the entry leaves the list's changes kept as before.
- **Add, on the record's page (v1.100.0):** someone who can edit the entry has an **Add** button above the list. It opens the same editor under the list's lines for what is added only: no record that is there already shows in it. Its buttons are those of the list's end, each named by its record ("In 60CI", "In Division 1", "In Part 2"), then the top's. A new record shows with its number and words, and its own add buttons under it; what is added stays at the end (no order buttons or **Move to…**). **Save changes** writes it and the lines above update; **Done** puts the editor away. Additions not saved yet are kept apart from the form's, and **Add** opens again with them. It is off while the list stops short of its records.
- **Add to a record (v1.102.0):** on the record's page, a line of a record that can take something (a Part, a Division, a Section) shows a small **+** at its end when the line is hovered or focused: **Add to *(its number)***. It opens the same add editor directly under that record, with only that record's buttons ("In 60CA"). Records of the list item level have none, and it is off while **As at** is on another day.

#### In effect, Replace and Stop (v1.102.0)

A record of the list can say when it is in effect, so text that is amended stays on record and the list still reads as it stands today.

- **Replace, by adding a number that is already there:** in the editor and in **Add**, a new record whose number matches a record in effect is checked against it. The match is at the same level in the same place, or anywhere in the list for the anchor level and for a level whose numbers never start again.
  - **Same words, or no words yet:** the row says "60CA is already there." and nothing is added. What you add inside it goes into the record that is there, checked the same way, so re-entering a section with one changed paragraph replaces that paragraph alone.
  - **Other words:** the row says "Replaces 60CA from" with a date (today unless changed). Saving keeps the old record, stopped that day, and puts the new one right after it with the same number.
- **Stop:** in the editor, a saved record has **Stop**: "Stopped from", a date, then **Stop**. It stays on record and is no longer in effect from that day. **In effect again** undoes it.
- **In the editor** every record shows, in effect or not, with a badge: "Replaced 1 Jul 2024", "Stopped 1 Jul 2024", "From 1 Jul 2026", or "Stops 1 Jul 2027" for a day still to come.
- **The record's page folds (v1.107.0).** A list built from records folds at its anchor level (with no anchor level, at the level above its paragraphs): each record there keeps its first line, with a small arrow after its words that shows or hides what it holds. A list of more than 200 lines opens folded, a shorter one opens whole, and **Expand all** and **Collapse all** sit with the list's buttons. Only what is open is drawn, so a long list opens quickly. A list whose records are its paragraphs (a set of orders: one level) folds by its headings instead (v1.110.0): each heading keeps its place, and everything after it down to the next heading folds away with it, whether the heading is kept in the list or is an order's own. A heading with nothing before the next one has no arrow, and what comes before the first heading never folds. **Copy** and **Freeze** take the whole list either way, a cross reference to a folded line opens its record and goes to it, and adding to a folded record opens it. The browser's own Find reads only what is open: use **Expand all** first to search the whole list.
- **A big list loads in portions (v1.117.0).** A list of more than 400 records opens with its records down to its anchor level only (with no anchor level, down to the level above its paragraphs): an Act's Chapters, Parts and sections, not what each section holds. What a record there holds is read when you open it, on the page and in the editor, and stays for as long as the page is open. So a list of any length opens about as fast as a short one, and the old stop at 2,000 records is gone for these lists.
  - **It opens folded.** Each arrow says how much the record holds. **Expand all** (the page) and **Show all** (the editor) read everything, a few records a request.
  - **Numbers still read right.** A cross reference to something not read yet shows the right number. It becomes a link once its record has been opened.
  - **Editing is the same.** You can change, move or remove what is there without opening it; a record removed goes with what it holds. Open a record to add inside it or to change what it holds. An edit under way keeps going as more is opened.
  - **Insert cross reference** offers what has been read: open a record first to point at something inside it.
  - **Copy** waits until the whole list is here: use **Expand all** first. **Freeze** takes the whole list either way.
  - **Read whole as before:** a list of 400 records or fewer, a list with one level of records, and a list that keeps numbered items (their numbers count through the whole of it). A save, Freeze and an amendment's own list always read everything.
  - **Set an anchor level** for the best of this. Without one, a record that sits straight under a Part (with no Division between) comes with the first read, so a list that never uses its middle level still loads most of itself at once.
- **On the record's page** only what is in effect shows. Once any record has a date, the list has **As at** (a date, today unless changed) and **Show replaced**, which shows the others in their place, each marked "Replaced 1 July 2024", "Stopped 1 July 2024" or "From 1 July 2026". **Copy** and **Freeze** take the list as at that day.
- **Numbers:** a record not in effect does not count, and keeps the number it last had. Its replacement takes that number, so nothing after it moves. A cross reference to an item of a replaced record still reads its number.
- **Where it is kept:** in the record's own Assembled list item, as `from`, `until` (the first day it is no longer in effect), `replaces` and `replacedBy`. A merge field can read **In effect from** (`{field.from}`) and **In effect until** (`{field.until}`).
- **Refused, with nothing saved:** replacing a record that is of another type, in another place, not in effect or replaced already; a day that is not a real date; a stop on or before the day it starts.
- **Where a change came from:** see Amendments, below (v1.103.0).
- **Not yet:** a date on a record that replaces nothing.

#### Amendments (v1.103.0)

A list built from records can keep a record of each change: where a Replace or a Stop came from.

- **Set up:** in the list's settings, **Amendments** picks the type those records are. It is a type that fits as a level's does (shared under a shared type, never an isolated type or the list's own type), and one that no level makes. If the list's type limits what it can hold (its allowed children), allow the amendment type there too.
- **In the editor and in Add:** a Replace row and a Stop row have a picker beside the date: no amendment, one of the list's, or a new one (type its name, then **Add**). The picker's words use the type's name ("No amendment", "New amendment…"). A new one is made when the list is saved, inside the list's own record. The next Replace or Stop starts from the one last picked.
- **On the list's page:** with **Show replaced** on, a mark names the amendment: "Replaced 1 July 2024 (Family Law Amendment Act 2024)". In the editor it is a second badge beside the date.
- **On the amendment's page:** the amendment type gets a field of its own, set by the server and named after the list ("Sections changes"). It shows what that amendment replaced, stopped or started, each marked with the day, in its place under the records it sits in and numbered as the list is. A record it replaced or stopped shows with all it holds. It is read only, has nothing to set, and is left off the create form. It can be renamed and moved like any field.
- **Where it is kept:** in each record's Assembled list item, beside its days: `fromBy` (the amendment its first day comes from) and `untilBy` (the amendment its last day comes from). A Replace writes both: the old record's `untilBy` and the new one's `fromBy`. **In effect again** takes the amendment off with the day.
- **Refused, with nothing saved:** an amendment that is not one of the list's; a new one whose name is there already; an amendment named for a day the record does not have.
- **Limits:** an amendment is made in the list's own record and records the changes to that list only. Taking **Amendments** off the list removes that field from the amendment type; the records keep what they say, and setting it again brings the field back.

#### Numbers, Insert and names (v1.94.0)

- **Automatic** (the button pressed): the record's place among its level's records, in the level's style. After each save the numbers that changed are written into the records' list items, so `{<item field>.number}` reads "II". A level set to **Starts again: Never** (v1.95.0) counts through the whole list, not in each record above; Insert follows the last counted record wherever it sits. A made record's own page can't see the records before it, so it shows such numbers as last saved, and a save made there numbers the whole list again from its top record.
- **Typed:** turn **Automatic number** off and type, such as IA, 9A or 9AA. A typed number doesn't count toward the places. Turning it back on drops the typed number.
- **No number** (v1.94.2): **Take its number off** (the # button on the row) leaves the record with none, no Before or After either, such as a section's only paragraph, which reads under the section's number. It doesn't count toward the places, and a number it had stored is cleared, so `{<item field>.number}` reads blank. **Give it a number** puts it back. Stored as its main item's `numbered: false`.
- **Insert** (when the level's Suffixes is Automatic): the record doesn't count toward the places; it takes the number of the record before it at its level plus the next suffix letter in the run: 9, 9A, 9B, 10. The run starts again after each counted record. An inserted record first at its level shows the letter alone ("A"). Stored as `insert: true`, with the shown number ("9A") written after the save.
- **Names are never typed here.** A record's name is its type's automatic name when it has one; else its number and words ("9A Use of protected names"), or the type name and number when it has no words ("Section 9A"). Names follow on records a save made or changed, or whose number moved. Names under one record must differ, so a fallback name already taken gets " (2)". A type whose automatic name comes out the same for two records under one parent keeps it on the first; the others keep the list's name (the save still goes through). Read the record's own number in the name to keep them apart, such as `{^section->contents_item.number}({contents_item.number})` for "60CA(1)". Below an [anchor level](#anchor-level-v1960) a record is named as it is cited instead, such as "60CA(1)".

#### Headings, subheadings and notes (v1.94.0)

They go between records at the top, and inside any record whose type has its own list. Inside a paragraph of the list item level, **+ Note** (v1.94.2, shown under it when its level can include notes) adds a line with no number under its words, kept in its list item as a sub-item with `numbered: false`; it shows indented under the paragraph, and works even when no levels follow for sub-items. Each is stored as `{ id, text, kind, before? }` in the list of the record it sits in: the top's field, or the made type's own list. It is attached to the record after it (`before`); with no record after it, it sits at the end, and a record then added at that end goes after it (v1.94.2). When that record is moved away or removed, it moves to the next record. A **Note** is plain words with no number, shown at its level's indent. Each sits in by the level of its place (the level after its holder's), however deep that record is (v1.94.3). **Only the list's own save writes them** (v1.94.3): any other write of the field, such as a form saved with the copy it loaded earlier, keeps them as stored.

#### Made types' fields (v1.94.0)

Saving the top type gives the made types what they need, after one question ("Add list fields to *(types)*?", **Add and save**):

- **Every made type** gets an [Assembled list item](#assembled-list-item) `<top field id>_item` (made unique when taken), labelled with the top list's label and " item", with `numberedConfig.setOn: { type, field, level }` written by the server. It holds the record's number (`number`, `auto`, `insert`) and its words, and at the list item level its sub-items. Other fields read the number as `{<field id>.number}`, such as `{contents_item.number}`.
- **Every made type above the list item level** also gets an Assembled list of its own, set on the top list (`assembledConfig.setOn`, the top field's id or `<id>_list`), with the top's Copy and Freeze. It keeps the headings, subheadings and notes among that record's records, and on that record's own page shows its part of the list, with the record's own line first (v1.119.4; read only there).
- **In the field list they carry the badge "Assembled list field"** (v1.119.1), with the top type in its tooltip, so the list's fields can be told from the type's own. An amendment type's list of what it changed carries it too.
- **Their settings boxes** show only "Set on *(top type)*": everything is set on the top type. The fields are re-synced whenever the top type's fields are saved. A type that leaves the list keeps its fields and values, without `setOn`.
- **A type can be part of one list only.** Making it in a second list is refused ("… is already set by …; it can be part of one list only").

#### Copy from a library (v1.94.0)

**Copy from** names a library type and its Assembled list item. The library is only read.

- **On a level with no Makes,** a library record's Assembled list item is copied in as wording. Each copy keeps where it came from, so **Update from** *(the record)* and **Update from the original** work (see [Following the records](#following-the-records-v18947)).
- **On a level with Makes,** **Copy from *(the library)*** picks a library record. It makes a new record of the Makes type in that place, with the library record's number (typed) and its Assembled list item copied into the new record's list item, keeping a link to the original (`source: { node, field, mark }`). The new records are made when you save.
- **It copies what the record holds too:** when the next level makes records and has its own **Copy from**, the library record's records of that library type come along, in their arranged order, level by level. One copy makes at most 300 records.
- **Suggestions as you type (v1.104.0):** a new record at a level with **Copy from** is offered the library records that match what is typed in it. They show under it as **Suggested**, up to five, after three letters and a short pause. The search reads a library record's name and its words, under your own read rules and in the entry's section, as **Copy from** does. Hovering one shows its words. Picking one makes the record from it where it is: its words, name and link to the original, and its number when the library has one. The **X** puts them away for that record. A record already copied from the library is offered none.
- **Changed in the library:** when the original's number or words change, the copied record says so, with **Update from the original** ("Its name, number and wording are replaced with the original's as they read now.").
- **Only an Assembled list item can be copied from.** A List doesn't go into an Assembled list.

#### Shared and scoped records (v1.90.0)

- **A new record takes its parent's primary scope,** at any depth.
- **A shared type's record is always just shared,** whatever primary scope is open, even under a record in one. Only an API call that names a `scopeRootId` for a shared type is still refused.
- **Makes offers only types that fit:** under a shared top type, only shared types; under any other, only types that aren't shared. Never the type itself, and never an isolated type, whose records are primary scopes of their own. The server refuses the same on save.

#### Endpoints and limits (v1.94.0)

- `GET /nodes/:id/outline/:fieldId` builds the list for the reader in one request (`structureService.getListOutline`). The record's read rights apply, and a field whose first level makes no records answers 404.
  - **A big list answers in portions (v1.117.0).** The answer then has `portion: { level }`, its `records` stop at the portion level, and each record there that holds something says how much in `holds`. Ask for what those records hold with `?inside=<id>,<id>`: the answer has their records (each whole, whatever its depth), the headings and notes among them, and `portion.inside`, the ids it answered for. When too much is asked for at once it answers for fewer, and the rest is asked for again. `?part=heads` asks for the first portion of any list.
  - **`refs`** (in a portion): the numbers of the items its words point at that it left out, by item id, so a cross reference reads right before its record is read. A made record's own part has it too (v1.119.4), for the items its words point at elsewhere in the list, read from the top record's list.
  - **`self`** (a made record's own part, v1.119.4): `{ record, level }`, the record itself as its top record's list holds it (its number as last saved, its item's words) and its own level there. `outlineSelfLines(outline)` gives its lines, and `outlineLines(outline)` cites the items on the page from it (`outlineNumbersFromSelf`). Not in a light read or an answer to `?inside=`, and not for a top record.
  - In code, `structureService.getListOutline(id, fieldId, userId, { part: 'heads' })` and `{ part: { inside: [ids] } }` do the same, and `{ whole: true }` reads everything whatever the size (not offered over HTTP).
- `GET /nodes/:id/list-refs` (v1.120.0) answers `{ records: [{ id, name, items: [{ id, number, text }] }], cut? }`: the records the entry links to that an Assembled list made, in the order linked, each with its numbered items (its own, then those of the records it holds) as its own list cites them, for the cross references of the entry's own lists (`structureService.getLinkedListRefs`). The entry's read rights apply, and each linked record is read as the reader may read it. `?ids=<item id>,<item id>` answers for those items only. Each list's levels come from its settings: the list is not read.
- `POST /nodes/:id/outline/:fieldId` with `{ "changes": [ … ] }` (1 to 500) saves an edit (`structureService.saveListOutline`). A change is one of:
  - `create`: `id` (a `new-…` id that a later change can name as its place), `parent`, `nodeType`, and optionally `number`, `auto`, `insert`, `items` and `source` (its library record);
  - `update`: `id` and any of `number`, `auto`, `insert`, `items` and `source`;
  - `move`: `id` and `parent`;
  - `order`: `parent` and `ids`;
  - `headings`: `parent` and `headings`, the place's headings, subheadings and notes in order;
  - `remove`: `id`, which goes to Deleted with what it holds.

  `parent: null` is the top. The answer is `{ saved, total, created, problem? }`. Names aren't sent: the server gives them.
- `GET /nodes/:id/outline/:fieldId/copy?level=&from=` (`structureService.getOutlineCopy`) gives the records a Copy from would make, without saving them. `level` counts from 0, and `from` is the library record, whose read rights apply.
- **Freeze** (`POST /nodes/:id/fields/:fieldId/freeze`) freezes what the list shows.
- **Not one transaction:** a save checks everything first, then writes through the normal create, update, move, reorder and delete, each on its own. A change refused part way stops the save, and `problem` says how many went through.
- **The numbers and names a save moves are written together (v1.117.1):** one transaction of about a dozen statements, however many records are renumbered (up to 1,000 a transaction). Each record still gets its own version in its history and its own `structure.node.updated`. Every name that changes is given up first, so no name is refused for being the one the record beside it still has, whichever way the numbers move. A record's own formulas and computed name are worked out from the record as it will be. A type that keeps a value per section, has a value rule, or computes its slug is written one record at a time as before, and so is a renumbering the database refuses whole (a name a record outside it already has).
- **The new records a save starts with are made together (v1.117.2):** one transaction for them all, where each record's own create was 11 trips to the database. Each is checked as a single create checks it, and still gets its first version in its history and its own `structure.node.created`. A record placed under another record of the same save works. It is all or nothing: when one is refused, or a type's create needs more, every record is made one at a time as before and the save says which one stopped it. Made one at a time: a type that keeps a value per section, has a value rule, a reference number or a computed slug; a record whose parent's type limits how many records it holds; a record that writes to another as it is made (a reference written back, one per section, a connection); and a new record that replaces another.
- **Limits** (`OUTLINE_LIMITS`): 8 levels; 2,000 records in one read (a list read whole, or what the records asked for hold), 6,000 in a big list's first read, 10,000 lines and 8 levels of records under the entry; a list of more than 400 records is read in portions; 60 records asked into at once; 400 cross references numbered for a portion; 50 linked records looked at and 600 records numbered for an entry's cross references to them; 30,000 records in the server's own reads (a save, Freeze); 500 changes in one save; 300 records in one copy; 300 headings in one place; merge fields filled in for the first 200 records. When it stops short it says why (for example "It shows the first 2000 records."), and Freeze is refused.

#### Following the records (v1.89.47)

The set holds copies, so a change to a library record's Assembled list item after it was copied doesn't change the set by itself. This is for wording; a copied record follows its original through **Changed in the library** (see [Copy from a library](#copy-from-a-library-v1940)).

- **On the detail page,** someone who can edit the entry sees "Some clauses have changed in their records. Edit to update." whenever a record has new, changed or removed items. A frozen set never shows it.
- **In the editor,** **Update from** *(the record's name)* (beside that record's first copy) appears when there is something to add or update. It says what it will do ("Adds 1 new item and updates 2. 1 item you changed here is kept."), then: copies in the record's new items where they sit in its list (sub-items under their parent's copy); updates copies you haven't changed here; and keeps copies you have changed, each still offering **Update from the original**.
- **Items removed from the record** are marked "Removed from the original", with a **Remove** button. Nothing is removed by itself.
- **An item you remove from the set** is remembered (`leftOut`) and isn't offered again as new.
- **Editing a record through a copy's Edit** also brings in items you added there ("Added 1 new item from …").
- The set's limits still apply (1,000 items, 5 levels): an update that would go past one changes nothing and says why.

#### What was removed (v1.94.0)

Refused as unknown settings: **Shows as** (`shows`), **Current only** (`current`), **Badge** (`badge`), **In effect from** and **until** (`dates`) with the detail page's **Current only** and **As at**, **Start from** (`from`: always the records inside), **Order** (`order`: always as arranged), a level's **Number field** and **Its list** (`numberField`, `listField`), and the older `records`, `collection`, `itemRecords`, `allowCreate` and `outline`. A made record's own wording (`own`) and typed record names went too. Current only, Badge and In effect are planned to come back in a new form.

#### Settings

**Assembled list settings**: **Copy** and **Freeze** (v1.89.50: both off until switched on, `copy` and `freeze`; with Freeze off a set already frozen still shows, an admin can still Unfreeze it, and a new freeze is refused: "Freeze is off for this field."), **Levels** (each a card, see [Levels](#levels-v1940), and **Add level** up to 8), and **Numbering sequences** (when no level makes records; each a **Name**, a **Start**, a style and an **Ending**: "A separate run of numbers, like A-1, A-2."). A made type's own list shows only "Set on *(type)*". No **Default value**, **Format and checks** or **Can't be changed after creating**.

#### Rules

**Set the value** isn't offered: "a list of wording is typed or copied in." Other rules can't test it.

#### In lists and tokens

No filter, sort or table column. No token: the pickers leave it out.

#### Limits and gaps

- **Held while a set is frozen.** While any record holds a frozen set, the field can't be removed, retyped or renamed without **Unlock**.
- **Unfreeze goes** when the module is set up with `disableFieldUnlock`.
- **Only on a node type or field template**, at the top level.
- **Wording in one list goes 5 levels deep at most,** even when the list has more levels.
- See the [known residuals of the Assembled list](/modules/structure/roadmap/#known-residuals-of-the-assembled-list-v1940).

---

## Special

### colour

A colour (v1.81.0). Its label in the editor is **Colour**.

```json
{
  "id": "brand_colour",
  "type": "colour",
  "label": "Brand colour",
  "defaultValue": "#3b82f6"
}
```

#### Holds

A colour as text, stored lower case: a theme name (`chart-1`), a palette name (`rose-500`), a hex code (`#3b82f6`, three, four, six or eight digits), or an `oklch()`, `oklab()`, `rgb()` or `hsl()` colour of numbers, at most 64 characters. The browser and the server use one check (v1.89.8), so a colour can never carry CSS into a page. Anything else is refused: `Field "brand_colour" must be a colour`.

#### On the form

The colour picker, offering the app's palette when an admin has set one in the Style module (**Theme → Colours → Swatches**, needs starter v2.16.11 or later), else Structure's swatches, with a custom colour and a way to clear it. Beside it a hex box (`#rrggbb`, up to eight digits), which says "Use a hex code, e.g. #3b82f6" for anything else.

#### On the detail page

A small swatch and the value as text (`#3b82f6`, `rose-500`). A stored value that isn't a colour shows as text. No **Display type** choices.

#### Settings

No settings box of its own and no **Format and checks**. **Create form**: **Default value** is a text box.

#### Rules

**Set the value** is offered (v1.89.10): a colour, or a copy of another colour field. Other rules can test it with the text tests, on the stored text.

#### In lists and tokens

- **Filter**: no. **Sort**: no. **Table column**: yes, as text.
- **Token**: `{brand_colour}`, as stored.

#### Limits and gaps

- **No list filter or sort.**
- **Shown as a code, not a name.** The page shows `rose-500` or `#3b82f6`, not a colour name.
- **The hex box takes hex only.** A palette or theme name comes from the swatches.

### hidden

A value that is typed on the forms but never shown on the entry's page. Its label in the editor is **Hidden**.

```json
{
  "id": "external_id",
  "type": "hidden",
  "label": "External ID",
  "defaultValue": ""
}
```

#### Holds

Text. The server runs none of its value checks on it (not even **Required**), though an **Editable by** lock on it still holds.

#### On the form

A plain text box, with the caption "Not shown on the page" unless the field's description or help text already says it is hidden or not shown. It is left out of the form while it is locked for the person, or while a value rule sets it. The create form starts it with its default, so a new entry saves it; on an edit form with nothing stored, the box shows the default but doesn't save it unless it is edited.

#### On the detail page

Never shown. No **Display type** choices.

#### Settings

No settings box of its own and no **Format and checks**. **Create form**: **Default value** is a text box.

#### Rules

**Set the value** is offered: a fixed text, a copy of a text-like field, or a value put together. Other rules can test it with the text tests.

#### In lists and tokens

- **Filter**: no. **Sort**: yes. **Table column**: yes.
- **Token**: `{external_id}`, as stored. A formula reads it as text.

#### Limits and gaps

- **Required is form only**, and no other check runs on the server.
- **The edit form shows a default it doesn't save** when nothing is stored.
- **It overlaps with the shown switches.** Any other type with **In create form** on and **Show on detail page** off does much the same, with that type's own checks. `isHidden` (no switch since v1.89.14) has no effect on a `hidden`-type field.

### secret

A password or key, with generate, reveal and copy, stored encrypted.

**secretConfig:**

| Property | Type | Default | Description |
|----------|------|---------|-------------|
| `length` | `number` | `32` | Number of random characters in the generated secret (the prefix is added on top) |
| `prefix` | `string` | `''` | Prefix prepended to generated secrets (e.g. `'sk_'`, `'pk_'`) |
| `charset` | `'alphanumeric' \| 'hex' \| 'base64'` | `'alphanumeric'` | Character set for generation |

```json
{
  "id": "api_key",
  "type": "secret",
  "label": "API Key",
  "placeholder": "Click generate to create a key",
  "secretConfig": {
    "length": 40,
    "prefix": "sk_live_",
    "charset": "alphanumeric"
  }
}
```

#### Holds

**The value must be text (v1.38.0).** A secret value is a string; `null` or `''` clears it, and a sealed envelope echoed back from a list read is accepted unchanged. A number, true/false, list or object in a request write is refused with **400** (`must be text`), at the top level and inside field-group rows — only a string is encrypted, so anything else would be stored in the clear. A module writing in-process has a number or true/false value converted to text (and so encrypted) instead.

<Aside type="note" title="Encrypted at rest (v1.19.0)">
Values of `secret` fields are **encrypted before they are stored**, using the starter's
`encryptField` (AES-256-GCM, key from env/KMS, never in the database — see
[Security Feature](/starter-template/security-feature/)). The stored shape is
`{ "__sealed": "<envelope>" }`, so a sealed value is unambiguously detectable.

- **A key is required** (v1.38.0): Structure encrypts secret values at every security tier — even
  where the host's own field encryption is off — so the server needs a ready key provider, normally
  `SECURITY_ENCRYPTION_KEY` (base64, 32 bytes). If that key is missing or invalid, a write carrying a
  new secret value answers **503** with code `encryption_key_missing` instead of an opaque 500, and
  nothing is stored unencrypted.
- **Decryption is narrow**: only the owner's node **detail** read (`GET /nodes/:id`) returns the
