  plaintext. Node list and tree responses deliberately keep the sealed envelope. Secret fields on
  **connection** types are the exception — the connection **list** endpoints unseal too, because a
  connection panel renders its field values inline.
- **Legacy values migrate lazily**: a plaintext value written before v1.19.0 keeps working and is
  sealed on its next write — no bulk migration step.
- **Inherited fields count**: secret detection walks the `superClasses` hierarchy, so a `secret`
  field declared on a parent type is sealed on the child too.
- **Nested secrets count too**: a `secret` sub-field inside a [`field-group`](#field-group) row is
  sealed and unsealed at `data[group][i][subId]` — the field resolver recurses one level into
  sub-fields for exactly this reason (v1.30.1).
- **Undecryptable values are kept** (v1.38.0): an envelope the owner's read cannot open — tampered,
  or sealed under a key the server no longer has — comes back as an opaque
  `{ "__unreadable": "<tag>" }` marker, never the envelope and never an empty string. The edit form
  shows an empty input with a "cannot be decrypted" notice, and the read-only view says *Stored value
  cannot be decrypted*. Until someone enters or generates a new value, saving the entry — including
  re-sending a whole field-group — puts the stored envelope back rather than wiping it, so the right
  key can still recover it.
</Aside>

#### On the form

Renders as a masked `<input type="password">` with three action buttons (**Reveal/hide** and **Copy** appear once the field holds a value):
- **Reveal/hide** — toggle visibility of the secret value
- **Generate** — create a new random secret using the configured charset and length
- **Copy** — copy the current value to clipboard (shows a checkmark on success)

An entry whose stored value can't be decrypted shows an empty box with a "cannot be decrypted" notice.

#### On the detail page

Masked (`••••••••••`) with a reveal toggle, a `secret` sub-field inside a [`field-group`](#field-group) row included. A value that can't be decrypted says *Stored value cannot be decrypted*. No **Display type** choices.

#### Settings

**Secret settings**: **Length** (at least 8; 32 when blank), **Prefix** and **Charset** (Alphanumeric, Hex or Base64). No **Default value** and no **Format and checks**.

#### Rules

**Set the value** isn't offered: "a secret is only ever typed in." Other rules can't test it, and a linked-record rule can't set it.

#### In lists and tokens

- **Filter and sort**: never. A `dataFilters` entry or `dataSortField` naming one is refused with **400** (`is a secret field and cannot be used to filter or sort`): those run on the stored data, so allowing them would leak information about another user's secret. **Table column**: no.
- **Token**: a stored value (a computed name, a merge template, a formula, a badge, a label) may not read it, directly or through a link: see [Computed fields cannot reference secrets](#formula).

#### Limits and gaps

- **Required is form only.** The value-shape rule is the only server check a secret gets, apart from **Editable by** locks.
- **Not per section**, and never searchable: searchable encrypted fields wait on the starter.
- **Needs an encryption key** on the server, or a write with a new secret value is refused with `503`.
- **History shows that it changed, never its value.** An entry's [History](/modules/structure/usage/#history-and-check-integrity-v18934) shows a secret, or a group row holding one, as hidden.

### formula

A read-only value worked out from other fields (v1.33.15). Its label in the editor is **Formula**.

#### Holds

The raw result (a number, text, true or false, or a list), written into the entry's data by the form and again by the server on every write. With **Work it out when shown (not stored)**, nothing is stored.

#### On the form

A greyed-out box with the worked-out value, and its error under it when it can't be worked out.

#### On the detail page

"Label / value", with its **Decimals**, **Prefix** and **Suffix**, and "—" when blank (v1.59.0). Until the value is worked out, the stored value shows. No **Display type** choices.

#### Settings

**Formula settings**: **Formula** (a box with the **Merge fields** button, which opens the Merge fields dialog: a merge template built from rows, or a formula), **Work it out when shown (not stored)**, **Decimals**, **Prefix** and **Suffix**. No **Default value**, no **Format and checks**, and no **Merge Template** in **More**.

#### Rules

**Set the value** isn't offered: "a formula field works itself out." **Visible when** and **Required when** can test it on the forms, but a value rule can't read it (`a rule cannot read the computed field`).

#### In lists and tokens

- **Filter**: no. **Sort**: yes, when it is stored. **Table column**: yes.
- **Token**: `{line_total}` gives its raw value, in a merge template or another formula. The pickers mark it "(formula)".

#### Limits and gaps

- **Worked out when shown** can't be sorted, filtered or read by a badge, because nothing is stored.
- **Required is form only.**
- **Functions the server lacks** (one a module registers only in the browser, or `semanticSimilarity`) fail on the server, so the stored value stays as the form wrote it.
- **Limits on following changes**: at most 200 entries recomputed per write, three levels deep. Past that, values wait until they are next saved.

#### How a formula is worked out

A **read-only calculated field** (added in v1.33.15). Its value is a safe expression over [merge-field tokens](/modules/structure/merge-fields/), configured with `calcConfig`. The form evaluates it as you edit, and since v1.42.0 the server evaluates it again on every write, with the same code, and recomputes it when a node it reads changes (see **Kept current on the server** below). The raw result (a real number, not a formatted string) is written into the node's `data` so downstream formulas, sorting and exports see a genuine value.

The expression language supports operators (`+ - * / % ^`, comparisons, `&& || !`, and `cond ? a : b` ternary) and **function calls**. It has no member access and no code execution — calls resolve only to registered functions by name — and evaluation is bounded (input length, expression size, call depth, work budget, output size).

The bare identifiers `PI`, `E`, `TAU` and `INFINITY` resolve as numeric constants; any other bare name is an evaluation error (`Unknown name`). The bounds are 2000 characters of source, 500 parsed nodes, a call depth of 32, 100 000 evaluation steps and a 100 000-character string result — plus, for the traversal forms below (v1.36.0), a 1000-item cap on any single list and at most 200 backend node fetches per evaluation. Since v1.47.0 an entry a `{ref->field}` token reads counts once per evaluation, however many tokens read it, on the form and the server alike; before, each read counted, so a part with text before it, which reads its reference twice, could run out at half the entries.

**calcConfig:**

| Property | Type | Description |
|----------|------|-------------|
| `formula` | `string` | **Required.** The expression to evaluate, e.g. `round({price} * {qty} * (1 + {tax}), 2)` |
| `precision` | `number` | Decimal places for a numeric result (omit to leave unrounded) |
| `prefix` | `string` | Prepended to the formatted result (e.g. `$`) |
| `suffix` | `string` | Appended to the formatted result (e.g. `%`) |

```json
{
  "id": "line_total",
  "type": "formula",
  "label": "Line Total",
  "calcConfig": {
    "formula": "round({price} * {qty} * (1 + {tax}), 2)",
    "prefix": "$",
    "precision": 2
  }
}
```

**Writing it.** The **Formula** box holds the formula's text. Since v1.60.0 its **Merge fields** button opens the Merge fields dialog: **Merge template** builds tokens from rows (a field, its part, format and join, and text before, after and if empty) and **Insert token** puts them in at the cursor (**Update token** inside one); **Formula** edits the whole formula, with **Insert a field** and **Insert a function**, and **Save formula** keeps it. Since v1.89.26 picking a function opens **Add a function**: its signature (`minutesBetween(from, to)`) and description, then one box per value, labelled with its name and hint. Each box takes a **Field** (the field tree), a **Value** (numbers and true/false as typed, other text quoted for you), another **Function** (up to 3 deep) or a **Formula** as written (`{qty} > 3`, for `if`, `filter` and `map`). Values that repeat get **Add another**. **Insert** writes the whole call at the cursor, commas, quotes and closing bracket included, once every needed box is filled. A line under the formula says why it can't be read yet ("A function needs a comma between its values, or its closing )."). A module's `formulaFunctionRegistry.register({ …, params })` names its values; otherwise they show as value 1, value 2. See [The Merge fields dialog](/modules/structure/merge-fields/#admin-ui--merge-templates).

**Build it (v1.43.0, before v1.60.0).** A formula field's **Formula** setting had **Build it** next to **Advanced**. Formulas it wrote work as before. It put the formula together from parts, in order: text, and field values. A field value can be a field of the type or a built-in, a field of the entry a reference points at, a part of a name or an address, a name in a short format, initials, a select's label, or a date in one of seven formats. One part is stored as it is (`{line_total}`), and several are stored in `concat(…)`, never with `+`, which would add two numbers. A multiple reference joined some other way than with commas becomes `join({others->full_name}, ' & ', ' and ')`. A part with text before, after or if empty (v1.47.0) becomes `isBlank({nickname}) ? 'Not given' : concat('aka ', {nickname})`; with only **If empty, show** the value is left as it is (`isBlank({qty}) ? 'none' : {qty}`), so a number stays a number when it has one. Since v1.60.0 text before, after and if empty, and a join other than commas, are written as [token options](/modules/structure/merge-fields/#token-options-v1600) in a merge template instead. Every part and how it is written is under [Build it](/modules/structure/merge-fields/#build-it-v1430).

**A formula field is a merge field.** Its value is read like any other field's: `{line_total}` in a merge template or in another formula. The merge-field pickers and Build it put "(formula)" after a formula field's label, and after any field with a merge template, as in **Line Total (formula)**, so an admin can see the value is worked out. On the server a formula that reads another one gets the value from the same write (see **Kept current on the server** below). For a stored value, Build it doesn't offer a formula that is worked out when shown, because the stored value would go stale.

The formula's **function library has two tiers**:

1. **Dev-registered functions** — built-in maths (`round`, `min`, `max`, `clamp`, `sqrt`, `pow`, `log`, `sum`, `avg`, `mod`, …), text (`len`, `lower`, `upper`, `trim`, `concat`, `contains`, `replace`, `substr`), logic (`if`, `coalesce`, `isBlank` — v1.47.0, `number`), **list** (`pluck`, `join`, `first`, `last`, `count`, `reverse`, `unique`, `includes`, `sortBy` — v1.36.0; `highest` — v1.42.0) and **similarity** (`levenshtein`, `stringSimilarity`, `jaccard`, `numericSimilarity`). A module can register more via [`formulaFunctionRegistry`](/modules/structure/registries-guide/). Unlike the field-type registry, registering a name that already exists **replaces** the earlier function.
2. **Admin-defined functions** — reusable named expressions authored under **App Structure › Functions** (e.g. `discountedPrice(p, d) = p * (1 - d)`), callable from any formula and from other functions. Stored in the `structure_function` table. A function body works only on its own parameters (and the constants). A `{field}` token inside one is an evaluation error, and the call must pass exactly as many arguments as the function declares. When an admin function has the same name as a dev-registered one, the dev-registered function is called. The **Functions** page refuses a new name that a built-in function or a keyword already uses ("A built-in function already uses this name."). A function saved earlier, or imported, under a name a built-in has since taken (such as `isBlank`, new in v1.47.0) is flagged in the list (v1.47.0): "A built-in function has this name, so formulas use the built-in instead. Rename this one to use it." The server doesn't refuse such a name on create or import.

**`isBlank(value)` (v1.47.0)** is true when a value shows as nothing: no value, text that is blank once trimmed, a number that isn't finite, a name or address whose text is blank, or a list that is empty or holds only blank items. `0` and `false` are not blank. Use it with the ternary, which works out only the side it picks: `isBlank({nickname}) ? 'none' : {nickname}`. Build it writes this shape for a part with text before, after or if empty (see [Build it](/modules/structure/merge-fields/#build-it-v1430)). For a computed name or description, which isn't stored when it works out blank, it stores 'none' where `{nickname}` alone would leave the value already there; a formula field stores whatever its formula gives, blank or not.

**Traversal and collections (v1.36.0).** Beyond one node's own fields, a formula can walk the node graph and operate over the results. Five **traversal keywords** resolve related nodes into *node-views* — one record per node, its built-ins (`name`, `nodeType`, `reference`, …) and its data fields flattened onto a single level:

| Keyword | Returns |
|---------|---------|
| `children([type])` | Direct children of the current node, optionally filtered to one node type |
| `parents([type])` | Ancestor nodes, nearest parent first |
| `scope([type])` | Every node in the current space (the `rootNodeId` subtree) |
| `connections([type])` | *(v1.37.2)* Nodes on the **other end** of each active connection edge, optionally filtered to one connection type; each view also carries the edge's `connectionType` / `connectionId` as built-ins |
| `nodes(ids)` | Hydrates node-reference ids — e.g. `nodes({parties})` — into node-views |

Two **higher-order keywords** operate over any list with a per-item sub-expression, in which `{field}` reads the current item's field and `{_}` (or `{it}`) is the whole item: `map(list, expr)` transforms each item, `filter(list, pred)` keeps the items whose predicate is truthy (`filter(list, 'field', value)` is a field-name shorthand). The pure list helpers `pluck`, `join`, `first`, `last`, `count`, `reverse`, `unique`, `includes` and `sortBy` round them out. A Matter name that disambiguates two parties both surnamed Schulz becomes:

```
join(map(filter(children(), {nodeType} == 'person'),
         concat({surname}, ' ', upper(substr({first_name}, 0, 1)))),
     ' & ')
```

Since v1.43.0 `join` takes an optional third argument, the separator before the last item: `join(names, ', ', ' and ')` gives "Ann, Bob and Cy". A token through a multiple reference, such as `{others->full_name}`, is already a list (one value per entry, blanks left out), so it can be joined without a `map`.

**Aggregation (v1.37.2).** `sum`, `avg`, `min` and `max` aggregate over a list as readily as over loose arguments, so a traversal result reduces to one number:

```
sum(pluck(children('invoice'), 'amount'))     // total of every child invoice
avg(pluck(connections('review'), 'score'))    // mean score across connected reviews
max(pluck(scope('task'), 'priority'))          // highest priority anywhere in the space
count(filter(children(), 'status', 'open'))    // how many children are open
```

They follow spreadsheet `SUM`/`AVERAGE` rules: a **blank** item — a child missing that field — is ignored (so it never sinks an average or a minimum), while a real `0` counts. Over an empty list `sum` is `0` and `avg`/`min`/`max` are blank.

**List literals and `highest` (v1.42.0).** `[a, b, c]` is a list literal, and `[]` is an empty list. `highest(values, order[, fallback])` returns the entry of `order` (listed lowest first) that ranks highest among `values`. Values not in the order are ignored, and when nothing ranks it returns `fallback`, or blank without one. Values match on their display value, so a node-view matches by its name.

Every node-view carries the built-ins `stage` and `status` (`active` or `archived`) alongside its data fields, so a formula can read them and filter on them. Since v1.57.0 a node-view of an entry whose type can link its entries to user accounts also carries `linked` (true or false, linked to anyone), unless the type defines a field called `linked`: `map(children(), {linked|label})` gives Yes or No for each child. A Matter's `court_involvement` field, the highest stage among its non-archived court proceedings, is one formula:

```
highest(pluck(filter(children('court_proceeding'), 'status', 'active'), 'stage'), ['none', 'concluded', 'considering', 'preparing', 'active'], 'none')
```

**Dates (v1.42.1).** `today()` gives today's date, `age({date_of_birth})` the whole years since a date, and `yearsBetween(a, b)`, `monthsBetween(a, b)` and `daysBetween(a, b)` the whole years, months or days between two dates (negative when `b` is earlier). Dates are `YYYY-MM-DD`; a datetime counts by its date part, and blank or invalid input gives blank. A 29 February birthday counts on 28 February in other years. **Times (v1.89.20):** `minutesBetween(a, b)` and `hoursBetween(a, b)` give the whole minutes or hours from `a` to `b` (negative when `b` is earlier). Each takes a time (`HH:MM`), a date and time, or a date. Two times, or a time and a date and time, compare their times of day as written, for example `minutesBetween({scheduled}, {arrived})` for how late someone was. Two dates and times compare the moments, across midnight too. Blank or invalid input gives blank. `today()` is the local date where the formula runs: the browser's in the app, the server's on the server (UTC unless the server sets a time zone). `formatDate(date, preset)` (v1.43.0) gives a date as text, with English month names on both halves: `long` 14 March 2026, `medium` 14 Mar 2026, `dmy` 14/03/2026, `mdy` 03/14/2026, `iso` 2026-03-14, `monthYear` March 2026 and `year` 2026. The same presets work as a token format, such as `{date_of_birth|long}`.

**Worked out when shown (v1.42.1).** Turn on **Work it out when shown (not stored)** (`calcConfig.evaluate: 'onRead'`) for a value that must never go stale, such as an age. A formula that uses `today()` or `age()` is worked out when shown automatically. Such a value is never stored: the server strips it from writes and works it out on reads (`getNode`, `listNodes`, and the views other formulas read), and the app works it out on forms, the detail view, cards, list rows and the table. A list can't sort or filter by it, because nothing is stored. A stored formula that reads it is fixed when that formula is saved, so it can go stale itself. Connection formulas and computed built-ins are not worked out on read.

**Kept current on the server (v1.42.0).** Before v1.42.0 a formula ran only in the browser, so its stored value went stale when a node it reads changed, until someone saved the entry again. Moving a court proceeding to `active` now updates the Matter's `court_involvement` without anyone editing the Matter.

- **One evaluator.** `utils/formula.ts` is generated into the frontend and the backend from one template, so both halves compute identical values.
- **On every write.** Create, bulk create and update, from a request or a module, compute the entry's own formula fields on the server, replacing what the write carried.
- **Computed built-ins too.** A computed `name`, `description` or `slug` (a merge template or a formula on the built-in field) is computed the same way, from the entry's own fields as the form does. It is written only when it resolves to a value within the field's limits. Since v1.47.0 a name or description that resolves to nothing but spaces counts as no value: neither the server nor the form writes it, so the one typed or already stored stays. A value with text in it is stored as it is, untrimmed. Since v1.46.2 it is written whatever the built-in's `editableBy`: a computed value always follows its template, and **Editable By** only decides who may type a value by hand (a typed value for a locked built-in is ignored, and the computed one is stored). Before, a locked computed built-in was never recomputed, so it fell behind. A computed slug is [slugified](#how-a-slug-is-made-v1430) and, when another live entry holds it, stored with the first free `-2`, `-3` suffix (v1.43.0). A computed name that clashes with a sibling doesn't fail the write: the write keeps the value it carried (or the stored one), and the clash is logged.
- **Formulas that read formulas (v1.43.0).** A formula field that reads another one, as `{total} + 1` reads `total`, is worked out after it, so it gets this write's value whatever the field order. Field-group sub-field formulas are ordered the same way. A cycle is worked out in field order.
- **After a write, the dependants.** The entry's own values are stored before the save answers; its dependants follow in a per-user job, which a person's save waits for up to 1.5 s (v1.89.48). The server recomputes the stored formulas that can see the changed node: its parent (`children`), its descendants (`parents`), the nodes of its space (`scope`), the nodes at the other end of its links (`connections`), and the nodes whose reference fields point at it (`nodes()` and `{ref->field}`). A formula or computed built-in that reads `{parent->field}` (v1.49.0) counts as a `parents()` read, so it is recomputed when an ancestor changes. It finds them from the formulas' text: the keywords used and their type arguments. Creates, updates (a stage change included), archive, restore, delete, move and connection create, update and delete all trigger it. A reorder recomputes the parent only. Since v1.57.0 a change to an entry's [user-account link](/modules/structure/merge-fields/#the-user-account-link-v1570) triggers it too, after the entry's own values that read `{linked}` are worked out again, so a `{ref->linked}` follows.
- **Limits.** At most 200 candidates per dependency and 200 recomputed nodes per write. A recompute that changes a node goes on to that node's own dependants, up to 3 levels deep, and each node is recomputed at most once per write. Bulk archive, delete and restore are batched, so a parent of 20 archived children is recomputed once. Nodes past a cap keep their stored value until they are next saved, and the server logs it.
- **How it is stored.** Only the formula keys that changed are written, as a system write, and `structure.node.updated` fires with `source: 'formula'`. A recompute that fails is logged and never fails the write that triggered it.
- **What it can read.** The server reads as the owner without admin rights, so admin-only types are left out (the form leaves them out too, so the two agree). It never sees a secret value, and it keeps to the host's isolation section plus the shared types. In the form, a host in a section is confined the same way. Through a reference, a token reads the referenced entry's data field first and, when that is empty, only its `id`, `name`, `description`, `slug`, `reference`, `nodeType`, `parentId`, `rootNodeId`, `depth`, `status`, `stage`, `createdAt` and `updatedAt` (v1.45.0, on the form and the server alike; see [Node reference dereference](/modules/structure/merge-fields/#node-reference-dereference)).
- **Functions the server lacks.** A formula calling `semanticSimilarity`, or a function a module registers only in the browser, fails on the server, and the stored value stays as the form wrote it.
- **Existing entries after a change (v1.45.0).** Changing a formula doesn't rewrite the values already stored. Saving the type asks whether to update the existing entries too, and the type page's **Update entries** button works out every stored value again. A formula field stores its raw value, so a change to only its `precision`, `prefix` or `suffix` changes no stored value, although the save still asks. See [Updating existing entries](/modules/structure/configuration/#updating-existing-entries-v1450).

A `computedTemplate` on a custom data field is still resolved only in the form, on every write. The exceptions are updating existing entries (v1.45.0), and a change to the entry's own user-account link (v1.57.0) for a template that reads `{linked}`: both work it out on the server, giving the same text the form gives. Either way, a template that works out blank writes nothing, so the field keeps the value it has. So a data field's template that reads `{parent->field}` or `{ref->field}` doesn't follow a change to the parent or the referenced entry: it updates when the entry itself is edited, or when an admin runs **Update entries**. Use a formula field for a value that must follow it.

**The entry's parent (v1.49.0).** `{parent->field}` reads a field of the entry's parent, with parts and formats (`{parent->full_name|surnameInitial}`, `{parent->court}`), and `{parent}` is the parent's id. It reads the parent as `{ref->field}` reads a referenced entry: within the entry's section, never a secret. On a create form it reads the parent the new entry goes under. A type with a field called `parent` reads that field instead. See [The entry's parent](/modules/structure/merge-fields/#the-entrys-parent-v1490).

**From another module (v1.44.0).** A backend module can run this same evaluator, with the same bounds and the functions the server has, through `serviceRegistry.get('structure')`. `evaluateFormula` works a formula out for one record, as a stored formula is worked out, kept to the record's section and never reading a secret. `structureService.formulas.evaluate` works one out over values the module supplies, where a traversal, `nodes()` or a `{ref->field}` is refused because there is no record. `formulas.check` and `formulas.functions` serve a module's own formula editor. See [Contracts](/modules/structure/contracts/#ai-core-record-reads-and-the-formula-engine).

A node-view is carried **opaquely** — there is still no member-access operator, so a record's fields are only ever read through `{tokens}` inside a `map`/`filter` body, or by name via `pluck` / a 3-arg `filter`, each own-key-guarded. On a brand-new node the structural forms (`children`/`parents`/`scope`/`connections`) return an empty list (there is no id yet); `nodes({refField})` still resolves off the form data, so a name formula works at create time. `map`/`children`/etc. are language keywords dispatched before the function registry, so an admin or module function cannot shadow them.

**Similarity** is the anchor use case — numeric similarity is a plain formula (`numericSimilarity({a}, {b})`), string/set similarity uses `stringSimilarity` / `jaccard`, and **node-to-node semantic similarity** uses `semanticSimilarity({node_a}, {node_b})`, which returns the cosine similarity (0–1) of two nodes' embeddings off the module's [vector search](/modules/structure/). Where embeddings are not enabled, `semanticSimilarity` resolves to nothing.

<Aside type="caution" title="Computed fields cannot reference secrets">
A computed value — a `name` / `slug` / `description` `computedTemplate` or `calcConfig` formula, or a computed data field — is stored **unencrypted** and returned by the public API, so it must not reference a `secret`-typed field. As of **v1.37.3 this is enforced** on save: creating, updating, proposing, approving or **importing** a node type whose computed field references a secret token — `{secret}`, `{group.sub}` or `{ref->secret}` — is rejected with an error naming the field. Since v1.43.0 a part or a format of a secret counts as the secret, as in `{code|year}` or `{ref->secret.x}`. Since v1.47.0 the check reads a formula's own tokens as well as its text, so a curly bracket in a string can't hide one: `concat('{', {secret})` is refused. Build it never offers a secret (through a reference, an id that is a secret on any type the reference can point at is left out). The admin forms flag most cases before you save; the server check is the authoritative one. Since **v1.38.0** it covers:

- every computed field the type's form evaluates — its own, those from attached field templates, and those inherited from parent types;
- every secret those fields can reach — the type's own, its attached templates' and its inherited secret fields; a field-group holding a secret sub-field (its `{group}` token returns the rows, secrets included); and `{ref->secret}` / `{ref->group}` through a node-reference, checked against the allowed types' fields when `allowedTypes` is set and against **every** type's secrets when it is not;
- connection types, whose computed fields are checked against the connection's own fields (there, a secret named `name`, `description` or `slug` counts too — connections have no built-in columns);
- field templates — saving one re-checks every node type and connection type that attaches it — and dependent types: saving a type re-checks the types that inherit from it, and a save that adds a secret re-checks the types whose computed fields could now reach it. The error names the affected type.

At runtime a formula is not handed secret values either. A node fetched by id — for `nodes()` or a `{ref->field}` deref — has every secret-typed field removed (field-group cells included) before the formula sees it, and traversal node-views (`children` / `parents` / `scope` / `connections`) drop every sealed or undecryptable value (v1.37.2). So a dynamic lookup such as `pluck(nodes({refs}), 'aSecret')` — where the field name is a runtime string the save-time check cannot see — resolves to nothing rather than the secret (v1.38.0). If a fetched node's type has not loaded in the browser yet, its data fields read blank and the computed result is not saved until the types load.
</Aside>

<Aside type="note">
`if(cond, a, b)` evaluates **both** branches (function arguments resolve before the call), so for a branch that is expensive (or calls `semanticSimilarity`) prefer the short-circuiting ternary `cond ? a : b`. An unset or non-numeric input makes a formula evaluate to nothing (shown blank) rather than a wrong number — comparisons against it are `false`, matching IEEE/Excel.
</Aside>

---

## Module-provided field types

Any module can add a field type with `nodeFieldTypeRegistry.register(...)`, or through the `fieldTypes` array of its `structureRegistry.register(...)` registration. The full interface and an example are in the [Registries Guide](/modules/structure/registries-guide/#6-nodefieldtyperegistry). In short:

- **Required:** `id`, `moduleId`, `label` and `component`, the edit component, which receives `field`, `value`, `onChange` and `disabled`. **Optional and used:** `category` (the menu heading, default `custom`), `order` (default 50), `inPicker` (`false` keeps the type out of the **Add Field** menu and the **Field Type** picker, while its fields still render and are checked, as Structure's own `multiselect` does), `displayComponent` (the read-only view) and `configSchema` (extra admin options, stored verbatim on the field's `config`).
- **Declared but not read** by Structure today: `icon`, `description`, `validate`, `transformBeforeSave` and `getDefaultValue`. Do that work inside your own component.
- **Ids must be unique.** The registry follows the standard `BaseRegistry` rules: a second registration with an id that is already present is **skipped** with a console warning, unless it passes `allowReplace: true`. Because Structure registers 27 built-in ids, give a custom type its own id rather than reusing one (`media` is left free for a storage module).
- **Server side**, Structure knows nothing about a module's type. It enforces the field's `required` flag, applies the `validation` length and pattern rules when the value is a string, and applies `editableBy` locks. It does no other check on the value's shape.
- A field whose type has no registration any more renders read-only with its value preserved, as described for `media` above.

### media — provided by a storage module

**Structure has no built-in `media` field type.** Structure owns organisational structure, not file storage — per its [scope boundaries](/modules/structure/#scope-boundaries), file attachments belong to a content/document module.

A storage module registers `media` through [`nodeFieldTypeRegistry`](/modules/structure/registries-guide/#6-nodefieldtyperegistry), supplying the uploader, the read-only display and its own `configSchema` (accepted types, max size, multiple). Where no such module is installed, `media` simply does not appear in the admin field-type picker.

<Aside type="caution" title="Changed in v1.21.0">
Earlier versions shipped a built-in `media` entry whose renderer fell through to a plain text `<Input>` — so editing one could overwrite a stored array or object with a string. The built-in type, its `mediaConfig`, and the short-lived `nodeMediaUploadRegistry` (v1.20.0 only) have all been removed in favour of a single mechanism: the field-type registry.

If a node type still references `type: "media"` with no storage module installed, the field renders **read-only with an explanation and its stored value preserved** — it is never bound to an editable text input.
</Aside>

---

## Retired: section break

`section-break` was a divider with a heading. It was retired in v1.67.0: use a section for a heading (Sections and field order, on the Fields tab).

- **It isn't offered** in the **Add Field** menu.
- **An old one shows nothing.** The forms and the page draw nothing for it, and the fields under it stay where they are.
- **The editor hides it and drops it on save.** The type form keeps an old one out of sight, so a new field's id can't take its place, and leaves it out (with its place in the sections) when the type is saved.
- **Imports still accept it**, so an old export imports; it is dropped the next time the type is saved in the editor.

---

## Reference Numbers

*(Added in v1.35.0.)* A node type can issue every one of its entries a short, **server-assigned, immutable** identifier — `MAT-2026-0147` — the handle a person quotes in an email, a letter or an invoice.

This is **not a field type**, and it is unrelated to the [`node-reference`](#node-reference) field above (which points at another node). You never declare it in a type's `fields`, it has no entry in the admin field-type picker, and it stores nothing in the node's `data` — it lives in its own `structure_node.reference` column. Switch it on per node type under **App Structure › (the type) › Basic › Reference numbers**; it is **off by default**, and a type with it off stores `NULL`. Enabling it on a type that already holds entries numbers those entries too, oldest first. See [Configuration](/modules/structure/configuration/) for the format options (`prefix`, `separator`, `padding`, `includeYear`, `resetYearly`), the counter and the import rules.

### How it differs from `name` and `slug`

| Column | Written by | Uniqueness | Can it change? | Use it for |
|--------|-----------|------------|----------------|------------|
| `name` | The user — or the type's `builtInFieldConfig.name.computedTemplate`, which derives it from other fields | Unique among a parent's **live** children — only stops two live **siblings** sharing a name (since v1.43.0 an entry in Deleted doesn't hold its name) | **Yes.** A derived name changes whenever its inputs do, and two unrelated entries can compute to the same string | Display |
| `slug` | The client, on create/edit, or computed from a template or formula | Unique among the user's **live** entries; a blank one is no slug | **Yes.** It is editable, and a computed one follows its inputs. A computed slug that another live entry holds is stored with a `-2`, `-3` suffix instead of failing the write (v1.43.0) | URLs |
| `reference` | The server, inside the node-create transaction | `@@unique([userId, reference])`, and `NULL` on types that issue none (Postgres allows unlimited NULLs in a unique index) | **Never.** No create, update, public-API, bulk or metadata path can set or change it — with one deliberate exception, the import door below | Citing one entry — in an email, an invoice, or another system |

`id` is stable as well, but it is a cuid: opaque, and no use to a person reading it aloud.

### How a slug is made (v1.43.0)

A slug is made URL-safe for you, so a template built from fields with spaces or capitals always gives a valid one. One `slugify(text)` does it on both halves, generated into `utils/formula.ts`:

1. Common accents are transliterated: é becomes e, ß ss, æ ae, ø o, ł l and þ th.
2. It is lower-cased.
3. Each run of anything that isn't a-z or 0-9 becomes one `-`, and `-` is trimmed from both ends.
4. It is cut to 255 characters (`SLUG_MAX_LENGTH`) without leaving a `-` at the end.

"Smith & Jones — Café 2" becomes `smith-jones-cafe-2`.

- **A computed slug** (a template or formula on the built-in slug) is slugified by the form as it computes it and by the server on every write: create, update, bulk create, new entries created from a reference field, and recompute. It is then made free: uniqueness counts live entries only, and when another one holds the slug, it is stored as `base-2`, `base-3` and so on. A stored suffix stays only while another entry of the owner holds the base, live, archived or in Deleted. So a slug built from a title of "Phase 2" (`phase-2`) becomes `phase` when the title is changed to "Phase", while `pat-lee-2` stays `pat-lee-2` as long as `pat-lee` exists, even in Deleted, so `pat-lee` can still be restored with its own slug. The Build it preview shows a computed slug slugified, without a suffix.
- **A typed slug** is slugified by the create/edit form and the entry's **Settings** tab when the URL Slug box loses focus, and again on save. The server doesn't change a typed slug, and one that another live entry holds is still a `409`.
- **A blank slug** (`''`, or only spaces) is stored as no slug (`NULL`). Before v1.43.0 the form's empty URL Slug box was saved as `''`, so a second entry with no slug was "An entry with this slug already exists".

### Where it shows up

- **Node detail header** — a monospace badge beside the name; clicking it copies the reference to the clipboard. Shown to every reader, owner or not.
- **List search** — the list `search` term matches `reference` (case-insensitive substring) as well as `name` and `description`, so pasting `MAT-2026-0147` into the search box finds that entry. A `NULL` reference can never match a substring, so the clause is inert on types (and apps) that issue none.
- **API reads** — it rides on the node list, tree and detail responses, and on the [public REST API](/modules/structure/public-api/).
- **Merge templates and formulas (v1.43.0)** — `{reference}` reads the entry's own reference, unless its type has a data field called `reference`, and `{ref->reference}` reads the reference of the entry a reference field points at. A computed name or slug can include it: the server works it out on the first save, once the number is issued. Build it offers it as **Reference number**. Before v1.43.0 a `{reference}` token resolved to nothing.

Since v1.48.0 it is one of the list table's columns, after the name. It can't be a card field or a list filter, which read keys from the node's `data`.

<Aside type="caution" title="The one door that writes it">
`POST /nodes/bulk` — the module's import door — accepts an optional `reference` per row so migrated
records keep the numbers they already had. It is honoured only for an admin session or an in-process
system caller — any other caller's row fails with that message in the response's per-row `errors` array,
and the request itself still returns **201**, since bulk create collects failures per row. The value must
match `^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._/-]*$` and be at most
100 characters, and pushes the type's counter past itself so a later assignment cannot reissue it.
`POST /nodes` strips the key outright, and no update schema carries it at all.
</Aside>

---

## The Badge (v1.54.0)

A node type can show a short label next to each entry's name: "You", "Urgent", or a value from the entry. Like a [reference number](#reference-numbers), it is **not a field type**: it sits with the built-in name, description and slug in the type's `builtInFieldConfig`, as `badge`, and stores nothing on the entry. The admin sets it in the type form's **Badge** block (see [Badge](/modules/structure/usage/#badge-v1540)); the JSON is under [`builtInFieldConfig.badge`](/modules/structure/json-reference/#builtinfieldconfigbadge-v1540).

```json
{
  "builtInFieldConfig": {
    "badge": {
      "rules": [
        { "when": { "field": "linkedToMe", "equals": "true" }, "text": "You", "colour": "#3B82F6" }
      ]
    }
  }
}
```

**How it is worked out.**

- **The first rule that holds wins.** Each rule is a condition (`when`), a text and an optional colour, edited as **If** and **Else if** in the Conditions dialog. When no rule holds, a select field's value gives the badge (below), then `otherwise` (the panel's own box); with none of these, there is none.
- **A badge from a select field (v1.64.0).** **Show a field's value** picks one of the type's select fields, stored as `fromField`: `field`, and `options` keyed by option value, each with its own optional **Badge text** (empty shows the option's label) and colour.
- **The text** is a merge template, resolved against the entry as its computed name is (`{status|label}`, `{client->name}`, `{parent->code}`). It may also read the entry's own `{name}`, `{description}` and `{slug}`.
- **Blank means no badge.** A text that is empty, or comes out as only spaces, shows no badge. A rule that holds with a blank text shows none either: it doesn't fall through to the next rule or to `otherwise`.
- **What shows.** Spaces and line breaks collapse to one space. A result longer than 40 characters is cut, its last character an ellipsis ("…"), and the whole text is the badge's tooltip.
- **Colours.** One of Structure's 12 badge swatches (`#EF4444`, `#F97316`, `#F59E0B`, `#EAB308`, `#84CC16`, `#22C55E`, `#10B981`, `#06B6D4`, `#3B82F6`, `#6366F1`, `#8B5CF6`, `#EC4899`, in any case). Since v1.64.0 a coloured badge is a rounded pill with a soft background in its colour and its text in the colour, mixed toward the theme's text colour so it reads in light and dark mode. No colour gives the theme's outline badge.
- **Per type.** A subtype doesn't take its parent type's badge: like the rest of `builtInFieldConfig`, it isn't inherited.

**Worked out for whoever is looking.** A badge is never stored. Each time an entry is read for a page, the server works its badge out for the person reading. So its conditions can test two targets that no stored rule can:

| Target | In the builder | Holds when |
|--------|----------------|------------|
| `linkedToMe` | **Viewer** is the linked user | The entry is linked to the reader's own user account (on a [profile type](/modules/structure/usage/#profile-types-v1500) whose entries can be linked) |
| `ownedByMe` | **Viewer** is the creator | The reader owns the entry, that is, created it |

A badge can't test **Viewer is an admin** (`viewerIsAdmin`), which a field's **Visible when** can.

They read like a yes/no field: **Is on** is `{ "field": "linkedToMe", "equals": "true" }` and **Is off** is `{ "field": "linkedToMe", "notEquals": "true" }`. A condition can also test the built-ins (`name`, `description`, `slug`, `stage`, `status`), the link to anyone (`linked`), and the type's fields, with any of the [condition tests](/modules/structure/merge-fields/#all-operators). A value can hold a `{token}`, to compare with another field.

- **A defined field wins.** A type with its own field called `linkedToMe` or `ownedByMe` keeps it: conditions read that field, as one called `linked` does.
- **Not in stored rules.** Since v1.60.0 a field's **Visible when**, **Required when** and shown settings may test the viewer too, on the forms and the page only (hiding a field this way is display only). A value rule (`setWhen`) or a reference field's `filter` that tests the viewer is refused when it is saved, because those are worked out on the server and can't depend on who is looking. Since v1.63.0 a badge can also test through a reference (**Parent › Viewer › is › the linked user**), with one batched read per list.
- **Stored values only.** A badge reads what is stored on the entry. A formula with **Work it out when shown (not stored)** isn't read: its value is left out on every read, the editor doesn't offer it, and an admin save refuses a badge that reads one (`Badge, rule 1: it reads "Age", which is worked out only when an entry is shown, so a badge can't use it (a badge reads stored values)`).
- **Never a secret.** A badge shows in lists and pickers, so its texts, the fields its conditions test and their `{token}` values may not read a secret field, directly, through a reference or through the parent: `The badge (rule 1) may not read secret field "x" — a badge shows in lists and pickers, so it would expose the secret`.

**Where it shows.** Beside the name in the detail page's header, list rows and cards, the table view's name column (and a table card's column that is just `{name}`), a page layout's featured and header cards, the entry picker (its options and chosen entry, so the scope selector too), a reference field's linked entries, the tree, and the list's and tree's search results. Not on the breadcrumbs, the Connections tab or the sidebar's entry links. The public REST API, AI Core's record reads and other modules never get a badge, because nobody is looking.

**Instead of "This is me".** A type with a badge of its own (a rule or an `otherwise`) doesn't show the built-in "This is me" mark on its entries; its badge replaces it everywhere. Types without a badge keep "This is me".

**What a save refuses** (a `400`; the configuration import answers `422`, and checks the shape only):

- A badge that isn't an object of `rules` and `otherwise`, or holds another key (`Badge: unknown key "x"`).
- More than 20 rules (`Badge: it has 21 rules, at most 20`).
- A rule that isn't an object of `when`, `text` and `colour`, with no condition (`Badge, rule 1: it needs a condition (when)`), or with a malformed one (`Badge, rule 1: its condition is malformed: …`).
- A text that isn't text, is longer than 500 characters, or leaves a `{token}` open (`Badge, rule 1: the {token} at character 4 of its text is not closed`).
- A colour that isn't one of the 12 (`Badge, otherwise: its colour must be one of the colours offered (…)`).
- A single-condition `contains`, `greaterThan` or `lessThan` whose value holds a `{token}` (`Badge, rule 1: the contains test on "notes" holds a {token}: use rules for a {token} value`). Such a test is skipped when its value is blank, so a `{token}` that came out blank would make the rule hold for every entry. The `rules` form compares a blank value, so use it; the badge editor does.
- On an admin save that brings the badge in or changes it: a condition on something that isn't a field of the type, a built-in, `linked`, `linkedToMe` or `ownedByMe` (`Badge, rule 2: its condition tests "x", which is not a field of this type`), and the on-read formula and secret refusals above.

---

## Field Settings

These settings apply to **every field type**. In the field editor (v1.72.0) each open field is a set of collapsible boxes:

| Box | What is in it |
|-----|---------------|
| **Every field** | **Field Type**, **Name** (what you call it here; it fills the label and id of a new field), **Field ID**, **Section**, **Label**, **Description**, **Permissions** (Anyone or Admin Only), **Can't be changed after creating**, **Keep a separate value in each …** (on a shared type), **Own section** and **Tags** |
| **Create form** | **In create form**, **Required** and **Rules** (one row), **Placeholder**, **Default value**, **Width** (Flex, 1/3, 1/2, 2/3, Full), **Line** (Inline or New line), **Description Position** (Above, Below or Tooltip) and **CSS Class**. A Timestamp has no Create form box |
| **Format and checks** | See [Format and checks](#format-and-checks-v1890). Only for text, long text, email, URL, phone, number, date, date and time, and name |
| **Display** | **Show on detail page** and **Rules**, **Heading** (As the section's, Above, Beside, Hidden), **Display type** where the type has choices, **Width on the detail page** (Full, Half, Auto, Flex, with **Min width** and **Max width** for Auto and Flex), **Filterable** (on a node type, for the types the list page filters by), **Collapsed by default** (with its condition) and **CSS Class (detail page)** |
| *Type* **settings** | The type's own settings, such as **Select settings**: listed under each type above |
| **More** | **Size** (long text only), **Sensitivity** (when the type has classification levels), **Merge Template** (not on a formula, a list of wording or a field in a group inside a group) and **Rules** (every rule on the field) |

The CSS class boxes show only with **Show CSS class boxes** on (at the bottom of the Properties tab), or when they already hold a class. A field-group sub-field, a connection type's field and the AI tab's fields have fewer settings: no shown switches, **Heading** or **Display type**. A connection type's and the AI tab's fields have no **Set the value** either; a sub-field has it only in a node type's or field template's group (see [field-group](#field-group)).

### Identity & Type

| Setting | Type | Description |
|---------|------|-------------|
| `id` | `string` | **Required.** Unique identifier in `snake_case`. Used as the key in the node's `data` JSON object. Since v1.81.0 two fields (or two sub-fields of one group) can't share an id, and a changed id keeps its place and is followed by the rules and tokens that name it |
| `type` | `string` | **Required.** One of the 27 built-in field types listed above, or a custom type registered via `nodeFieldTypeRegistry` |
| `label` | `string` | **Required.** Display label shown beside the input. Since v1.59.0 it can hold merge tokens, such as `{name}`, filled in on the entry's page and in its forms |

### Help Text

| Setting | Type | Default | Description |
|---------|------|---------|-------------|
| `description` | `string` | — | Help text shown near the input on forms. The read-only detail view does not show it |
| `descriptionPlacement` | `'above' \| 'below' \| 'tooltip'` | `'below'` | Whether the description appears above or below the input, or in a tooltip beside the label. A `formula` field or a field with a `computedTemplate` ignores it and always shows the description between the label and the value |
| `placeholder` | `string` | — | Placeholder text inside the input |
| `helpText` | `string` | — | Extra help, shown as quiet text directly under the input on forms: the node create/edit form, the Info tab in edit mode and the Add Connection dialog (a `formula` field or a field with a `computedTemplate` shows it under its value). When it repeats the `description` word for word it is shown once. The read-only detail view does not show it. It has no control in the admin field editor — set it with the Fields tab's **Import Fields** button, through the node type config import/export, or through the API. A module type's `configSchema` entries use it too, where it shows under that option in the admin **Type options** panel |

### Value & Validation

| Setting | Type | Default | Description |
|---------|------|---------|-------------|
| `required` | `boolean` | `false` | Whether the field must have a value. Enforced on the node and connection create/edit forms as of v1.33.19 (submit is blocked and the missing fields are listed), and by the server except on the types marked "Form only" [at a glance](#at-a-glance), on a field with a `visibleWhen` and on a computed field. A [tag](#visibility--behaviour) with **Must be filled in** counts the same. Empty means absent, `null`, `''` or an empty list, so an address or name object counts as filled once any part has been typed, and a yes/no switch once it has been touched |
| `requiredWhen` | `object` | — | Make the field required **only when a condition holds** — same shape as `visibleWhen` (single condition or `rules[]` with `logic`). Combines with `required`; a field hidden by `visibleWhen` is never treated as required. Enforced on the forms only (v1.33.19). In the editor it is a **Required** rule with tests, in the [Rules box](#the-rules-box-v18910) |
| `defaultValue` | `unknown` | — | The value a **new entry** starts with. The node create form opens each top-level field with its default, but only where the form has no value for that field yet, and never on an edit. A field the form may not show, one with `isHidden` or a `visibleWhen` condition, is not pre-filled, because the user couldn't correct a default that no longer fits. The `hidden` type still shows an input, so it is pre-filled. A field [`editableBy`-locked](#visibility--behaviour) for the user is not pre-filled by the form: on a request create — node or connection — the server fills a locked field left absent or empty with its default (v1.38.0), once it has checked the default fits. The Add Connection dialog seeds a new connection from its fields' defaults (v1.14.0). Since v1.45.0 a reference field's **Create new** popup starts the new entry with its type's create defaults too, by the same rules, whether or not the popup asks for the field, and hands them over with the entry; a `node-reference` default is left out there, because a new entry can't hold references until it exists. A default also applies to a field set not to show when creating (`showOnCreate: false`). A create through the API, or by a module in-process, stores only what it submits, plus those locked-field defaults. A default applies only when it fits the field's input: `true` or `false` for a `toggle` or `boolean`, a number for a `number`, an option value or label for a `select` or `multiselect`, an entry id for a `node-reference`, and text for the text-like types. One that does not fit is skipped. An `address` or `name-field` default set as an object, or a `chained-select` one set as a list, through **Import Fields** or the config import, applies too. The admin field editor offers a default for every type except `field-group`, `secret`, `formula`, `address`, `name-field`, `chained-select`, `connections`, `entries-list`, `list`, `assembled-list-item`, `assembled-list` and `timestamp` (which has no **Create form** box), and not for a field with a `computedTemplate`. For `toggle` and `boolean` it is a switch labelled "Pre-checked on new entries". For `text`, `textarea`, `email`, `url` and `phone` it takes merge tokens, such as `{parent->suburb}`, worked out when the create form opens. For the other types it is a text box whose placeholder says what to enter. A new field-group row starts with each sub-field's default |
| `validation` | `object` | — | Validation rules (see below) |

### Format and checks (v1.89.0+)

**Other modules' fields (v1.89.28).** A field another module adds (for example the Tasks module's start time) has **Format and checks** on the Fields tab too, between **Create form** and **Display**, when its type takes a format. The admin's settings go over the module's own: for how it shows, the admin's win (date style, money, case, phone pattern, name order); for checks, a value must pass both, so an admin can add or tighten a check but never loosen the module's ("Tasks checks still apply too."). The format shows everywhere the field does, the server's merge tokens included. Its checks bind what people enter (the forms and the HTTP API), not the module's own writes or system writes, as the admin's Required doesn't. A field a module registers only in the browser is checked in the browser only. A format template used there counts as in use.

Every field of the types below has one **Format and checks** box in the field editor, showing only what fits its type. The same checks run in the form (a message under the field, and Save blocked) and on the server for every write. They're stored as `field.format`.

| Type | Settings in the box | Checked, in the form and on the server | Shown as |
|---|---|---|---|
| Text, Long Text | **Minimum length**, **Maximum length**, **Pattern**, **Pattern message**, **Case** | The lengths and the pattern | In its case (UPPER, lower, Title); stored as typed |
| Email | **Allowed domains**, and the text settings | An email address (built in), at an allowed domain; the lengths and the pattern | As typed |
| URL | The text settings | A web address (built in); the lengths and the pattern | As typed |
| Phone | **Display pattern**, **Must match the pattern**, and the text settings | With **Must match the pattern**, as many digits as the pattern has `#`s; the lengths and the pattern | Through the display pattern (`#` is a digit), such as `#### ### ###`. With a pattern, only the digits are stored |
| Number | **Minimum**, **Maximum**, **Step**, **Decimals** (0 to 4), **Show as** (Number, Currency with a **Currency code**, or Percent) | Minimum, maximum and step | As a number, money or a percentage, with its decimals |
| Date, Date & Time | **Earliest**, **Latest** (Any date, A date, Today, or Days from today), **Show as** | Earliest and latest; the server allows one day either side for "today" | `13 Nov 2026` (the default for a date), `13/11/2026`, `13 November 2026`, or the viewer's browser. A date never shows a time; a date and time shows the browser's form unless a style is chosen |
| Name | **Display order** | Nothing | The order lists, cards and table cells show it in |

Plus one **Message**, said when any check fails (a **Pattern message** wins for the pattern). Time, Timestamp, Select (one pick or several), Chained Select, the yes/no types, Colour, Address, the link and list types, Hidden, Secret and Formula have no Format and checks box.

- **What gets checked.** Only values a write changes are checked, and values copied from a stored record (a merge choice, a linked-record copy) aren't held to new rules.
- **Older settings.** The older `validation`, `numberFormat` and `nameConfig.displayOrder` settings are still read, and are rewritten as `format` when the field is next edited.
- **Values stored by a template.** A name, slug, description, data template or default that is stored reads values as stored (only money formats as before), so existing names and web addresses don't change. Ask for the shown form in a token with `{due|label}`.

**Format templates.** Pick one with **Use a template** to fill the box.
- **The starter set** is built in: Email, Web address, Australian mobile, Australian landline, International phone, Postcode, Money (AUD), Percent, Whole number, Date only, Date and time, and the name orders.
- **Your own** are made on App Structure → **Formats**, and go with the configuration export and import.
- **Overrides.** A field can still change any setting (kept in `format.own`). A changed template updates every field that uses it, except the settings a field changed itself, and is refused when that would contradict a field's own setting.
- **Deleting.** A template a field uses can't be deleted.
- **Storage.** Your templates live in the `structure_format_templates` table, so v1.89.0 needs `npx structure update` without `--skip-schema`.

**Validation object (before v1.89.0, still read):**

| Property | Type | Description |
|----------|------|-------------|
| `min` | `number` | Minimum. On `number` it applies as the HTML `min` attribute (value bound); on `text`, `email`, `url`, and `textarea` it applies as the HTML `minLength` attribute (character length). Enforced on the input by the browser as of v1.20.0 |
| `max` | `number` | Maximum. On `number` it applies as the HTML `max` attribute (value bound); on `text`, `email`, `url`, and `textarea` it applies as the HTML `maxLength` attribute (character length). Enforced on the input by the browser as of v1.20.0 |
| `pattern` | `string` | Regular expression pattern. Applied as the HTML `pattern` attribute on `text`, `email`, and `url` inputs and enforced by the browser as of v1.20.0, and on the server (below). A pattern that could backtrack catastrophically is refused when saved — see **Pattern safety** below (v1.38.0) |
| `message` | `string` | Custom validation message. Surfaced via the input's `title` attribute (native browser tooltip) on `text`, `email`, `url`, and `textarea`, and used as the server's error text when a value fails the `pattern`. Not used on `number` |

The field editor no longer writes `validation`. It is read under `format` (`min` and `max` are a number's bounds, or a text's lengths) and moved into `format` when the field is next edited.

**What the server checks (since v1.33.31).** `POST /nodes`, `PATCH /nodes/:id`, the bulk create path, the public API and a duplicate's data overrides check the data against the type's resolved fields, and refuse the first problem with **400**, naming the field. Since v1.38.0 a request's connection fields are checked too: `POST /connections` (single and bulk) and `PATCH /connections/:id`, which replaces the whole record and so is checked like a create.

- **Required**: on create, and on an update whenever the key is sent, so a required field can't be cleared. Empty means absent, `null`, `''` or an empty list. Not on `secret`, `field-group`, `hidden`, `formula`, `timestamp`, `connections` or `entries-list`, on a field with a `visibleWhen`, or on a computed field. A Numbered or Assembled list also counts an empty list as blank.
- **`number`**: a finite number (numeric text is accepted), then its minimum, maximum and step.
- **`boolean`, `toggle`**: a real `true` or `false`.
- **`select`, `multiselect`**: one of the declared options (when there are any), and a multiselect a list. **Allow Other** takes one typed text up to 200 characters (a multiselect with **Several Others**, up to 20 of them). Since v1.55.0 a field with `optionSources` takes what its typed options and sources can give instead, at most 100 picks, with the records and values a pick names checked too (see [Options from other records](#options-from-other-records-and-values-already-entered-v1550)).
- **`chained-select`**: each pick one of the options, under a picked option (see [chained-select](#chained-select)).
- **`colour`**: a colour (see [colour](#colour)).
- **`date`, `datetime`**: the stored shape (`YYYY-MM-DD`, `YYYY-MM-DDThh:mm`), then **Earliest** and **Latest**.
- **`name-field`**: an object of text parts.
- **Text-like types** (`text`, `textarea`, `email`, `url`, `phone`, `time` and a module's type holding text): the lengths and the pattern (see **Pattern safety** below), the built-in email and web address checks, and a phone number's **Must match the pattern**.
- **`list`, `assembled-list-item`, `assembled-list`**: the shape and the limits, every id once, for every caller.
- **`secret`, `node-reference`, `field-group`**: value-shape checks (v1.38.0): text, record ids, and a list of rows. See [`secret`](#secret), [`node-reference`](#node-reference) and [`field-group`](#field-group). A `node-reference` field also gets the `allowedTypes` check, and a multiple one its `maxItems` (v1.45.0, for every caller).
- **A field group's rows**: each row a write adds or changes is checked cell by cell, by its sub-field's rules, its **Required** included. A row as stored isn't checked again.
- **`connections`, `entries-list`**: no value at all. A write that gives one a value is refused, for every caller.

Since v1.45.0 a type's [value rules](#set-automatically-setwhen) (`setWhen`) are applied before these
checks, so the value a rule sets is the one checked.

On an update (v1.38.0) only the values the write **changes** are checked: a value left exactly as stored
passes, so a value saved before a rule existed does not block an unrelated edit.

Not covered server-side: `requiredWhen` (the submit check is client-side only), fields with a `computedTemplate`, the `formula` and `hidden` types (a `timestamp` is the server's to set), `required` on `secret` and `field-group` fields, and any check of an address's parts or a time's shape. A field carrying a `visibleWhen` is skipped for the **required** check only (it may legitimately be hidden), but its value checks still run. Data keys with no matching field definition are ignored. A module writing in-process isn't refused for a date or a name in another shape, or a value too long to test against its pattern: it gets a warning instead, and the built-in email and web address checks don't bind it.

**Pattern safety (v1.38.0).** Because a field's **Pattern** (`format.pattern`, or an older `validation.pattern`) runs on the server, a pattern that can
backtrack catastrophically — one that can match the same text in very many different ways, such as the
nested repeat `(a+)+` — is **refused when it is saved**, with a 400 naming the field. The check runs on
every path that saves field definitions: node types (field-group sub-fields included), field templates,
connection types, the type and config imports, and node-type proposals and their approval. A pattern
longer than 500 characters, one that is not a valid JavaScript regular expression, and one too complex to
analyse are refused the same way, and one type, template or connection type may carry at most 50
distinct patterns and 2,000 fields (field-group sub-fields included). Everyday shapes — email addresses, URLs, phone numbers, postcodes, IPv4 addresses,
slugs such as `^[a-z0-9]+(?:-[a-z0-9]+)*$` — pass.

- A **stored** pattern the check flags (one saved before v1.38.0) is not run: the server logs a warning
  naming the field and accepts values without the pattern test until the pattern is fixed. The browser
  still applies it as the input's `pattern` attribute.
- A safe pattern runs only on a value of bounded length: up to 100,000 characters when its matching time
  grows linearly with the value (lower, down to 1,000, for patterns with wide counted repeats), and, when it can grow faster (for example `^.+@.+$`), 1,000 characters
  or the field's **Maximum length** if that is longer, never more than 5,000. A longer value in a request
  is refused with 400 (`too long to validate`); a module writing in-process skips the pattern test with a
  warning instead.

### Layout

| Setting | Type | Default | Description |
|---------|------|---------|-------------|
| `order` | `number` | `50` | Sort order on the detail page. Lower numbers appear first — see the note below. A field added in the admin field editor is given `order` = its position × 10 |
| `width` | `'full' \| 'half' \| 'auto' \| 'flex'` | `'half'` | **Width on the detail page** (Display box). `full` spans the row; `half` takes half. `auto` (v1.74.0) is as wide as its content needs, and `flex` takes what's left of the row. The picker shows **Half** when `width` is unset, matching how the page lays the field out |
| `detailMinWidth`, `detailMaxWidth` | `number` (px) | — | v1.75.0+. With `auto` or `flex`: **Min width** and **Max width**. A Min width is where a Flex field wraps |
| `labelPosition` | `'above' \| 'beside' \| 'hidden'` | the section's | v1.89.11+. **Heading**: the field's label on the detail page goes above, beside the value, or is hidden. Absent: **As the section's** (its **Labels** setting) |
| `formWidth` | `'flex' \| 'third' \| 'half' \| 'two-thirds'` | absent: Full | v1.72.0+. **Width** on the create and edit form (Create form box). **Full** is stored as no key; the server refuses any other value |
| `formLine` | `'new'` | inline | v1.72.0+. **Line**: start the field on a new line of the form |
| `section` | `string` | — | Group fields visually on the detail page by assigning the same section name. A type with its own sections (v1.51.0, **Sections and field order**) groups by those instead, and this name only places a field the sections don't list: it joins the section of the same name, at its end |
| `size` | `'small' \| 'medium' \| 'large'` | `'medium'` | For `textarea`, maps to height (as of v1.20.0): `small` = 2 rows, `medium` = 4, `large` = 8. The **Size** picker shows only on a long text field |
| `cssClass` | `string` | — | Custom CSS class on the field's wrapper on the forms (and on the page, when `displayCssClass` is empty). Not applied to `formula` fields or to any field with a `computedTemplate`, which render through their own read-only wrapper |
| `displayCssClass` | `string` | — | v1.72.0+. **CSS Class (detail page)** |

<Aside type="note" title="Where order, width and section apply">
On the **detail page**, fields are grouped by the type's sections (or each field's `section` name) and
laid out with `width`, `labelPosition` and the section's own settings (its layout, labels and tabs). The
detail page as a whole, its header, navigation, sections and side column, is designed on the type's
**Pages** tab (`navigation.detailPage`, v1.65.0). With no sections and no field order, each group is
sorted by `order` (treated as 50 when unset).

The **create/edit sheet** shows each field it shows (see [`showOnCreate` and `showOnDetail`](#show-when-creating-and-on-the-detail-page))
with its `formWidth` and `formLine`, under the type's sections when it has them, in the type's field
order (else the order of its `fields` array, set with the arrows on the Fields tab). `width` and
`order` have no effect there.

**Field order (v1.49.0).** A type can also have its own order of every field its entries have, its
shared templates', parent types' and modules' fields included, stored as `navigation.fieldOrder`. When
it is set, and the type has no sections, the create and edit sheet, the Info tab (each section in
that order, with no `order` sort) and the table's default columns all follow it, so they agree. The
fields it lists come first, in its order; the others follow as they would without it.

**Sections and field order (v1.51.0).** A type can have its own sections instead: **Sections and field
order** on the Fields tab, stored as `navigation.fieldLayout`. Each section has a name and its fields in
order, and the sections are in order too. When a type has them, they decide the grouping and the order
everywhere, over the field order and each field's `section` name: the create and edit sheet and the
Info tab show each section as a heading, in order, then the fields in no section with no heading, and
the table's default columns follow the same order. A field the sections don't list joins the section
whose name matches its `section` (ignoring capitals and spaces at either end), at its end, or else
comes last. While a type has sections, a field's **Section** setting in the admin is a menu of them,
plus **No section**, instead of a free-text name. See
[Sections and field order](/modules/structure/usage/#sections-and-field-order-v1510).
</Aside>

### Admin

| Setting | Type | Description |
|---------|------|-------------|
| `adminLabel` | `string` | **Name** in the Every field box: "What you call it here. Not shown to users." Typing it fills a new field's label, and the id of a field not saved yet. The field list shows `label` |
| `config` | `Record<string, unknown>` | Options for a [module-provided field type](#module-provided-field-types), shaped by that type's `configSchema` and edited in the admin **Type options** panel. Structure stores it verbatim and never reads it |

Three more properties appear on fields the server hands back, but you never set them yourself. `inheritedFrom` (the ancestor type a field was inherited from) and `fieldTemplateId` (the [field template](#shared-field-templates) it came from) are set only on a type's resolved fields, never on stored ones. `templateLocal` is set by the server on a field it copied into a type when a field template's owner was erased (see the note under Shared field templates). Keep `templateLocal` if you rewrite the field object.

### Visibility & Behaviour

| Setting | Type | Default | Description |
|---------|------|---------|-------------|
| `isHidden` | `boolean` | `false` | Older setting, with no switch since v1.89.14: use **In create form** and **Show on detail page** off. A field that still has it shows both switches off, and turning either on clears it. Hide the field from user-facing forms and the read-only detail view. The field still stores data in the node's `data` object, but the input is not rendered. Works on any field type **except** `hidden` itself (see [hidden](#hidden)), `formula` fields and fields with a `computedTemplate`, which render through their own read-only branch and still show |
| `defaultCollapsed` | `boolean` | `false` | **Collapsed by default**: the field shows collapsed under a label and chevron; the user expands it. Works in both edit and read-only, and a whole `field-group` collapses too. Added in v1.26.0. It has no effect on `formula` or `computedTemplate` fields |
| `collapsibleWhen` | condition | — | v1.72.0+. With **Collapsed by default**, collapse it only while the condition holds (**Collapsible** in the Rules box) |
| `notes` | list | — | v1.62.0+. **Show a note** rules: a note under the field or a tooltip beside its label, on the create and edit forms, each with its condition |
| `editableBy` | `'anyone' \| 'admin' \| 'system'` | `'anyone'` | **Permissions** (Anyone or Admin Only). `system` (since v1.47.2) is the same as `admin`, and a stored `system` shows as Admin Only. A field a module supplies can have `'source'`: only its module writes it. **Enforced server-side** — on update since v1.19.0, and on create, duplicate and connection writes since v1.38.0 — see below. Field-group sub-fields have it too |
| `lockAfterCreate` | `true` | — | v1.72.0+. **Can't be changed after creating**: once the record (or row) exists, nobody can change it, admins included; left blank at creation, it stays blank. Not on a worked-out field, a field set by a rule, a one-per-section field, a Timestamp, a Connections field, an Entries list or an Assembled list. Once the type has entries, turning it off needs an admin's recorded **Unlock** (`lockHistory`), unless the module is set up with `disableFieldUnlock`. Since v1.89.17 a section's settings (its cog, **Create form** box) have the same switch for all its fields at once: it sets this on each of the type's own fields in the section that can take it, and lists the ones it leaves as they are (a parent type's, a template's or a module's field, or one that can't be locked). A field added to the section later needs its own switch |
| `perSection` | `true` | — | v1.83.0+. **Keep a separate value in each …** (a shared type's top-level field): each section has its own value. Not on a secret, a link field, a Connections field, an Entries list or a field with a merge template. See [Per-section values](/modules/structure/sections-and-isolation/#per-section-values-v1830) |
| `tags` | tag ids | — | v1.72.0+. **Tags** from the type's own list (`navigation.fieldTags`). A tag with **Must be filled in** makes the field behave as required, while its condition holds |
| `ownSection` | object | — | v1.72.0+. **Own section**: the field gets a section of its own, named after it |
| `computedTemplate` | `string` | — | **Merge Template** (More box): a [merge template](/modules/structure/merge-fields/) that works this field's value out from other fields as an entry is edited. When set, the field becomes read-only. If a field has both a `computedTemplate` and a `formula` `calcConfig`, the template wins. Its box has the **Merge fields** button (v1.60.0). Not offered on a formula or a list of wording |
| `calcConfig` | `{ formula, precision?, prefix?, suffix? }` | — | Config for the [`formula`](#formula) field type — a safe calculation over merge-field tokens + the function library. The field is read-only |
| `visibleWhen` | `object` | — | [Conditional visibility](/modules/structure/merge-fields/#conditional-visibility) rules that show or hide this field based on other field values (a **Visible** or **Hidden** rule on both the form and the page). Since v1.60.0 it can test the viewer, and since v1.89.5 [the entry's stage](/modules/structure/merge-fields/#the-entrys-stage-v1895), in the forms and on the page |
| `showOnCreate` | `boolean` or condition | `true` | v1.45.0+. **In create form**. `false` leaves the field off the create form and a reference field's **Create new** popup, unless the create can't do without it. Since v1.60.0 it can hold a condition (a Visible or Hidden rule on the form). See [Show when creating and on the detail page](#show-when-creating-and-on-the-detail-page) |
| `showOnDetail` | `boolean` or condition | `true` | v1.45.0+. **Show on detail page**. `false` leaves the field off the detail page, unless it is required and empty. Since v1.60.0 it can hold a condition. See [Show when creating and on the detail page](#show-when-creating-and-on-the-detail-page) |
| `setWhen` | `Array<{ when, value }>` | — | v1.45.0+. Value rules (**Set the value** in the Rules box): while a rule's condition holds, the field takes the rule's value, on the forms and on every server write. Since v1.52.0 a value can also be a copy of another field or a value put together. See [Set automatically](#set-automatically-setwhen) |
| `setOtherwise` | a rule value | — | v1.52.0+. The value the rules set when none of them holds. See [Otherwise](#otherwise-v1520) |
| `setSync` | `'automatic' \| 'prompted' \| 'initial' \| 'suggested'` | `'automatic'` | v1.52.0+. When the rules apply: kept up to date, asking first, set once, or (v1.89.22) only suggested. See [When the source changes](#when-the-source-changes-v1520) |
| `setReplace` | `true` | absent | v1.89.10+. A link field to several records: its rules replace what it holds instead of adding to it. See [Set the value on a link field](#set-the-value-on-a-link-field-v18910) |
| `uniqueInScope` | `'section'` | — | v1.45.0+. On a yes/no field: at most one entry per isolation section has it on. See [One per section](#one-per-section-uniqueinscope) |
| `sensitivity` | `string` | — | Classification level for this field, chosen from the node type's `governance.classification.levels`. The picker appears in the admin field editor only when the type has levels configured, and the value round-trips through config export/import. Advisory metadata for consumers today — Structure does not itself redact on it |

<Aside type="tip">
Use the type's sections (**Sections and field order**) to group fields under headings. Combine `formWidth: 'half'` fields to lay out two fields side by side on the form, and `width: 'half'` (or `auto` and `flex`) on the detail page.
</Aside>

<Aside type="caution" title="editableBy is enforced server-side">
A write that sets or changes a locked field is refused with **400** (`Field "x" is not editable`) —
`system` fields for everyone, `admin` fields for non-admins. Before v1.19.0 the lock was
**browser-only**: the form hid the input, but a direct API call wrote the value happily.

- **Update** (`PATCH /nodes/:id`, since v1.19.0) — since v1.38.0 only a **change** to a locked value is
  refused. A value submitted unchanged passes, including the locked cells of a field-group, which is always
  re-sent whole. Field-group rows are compared by their non-empty locked values in order, so adding or
  removing a row whose locked cell is empty is fine, but adding, removing, changing or reordering a
  non-empty locked value is not.
