- **Create** (`POST /nodes`, bulk create, the public API and `POST /connections` single and bulk, since
  v1.38.0) — a locked field may be absent, empty, or exactly its configured `defaultValue`. A top-level
  locked field left absent or empty is **filled with its default**, so a required locked field with a
  default never blocks the create. A required locked field with **no** default cannot be filled by a
  caller it is locked for, so that caller cannot create the entry or connection — the node create form
  lists the field as missing, and the Add Connection dialog explains why it cannot add that type.
- **Duplicate** (since v1.38.0) — data overrides may not change a locked value from the source entry's.
- **Connection update** (`PATCH /connections/:id`, since v1.38.0) — the payload replaces the stored
  fields, so leaving out a locked field that holds a value counts as changing it.

The forms render a locked field read-only — in the Add Connection dialog and inside field-group rows
too. The create, duplicate and connection checks bind request callers; a module writing in-process
through the service is not checked there. Updates are checked for every caller, as before.

A value rule ([`setWhen`](#set-automatically-setwhen), v1.45.0) is not a caller's write. Its value is
applied on every write and wins over a lock, so a rule can set a field locked to `system`.

Two consequences for integrators:
- A module or script that writes a `system` field through the node API must move to a path that
  legitimately owns it (an in-process create through the service, which is not lock-checked, or an
  `admin` lock plus an admin caller). No node update can change a `system` value — `updateNode` checks
  every caller.
- **Public API-key callers are never treated as admins**, so a key cannot write an `admin`-locked
  field regardless of who owns the key.

A field with a `computedTemplate` (or a `formula`-type field) renders read-only in every form, but it
is **not locked server-side**, on create or update, and a computed built-in `name` / `description` /
`slug` is not locked just for being computed. Since v1.42.0 the server computes `formula`-type fields
and computed built-ins itself on every write, so a value the client submits for one is replaced (see
[`formula`](#formula)). A `computedTemplate` on a custom field is still resolved by the client and
accepted as submitted. Since v1.38.0 a computed
**custom** field stays unlocked even when it carries `editableBy: 'admin'` or `'system'`: the forms
auto-submit the computed value, so locking it would refuse every create, and every edit of one of its
inputs. The built-ins differ: an `editableBy` lock on `name`, `description` or `slug` still holds on
update, where the server ignores a change to the locked value rather than refusing the write. What a
computed field may copy is policed instead by the [secret guard](#formula).
</Aside>

### The Rules box (v1.89.10)

In the field editor, every rule on a field is in one list: the **Rules** box in the field's **More** box. It has a line for each of:

- **Visible when** and **Required when** (`visibleWhen`, `requiredWhen`), for example "Visible when Status is Closed" or "Required unless Status is Done";
- the conditions of **In create form** and **Show on detail page** (`showOnCreate`, `showOnDetail`), when they have one. A plain on or off is the switch, not a rule;
- **Collapsible** (`collapsibleWhen`);
- each note (`notes`), as `Note "…"` with its condition;
- the value rules (`setWhen`, `setOtherwise`), as "Set the value when …";
- each rule that changes a linked record started from this field, as "Change a linked record: …".

Click a line, or its Conditions button, to open that rule in the Conditions dialog. **Remove** clears it, as clearing that setting did. With none, the box says "No rules yet."

**Add a rule** opens the dialog on a new, empty rule, every time. Its **Then** offers every action the field can take: **Visible** or **Hidden** (on the create and edit form, the detail page or both), **Required** or **Optional**, **Show a note**, **Set the value** where the field can be set, **Collapsible**, and **Change a linked record**, which makes the rule's Target a linked record. Where **Set the value** isn't offered, the dialog says why in one line, for example "Set the value isn't offered: a formula field works itself out." Since v1.89.19 **Add an action** lists the same actions, in the same order, for every field: one this field can't take is shown greyed with a short reason ("This field holds no value.", "a formula field works itself out.", "Only from a node type's own fields.").

A field holds one Visible when and one Required when (and one of each other yes/no setting). Nothing is replaced without being shown:

- **A new rule with no tests yet** that takes an action the field already has a rule for opens that rule, with the new rule's actions added where they fit. It says "This field already had a rule for that: it's opened here, with your actions added.", or "This field's own rule for that is opened here." when one of them couldn't be added (for example Hidden where the rule shows the field).
- **A rule with tests** that would replace a yes/no setting the field already holds can't be saved. The dialog says "This field already has a Visible when rule. Change it there." with **Open the existing rule**. Every action of the rule is checked, also on a rule opened from the list.
- **Set the value** with tests is added to the field's value rules as one more rule (the first that holds wins). A value with no tests where the field already has an Otherwise waits on **Open the existing rule** too.

The **Create form** box is one row: **In create form**, **Required** and **Rules**. The **Display** box starts with **Show on detail page** and **Rules**. These two **Rules** boxes are one line each ("No rules", the rule, or "2 rules") that opens the same list, kept to the rules that act there: the form's are the In create form condition, Required when, Visible when, the notes and Set the value; the page's are the Show on detail page condition and Visible when. Their **Add a rule** starts on **In the create form** (or **Required**) and **On the detail page**. The rules are stored as before, so existing configurations show their rules in the list unchanged.

### Showing, setting and one per section (v1.45.0)

Four field settings decide where a field shows and what sets its value. They are stored with the field, so there is no schema change, and they round-trip through **Import Fields** and the [configuration export and import](/modules/structure/json-reference/#show-set-and-one-per-section-v1450).

| Setting | Type | Default | Allowed on | Field editor control |
|---------|------|---------|------------|----------------------|
| `showOnCreate` | `boolean` or condition | `true` (shown) | Any field. The forms read it on top-level fields only | **In create form** (Create form box) |
| `showOnDetail` | `boolean` or condition | `true` (shown) | Any field. The forms read it on top-level fields only | **Show on detail page** (Display box) |
| `setWhen` | `Array<{ when, value }>` | no rules | A field of a node type or a field template (top level, or since v1.89.21 a field group's sub-field), of a type a rule can set, with no merge template, that isn't one per section | **Set the value**, in the [Rules box](#the-rules-box-v18910) (**Set automatically** before v1.89.10) |
| `uniqueInScope` | `'section'` | off | A top-level `boolean` or `toggle` field of a node type or a field template, with no value rules | **Only one per …**, named after the isolated type |

Since v1.52.0 the value rules have two more keys, `setOtherwise` and `setSync`, on the same fields as `setWhen` (see [Set automatically](#set-automatically-setwhen)).

The controls are in the field editor of a node type's **Fields** tab and of a field template. A connection type's fields and the **AI** tab's fields don't offer them. The **Rules** tab that edited them from v1.52.0 was removed in v1.64.0: everything it edited is in each field's [Rules box](#the-rules-box-v18910). A field group's sub-fields offer **Set the value** too (v1.89.21), worked out row by row (see [field-group](#field-group)).

#### Show when creating and on the detail page

`showOnCreate: false` leaves a field off the create form. `showOnDetail: false` leaves it off the detail page and the edit form. Both default to `true`, and the field editor stores only `false`: switching one back on removes the key. They change only where the field shows. Its value, its default and its value rules still apply. (`isHidden` is different: it hides the field from every form and from the detail page.) Since v1.60.0 either can hold a condition instead, written as a **Visible** or **Hidden** rule on **Create/Edit form** or **Details page** in the [Rules box](#the-rules-box-v18910).

**Editing (v1.71.0).** In the edit form and the page's edit mode, a field whose `showOnCreate` is on or absent always shows, and a condition there applies to creating and editing alike. A plain `showOnCreate: false` means "fill in later": while editing, it follows `showOnDetail`.

```json
[
  { "id": "notes", "type": "textarea", "label": "Notes", "showOnCreate": false },
  { "id": "intake_code", "type": "text", "label": "Intake code", "showOnDetail": false }
]
```

Notes is filled in later, on the detail page. The intake code is asked for when the entry is made, and then kept off the detail page and the edit form.

- **Where `showOnCreate` applies.** The full create form, and a reference field's **Create new** popup.
- **A field the create can't do without stays on the create form,** whatever `showOnCreate` says. That is a field that is typed in (not a formula or a merge template), that the create fills in no default for, and that is required (`required`, or `requiredWhen` holding) or that the type's computed name is made from. A default on a field with a `visibleWhen` condition, or with `isHidden`, is never filled in, so it doesn't count. Under the switch, the field editor says why the field still shows:
  - "Still shown when creating: it's required and has no default."
  - "Still shown when creating: it's required, and its default isn't filled in on a field that only shows sometimes."
  - "Still shown when creating: the entry's name is made from it."
  - "Still shown when creating while it's required: it has no default." (a `requiredWhen` field)
  - "Still shown when creating while it's required: its default isn't filled in on a field that only shows sometimes."
- **Defaults still apply.** A field hidden on create still gets its create default. The **Create new** popup starts the new entry with it and hands it over.
- **A field that a refused save names** stays shown on the form, create or edit, until the form closes, so it can be fixed.
- **Where `showOnDetail` applies.** The detail page's **Info** tab, read-only and in edit mode, and the edit form. A field set not to show there still shows while it is required and empty, so an edit is never blocked by a field you can't see. Once an edit shows it, it stays until that edit ends, even after it is filled in. Cards and list rows aren't affected, and a field chosen as a table column still shows in the table. Since v1.48.0, a type with no table or card columns leaves such a field out of the table's default columns (its first five fields shown on the detail page).
- **When an entry has fields but none is shown,** the detail page says "None of its fields are shown on this page."
- **What the server checks.** Only that each key is `true`, `false` or a well-formed condition. Anything else, `null` included, is a `400`: `Field "notes": showOnCreate must be true, false or a condition` (the same for `showOnDetail`; a sub-field is named `group.sub`). A sub-field may carry the keys, but the forms don't read them there, and on a connection type's field they change nothing.

#### Set automatically (`setWhen`)

`setWhen` is a list of **value rules**. While a rule's condition holds, the field takes the rule's value, on the forms and again on the server on every write.

```json
{
  "id": "person_category",
  "type": "select",
  "label": "Category",
  "options": [
    { "value": "family", "label": "Family" },
    { "value": "friend", "label": "Friend" },
    { "value": "other", "label": "Other" }
  ],
  "setWhen": [
    { "when": { "field": "is_me", "equals": "true" }, "value": "family" }
  ]
}
```

While **This is me** (`is_me`, a toggle) is ticked, the category is Family, and the form shows it read-only with "Set to Family while This is me is on."

- **A rule** is an object with exactly two keys: `when`, a condition in the [`visibleWhen`](/modules/structure/merge-fields/#conditional-visibility) grammar (the single-condition form, or `logic` with `rules`), and `value`, the value it sets. At most 20 rules. Leave the key out, or give `[]`, for none; `null` is refused.
- **The first rule that holds wins.** A rule only sets a value. When no rule holds any more, the field keeps the value it has: nothing is put back. The exception (v1.52.0) is a field with [Otherwise](#otherwise-v1520), which then takes the Otherwise value.
- **A yes/no field in a condition** compares as the text `"true"`. **Is on** is `{ "field": "is_me", "equals": "true" }`, and **Is off** is `{ "field": "is_me", "notEquals": "true" }`, which an unset box matches too.
- **What a rule may set.** A field of type `text`, `textarea`, `email`, `url`, `phone`, `hidden`, `number`, `date`, `datetime`, `time`, `select`, `multiselect`, `boolean` or `toggle`, and since v1.52.0 an `address` or `name-field`, by a [copy](#a-value-a-copy-or-a-value-put-together-v1520) only. Since v1.89.10 also a `colour` field, and a `node-reference` field (see [Set the value on a link field](#set-the-value-on-a-link-field-v18910)). A top-level field, or since v1.89.21 a field group's sub-field (not in a group inside a group or one kept per section). Not a field with a merge template, and not a field that is [one per section](#one-per-section-uniqueinscope). A field that can't be changed after creating, and a cell of a group whose rows can't change, take only **Suggest it**. The Conditions dialog says in one line why **Set the value** isn't offered on any other field.
- **A fixed value must fit the field.**

  | Field type | `value` |
  |------------|---------|
  | `text`, `textarea`, `email`, `url`, `phone`, `hidden`, `date`, `datetime`, `time` | Text, such as `"2026-03-15"` for a date |
  | `number` | A number: `3`, not `"3"` |
  | `boolean`, `toggle` | `true` or `false` |
  | `select` | One of the option values, as text. A field with no options takes any text |
  | `multiselect` | A list of option values. `[]` is allowed |
  | `colour` | A colour, as the field stores one (v1.89.10) |
  | `node-reference` | A record id, or for a link to several a list of ids, no more than its `maxItems` (v1.89.10) |

  It must also pass the field's own `required`, `validation.min`, `validation.max` and `validation.pattern` checks.
- **A record above (v1.119.0).** A rule that sets a value can test a field of the nearest record above the entry of a type: the Field picker lists each type that can sit above it as "*(Type)* above", its fields a level below. Stored as `"field": "^type->field"`. The server finds that record by the tree (the parent, then up, at most 20 records) and reads it as a link's record is read: the same section wall, readable types and secrets left out. With none above, the test reads blank. A type save refuses a type that is not one here (`its condition reads the nearest "type" above, which is not a type here`). Visible when, Required when and the other conditions don't offer it.
- **What a condition may read.** Only the entry's own data fields. Not a built-in (`name`, `description`, `slug`, `stage`, `status`), not the field the rule sets, not a secret, formula or merge-template field, and not a field that has value rules of its own, so rules never chain. In the field editor the condition offers only the fields it may read. The one exception (v1.50.0) is the user-account link, `linked`, on a type whose entries can be linked: see [The link as a condition](#the-link-as-a-condition-v1500). Since v1.52.0 the condition has [every test](/modules/structure/merge-fields/#all-operators), and the builder can hold several.
- **On the forms.** The create form, the edit form, the detail page in edit mode (a rule that already holds when the edit starts included) and the **Create new** popup apply the rules as the values change. While a rule holds, the field is read-only, with a hint such as "Set to Family while This is me is on.", or for a yes/no field "Turned on while …" or "Turned off while …". Since v1.52.0 a copy says where it comes from ("From Parent A's Home address while Lives with Parent A is on."), a put-together value says "Put together from other fields while …", and an Otherwise value ends "when no other rule matches." An **Only set it once** or **Suggest it** field is never read-only. A field the create form hides is set too. A field locked for the user by `editableBy` is left to the server.
- **On the server, on every write, system callers included,** because a rule is part of the type's data definition. That covers create, bulk create, a new entry made in a reference field, update, a duplicate's overrides, a merge's backfill, the entries the reference write-back flags (`syncTargetField`), the entries one per section turns off (a duplicate's copies included), and the entries that an assign, a move or a delete-and-keep takes references out of. The condition reads the entry as it will be: the stored data with the write over it. A copy through a link or the parent, and a put-together value, are worked out on the entry's own writes (create, bulk create, a new entry made in a reference field, update); on the other writes, see [When the source changes](#when-the-source-changes-v1520).
- **The rule's value wins** over a value the write sends, over a locked field's default and over an `editableBy` lock, with no error. It is then checked, sealed and reported in the write's hooks like any other value.
- **A rule whose value no longer fits** its field (an option since removed, say) is skipped with a server warning, never a `400`.
- **Not run by** a restore, which writes no data. **Update entries** runs them since v1.118.3 (an entry a rule would change is saved with nothing else changed); the update of only the changed fields that a type save offers does not.

**What a save refuses.** Each is a `400` naming the field; the configuration import answers `422` naming the item.

- `Field "x": setWhen must be a list of rules`
- `Field "x" has 21 value rules — at most 20`
- `Field "x": a computed field cannot be set automatically` (a field with a merge template)
- `Field "x": a "formula" field cannot be set automatically` (any type a rule can't set)
- `Field "x": a field that is one per section cannot also be set automatically`
- `Field "x": a field set automatically cannot also be locked after creating` (unless it only suggests)
- `Field "group.sub": rows that cannot change cannot hold a field set automatically` (unless it only suggests), and `… a group that keeps a separate value in each section cannot hold a field set automatically yet`
- `Field "outer.inner.sub": a field in a group inside a group can't be set automatically`
- `Field "x": the fields of a connection type cannot be set automatically (setWhen)`
- `Field "x", rule 2: ` followed by one of:
  - `a rule must be an object with a condition (when) and a value`
  - `a rule has an unknown key "k"`
  - `a rule needs a condition (when)`
  - `its condition is malformed: …`, with what is wrong (an unknown key or operator, a single-condition `notIn`, `rules` beside a single condition, more than 100 rules, and so on)
  - `a rule needs a value`
  - `a rule cannot depend on the field it sets`
  - `its condition tests "y" through a link, which a field inside a group can't do` (a sub-field's rule)
  - `a rule reads the fields of the entry, not its built-in "name"`
  - `a rule cannot read the secret field "y"`
  - `a rule cannot read the computed field "y"`
  - `"y" is set automatically itself, so a rule cannot depend on it`
  - `its value must be text`, `its value must be a number`, `its value must be true or false`, `its value must be one of the options`, `its value is not one of the options`, `its value must be a list of options` or `its value holds a value that is not one of the options`
  - `its value does not fit the field — `, followed by the field's own check
  - since v1.52.0, for a copy or a put-together value, one of those under [What a save refuses for a copy or a build](#what-a-save-refuses-for-a-copy-or-a-build-v1520)
- `Field "x", otherwise: ` followed by any of the value refusals above (v1.52.0)
- `Field "x": setSync must be "automatic", "prompted", "initial" or "suggested"` (v1.52.0; `"suggested"` since v1.89.22)

A condition that reads a field the type inherits, or gets from a field template it attaches, isn't checked when it is saved, because the saved list doesn't hold that field. At write time the form and the server read it the same way.

##### Set the value on a link field (v1.89.10)

A **Node Reference** field, to one record or several, can be set by a rule. In the Conditions dialog, **Then set it to** offers **Records picked** (chosen with the field's own picker) or **Copy from another field**: another link field of this entry, **The parent** (the entry's parent itself), a link field of a record the entry links to or of its parent, or since v1.118.2 **The record itself** under "*(Type)* above" (the nearest record above of that type). Since v1.119.0 **Copy from another field** is one levelled list, as the If picker is: the entry's own fields, then a row for each link field, the parent and each type above, each opening its fields; a link that can be copied itself is "The record it holds" in its own row, and the parent "The parent itself".

```json
{
  "id": "household",
  "type": "node-reference",
  "label": "Household",
  "nodeReferenceConfig": { "allowedTypes": ["household"], "multiple": true },
  "setWhen": [
    { "when": { "field": "lives_with_parent", "equals": "true" }, "value": { "copy": "parent->household" } }
  ]
}
```

While **Lives with parent** is on, the entry's Household gets the parent's households.

- **Add or replace.** A link to several records adds the rule's records to the ones it holds. Choose **Replace them** (`"setReplace": true`) to hold only the rule's. A link to one record holds the rule's first record.
- **A copy of the record above itself** (v1.118.2) is `{ "copy": "^type" }`: the nearest record above the entry of that type or one of its subtypes, however many levels up (at most 20). Use it for a link that must reach past the parent, such as a section's link to its Act when the section sits in a Part or a Division. It is found by the tree when the entry is saved, so it works for a record made through an Assembled list. Only a link field can hold it (`it copies the nearest "type" above itself, which only a link field can hold`), the type must be one here (`which is not a type here`) and a record the field takes (`which is not a record the field takes`). With no such record above, the field is left as it is. Existing entries take it on their next save, or all at once with **Update entries** (v1.118.3).
- **A copy of the parent itself** is `{ "copy": "parent" }`, on a type with no field called `parent`. Only a link field can hold it; on any other field the save refuses it with `it copies the parent itself, which only a link field can hold`.
- **The type save checks the records picked.** Each must be a record you can read (yours, or of a shared type), not deleted, and of a type the field takes: `it picks "…", which is not a record here`, `it picks a "type" record, which the field does not take`. A copy of the parent needs a parent the type allows (any type, when it sets none) that the field takes: `it copies the parent, but no parent this type allows is a record the field takes`. The records picked aren't checked inside a configuration import, whose ids are another app's.
- **Checked like a typed link, on every save.** Each record the rule would add (one the entry didn't hold before the save) is looked up again: it must be a record you can read and not deleted, of a type the field allows, inside the entry's section (the [wall](/modules/structure/sections-and-isolation/)), and match the field's filter and excluded types. More records than the field's `maxItems` refuses the rule. So does a rule that comes to no records (an empty copy): a rule never clears a link. In any of these cases the field is left as it is, with a server warning. A rule never links an entry to itself.
- **Its side effects follow.** The new value joins the write before its reference connections and write-back are worked out, so [Also connect the chosen entries](#connecting-the-chosen-entries-v1460) and `syncTargetField` follow it. A save that changes only the stage, name or colour gets a data write for it, so they follow there too. A link field never keeps a separate value per section.
- **When it runs.** On the server, on the entry's own saves: create, bulk create (and import) and update. It isn't run for a new entry made inside another record's reference field (whose own links wait until it is created), by a system write (the reference write-back, one per section's turn-off), a duplicate or a merge, and not again when the record it copies from changes. So **When it applies** offers **On every save** (`automatic`), **Only set it once** (`initial`) and **Suggest it** (`suggested`, v1.89.22); `setSync: "prompted"` is refused: `a link field's rule can't ask first (setSync "prompted")`.
- **On the forms**, the rule is left to the server (except **Suggest it**): its records are never set in the form or sent with it, so a save is never refused for them. The form shows what it will do. A link that adds stays editable, with the records the rule adds shown read-only under it ("Added by a rule when saved"); the detail page says how many. One that replaces, or a link to one record, shows the value it will set, read-only while its rule holds ("When saved, set to the records picked while …").
- **No loops.** As for any value rule, a rule can't copy the field it sets or a field that is set automatically itself, so two rules can't feed each other. `{ "copy": "$entry" }` is not a field and is refused.
- `setReplace` must be `true` or left out (`setReplace must be true (or left out)`).

##### A value, a copy or a value put together (v1.52.0)

A rule's `value`, and `setOtherwise`, is one of three kinds. In the Conditions dialog, **Then set it to** offers **A value**, **Copy from another field** and **Put it together**.

| Kind | Written as | Sets the field to |
|------|------------|-------------------|
| A fixed value | the value itself, as before: `"family"`, `3`, `true`, `["a", "b"]` | That value |
| A copy | `{ "copy": "home_address" }`, `{ "copy": "parent_a->home_address" }` or `{ "copy": "parent->home_address" }` | The other field's stored value, as it is: a whole address or name, a list, an option's value |
| A value put together | `{ "build": "{first_name} {last_name}" }` (a merge template) or `{ "build": "round({price} * {qty}, 2)", "formula": true }` (a formula) | The result, made the field's kind of value |

An object that is exactly one of those shapes is a copy or a build; any other value is a fixed value, so every rule saved before v1.52.0 means what it did.

```json
{
  "id": "home_address",
  "type": "address",
  "label": "Home address",
  "setWhen": [
    { "when": { "field": "lives_with_a", "equals": "true" }, "value": { "copy": "parent_a->home_address" } },
    { "when": { "field": "lives_with_b", "equals": "true" }, "value": { "copy": "parent_b->home_address" } }
  ]
}
```

While **Lives with Parent A** is ticked, the entry's home address is a copy of the address on the entry its **Parent A** field points at, and the form shows it read-only with "From Parent A's Home address while Lives with Parent A is on." This is a child's address copied from the parent they live with.

**Where a copy reads from.** A copy's path is `field`, `link->field`, `parent->field` or, since v1.93.0, `^type->field`, each id made of letters, digits, `_` and `-` (a link field also takes `parent` and, since v1.118.2, `^type`: the record itself, see [Set the value on a link field](#set-the-value-on-a-link-field-v18910)):

- **`field`**: a field of this entry. It follows the condition's read rules: not the field the rule sets, a built-in, a secret, a formula or merge-template field, or a field set automatically itself.
- **`link->field`**: a field of the entry a reference field of this type points at. Through a link to several entries, the copy reads the first one picked (the picker says "(the first picked)"). Through a link the copy can also read the other entry's `name`, `description`, `slug` or `reference`, as text.
- **`parent->field`**: a field of this entry's parent, unless the type has a field called `parent`, which is then the link it reads through. The parent's `name`, `description`, `slug` and `reference` can be read too.
- **`^type->field`** (v1.93.0): a field of the nearest record above of that type (or one of its subtypes): the parent, the grandparent, and so on, at most 20 records up. The copy picker offers it as a group per type that can be above the entry, "*(Type)* above", nearest first, and the form's hint reads "From the *(Type)*'s *(Field)*". With none above, or a record on the way you can't read, it copies nothing. Use it to set a field from the top of a list, however deep: `{ "copy": "^act->jurisdiction" }`. A change on that record updates the entries below it directly, with no chain of parent copies (see [Kept current on the server](#formula)). It can't read a secret field (refused on save), and a condition can't test through it.

A copy reads what a `{ref->x}` token reads: inside the entry's isolation section and the shared types, of the types the owner may read, with secrets taken out. A source outside the section reads as nothing. A copy through a field the type no longer has, or one that is no link, can't be worked out, so the field is left as it is, never cleared.

**Which fields fit.** A copy needs a source of a kind its field can hold. The field picker offers only those, grouped under **This entry**, **Through** each link, and **Parent**.

| The field the rule sets | Can copy from |
|-------------------------|---------------|
| `text`, `textarea`, `hidden` | `text`, `textarea`, `hidden`, `email`, `url`, `phone`, `select`, `date`, `datetime`, `timestamp`, `time`, and a `formula` field through a link or the parent |
| `email` | `email`, `text`, `hidden` |
| `url` | `url`, `text`, `hidden` |
| `phone` | `phone`, `text`, `hidden` |
| `number` | `number`, and a `formula` field through a link or the parent |
| `date`, `time` | The same type |
| `datetime` | `datetime`, `timestamp` |
| `select` | `select`, `text`, `hidden` |
| `multiselect` | `multiselect` |
| `boolean`, `toggle` | `boolean`, `toggle` |
| `address` | `address` |
| `name-field` | `name-field` |
| `colour` | `colour` (v1.89.10) |
| `node-reference` | `node-reference` (v1.89.10, see [Set the value on a link field](#set-the-value-on-a-link-field-v18910)) |

A record's `name`, `description`, `slug` and `reference` count as `text`. Through a link to named types, every one of them that has the field must have it as a kind that fits. Through a link to any type, or a parent of any type, one type whose field fits is enough.

**A copy of nothing** clears the field, except that it never empties a required field. On an update the required field keeps its value. On a create, a required field that a copy or a put-together value will fill isn't asked for up front; if it is still empty once it is worked out, the create is refused with the field's own "required" message.

**Put it together** is for `text`, `textarea`, `hidden`, `email`, `url`, `phone`, `number` and `date` fields. It is written in the Merge fields dialog, as a merge template or a formula (the **Formula** switch). "The result is made the field's kind of value: text, a number or a date."

- **A text field** takes the text. A blank result clears it.
- **A number field** takes a number, or text that reads as one. Blank clears it.
- **A date field** takes text that starts with a date (`2026-03-15`). Blank clears it.
- **Anything else** (text that isn't a number, for a number field), or a formula that fails, leaves the field as it is.
- **What it reads.** Its `{tokens}` read this entry's fields by the condition's read rules (no built-in, no secret, no computed field, no field set automatically itself), and other records through `{link->x}` and `{parent->x}` as a copy does.

Every copy and build must pass the field's own `required`, `validation.min`, `validation.max` and `validation.pattern` checks, as a fixed value does; one that doesn't is skipped with a server warning.

##### Otherwise (v1.52.0)

`setOtherwise` is the value the rules set when none of them holds, in any of the three kinds. With it, the rules are a full if / else-if / else chain: the field editor shows them as "When …", "Or else, when …" and **Otherwise**. A nested "if" is written as more rules, each with all the tests it needs (**Match all of these** or **Match any of these**), in the order they should be tried.

```json
{
  "id": "contact_note",
  "type": "text",
  "label": "Contact note",
  "setWhen": [
    { "when": { "field": "lives_with_a", "equals": "true" }, "value": { "copy": "parent_a->phone" } }
  ],
  "setOtherwise": "Ask the office"
}
```

- **With Otherwise**, the field is always set by its rules: while no rule holds it takes the Otherwise value, and it is read-only (unless it is **Only set it once** or **Suggest it**). A field can have Otherwise and no rules at all, which means "always".
- **Without it**, as before: "When no rule matches, the field is left for people to fill in."
- **In the field editor**, the **Otherwise** switch shows under the rules once there is one.

##### When the source changes (v1.52.0)

`setSync` says when the rules apply, and what happens when the record a copy or a put-together value reads changes: the entry a link points at, or the parent. In the Conditions dialog it is **When the source changes** under any **Set the value** (on a link field, **When it applies**, with **On every save** and **Only set it once**). Leave it out for **Keep it up to date**.

| `setSync` | In the Conditions dialog | What it does |
|-----------|--------------------------|--------------|
| `"automatic"` (the default) | **Keep it up to date** | A copied or put-together value follows its source: when the other entry changes, this field is updated too. The field is read-only while a rule applies |
| `"prompted"` | **Ask first** | When someone saves the other entry, they are asked whether to update the entries that copy it. The field is read-only while a rule applies |
| `"initial"` | **Only set it once** | "Works like a default that depends on the tests: it is set when they first apply, then can be changed by hand." The field is never read-only |
| `"suggested"` (v1.89.22) | **Suggest it** | "Filled in on new entries and rows; people can change it before saving." The field stays editable, marked "Suggested by a rule: change it if needed." |

Where the value can't change once saved (a field that can't be changed after creating, or a cell of an append-only or locked group), the other three are greyed out and only **Suggest it** can be picked.

**Suggest it (v1.89.22).** On the forms, a field (or a group row's cell) that is blank, and was blank when the form opened, is filled in with the rule's value while the rule holds. It stays editable, and once the person changes it (clearing included) the suggestion stops following. A saved value, a default or a start value is never replaced, and saved rows of append-only or locked groups and locked fields on an existing entry are never filled. Saving confirms it: the server stores what was submitted and never enforces the rule. Only when a create, or a new row, is written without the field at all (another module's or the API's write) does the server fill the suggestion in, as a default would. It never fills an update's top-level fields, a system write, a duplicate or a merge, and **Keep it up to date** and **Ask first** ignore it. It's the only choice allowed on a field that **Can't be changed after creating** and in an append-only or locked group, so what is saved first is what then stays. In edit in place only the open field takes a suggestion, and on a link field a suggestion holds the rule's records alone.

**Keep it up to date.** When the other entry is saved, the entries whose rules read it are worked out again, as a formula that reads it is ([formula](#formula)): the same limits (a chain three deep, at most 200 entries) and the same `structure.node.updated` hook with `source: 'formula'`. A system write that changes an entry (the reference write-back, one per section's turn-off, the user-account link), a duplicate or a merge applies its fixed values and own-field copies straight away, and its kept-up-to-date copies and builds just after.

**Ask first.** The entry's own saves set the value as usual. A change to the other entry isn't copied until someone agrees:

- After you save the other entry, in the edit form, with **Save changes** on the detail page, with save and leave, in the save when the page goes away, or in the **Info** tab's auto-save (once, when the edit ends), a question asks "3 records copy this. Update them too?" (or "1 record copies this. Update them too?"): "They copy some of what you just saved. Ticked ones are updated to match; the others keep what they have."
- It lists each entry by name, with its type and the fields that would change, each ticked. **Yes, update them** updates the ticked ones ("2 records updated"); **No, leave them** leaves them all.
- It lists only your own entries that you may read and edit, in your active space and not unassigned, at most 200, and only those whose rule reads through the record you saved: its direct children reading `parent->`, or entries whose link holds it. You need edit permission on the record you saved. Nothing is asked when nothing would change.
- It isn't asked after a save on the **Settings** tab, a bulk edit or an import. After a system write, a duplicate or a merge, the value waits for the entry's own next save.

**Only set it once.** The value is set on the save in which a rule first applies: its condition turns true on that entry (or another rule's does, or Otherwise takes over), or it holds when the entry is made. After that the value is left alone, so a hand edit stays and later saves never reset it. A save that changes the field itself keeps what was typed. On a create, a value that is just the field's default doesn't count as typed. A system write, a duplicate or a merge leaves it for the entry's own next save. It is offered for fixed values as well as copies and builds.

##### What a save refuses for a copy or a build (v1.52.0)

Each is a `400`, after `Field "x", rule 2: ` or `Field "x", otherwise: `; the configuration import answers `422` naming the item.

Checked against the fields saved with the rule, on every save and on the import:

- `its value is neither a copy ({ copy: "field" }) nor a build ({ build: "..." }, formula: true for a formula)`: an object with a `copy` or `build` key that isn't exactly one
- `its value copies from "a->b->c", which is not a field ("field", "link->field" or "parent->field")`
- `its value puts nothing together (its build is blank)`
- `its value is put together, which only a text, number or date field can be`
- `its value must copy another address field: an address field can only be copied` (the same for a name field)
- `a rule cannot copy the field it sets`, or `a rule cannot read the field it sets` for a build
- `a rule reads the fields of the entry, not its built-in "name"`, `a rule cannot read the secret field "y"`, `a rule cannot read the computed field "y"` or `"y" is set automatically itself, so a rule cannot depend on it`, for a copy of this entry's field or a build's token
- `it copies "y", a "number" field, which does not fit a "text" field`
- `it copies through "y", which is not a link to entries`
- `what it puts together does not work: `, followed by the template's or the formula's problem

Checked against the type's other fields (inherited and field-template ones included) and the types a copy reads through, when a node type, a type proposal or a field template is saved. The configuration import doesn't check these, because a type a copy reads may come later in the file:

- `it copies "y", which is not a field of this type`, or `it copies through "y", which is not a field of this type`
- `it copies "y", a "number" field on contact, which does not fit a "text" field`
- `a rule cannot read the secret field "parent_a->pin"`
- `the parent can point at no type, so there is nothing to copy` (or `"y" can point at no type …`)
- `no type the parent can point at has a field "y"` (or `no type "y" can point at …`)
- `it copies the name, which is text and does not fit a "number" field`

A copy the stored type already had isn't checked again when the type is saved, so a change to another type never blocks an unrelated save; at write time a copy whose source no longer works leaves the field as it is. A field template's save checks its copies against its own fields.

**Secrets.** A copy or a build is stored as a computed value is, so the [secret guard](#secret) reads it as one: a node type, field template or import whose rule reads a secret is refused, `Computed field "Home address (set automatically)" may not reference secret field …`. A `parent->x` read is checked against every type the parent could be. At write time a secret never reaches a copy, even in a config written past the check.

**Loops.** A rule can't read the field it sets, or a field that is set automatically itself, so the rules on one entry never go round in a loop. Entries whose rules copy from each other are kept in step only as far as the recompute's chain depth of three.

#### One per section (`uniqueInScope`)

`uniqueInScope: "section"` on a top-level yes/no field keeps it on for at most one entry in each [isolation section](/modules/structure/configuration/#the-hard-wall-around-sections-v1420), that is, each top-level record of an isolated type such as a Matter. Turning it on for one entry turns it off for the others in the same section, in the same save.

```json
{ "id": "is_me", "type": "toggle", "label": "This is me", "uniqueInScope": "section" }
```

- **The value** is `"section"`, the only scope there is. Leave the key out for no limit; `null` is refused.
- **Allowed on** a top-level `boolean` or `toggle` field of a node type or a field template, without value rules on the same field. On a type whose entries are never in a section it is accepted but does nothing.
- **Which entries compete.** Entries in the same live section, of the type that declares the field and of its subtypes that inherit that field. A subtype that declares its own field with the same id competes on its own. Archived entries count; entries in Deleted don't. A shared type's entries never compete, and neither does the section's own top-level record.
- **Entries in no section are left out.** An [unassigned](/modules/structure/configuration/#unassigned-records-v1430) entry isn't counted and is never turned off, so two unassigned entries can both have it on. Once one is assigned, or moved into a section, it counts: if it is on, it turns the field off for the entry that held it there.
- **Which entry wins** when more than one has it on:
  - an ordinary save: the entry saved;
  - a bulk create: the later row;
  - a new entry made in a reference field: the entry the field belongs to (and of several new entries, the later one);
  - an assign or a move: the entries that arrive. A section's top-level record moved under a record of another section brings its whole section, and its entries win there;
  - a merge: the canonical entry;
  - a restore from Deleted: the restored entry comes back with it off if another entry has it on;
  - a duplicate: the copies come back with it off, unless the duplicate's overrides turn it on, and then the copy wins.
- **How it is written.** The other entries are turned off in the same transaction as the save, so a refused save takes it back. It is a system write: no field lock applies, and each entry's own value rules are applied in the same write. Every caller is held to it: requests, in-process modules and system callers. Saves that arrive together queue, and the later one wins.
- **Hooks.** After the save commits, each entry turned off fires `structure.node.updated` with `changes.data` holding the field set to `false`, plus anything its own value rules set, and no `source`. The formulas that read it are then recomputed.
- **In the field editor** the switch is **Only one per Matter**, with the isolated type's own name; several isolated types are joined with "or". Its help says "Turning it on for one entry turns it off for the others in the same Matter. Entries not in one yet are left out." It is shown only for a yes/no field of a type whose entries live in sections. A field template shows it once any type is isolated, named after all the isolated types. While the field has value rules, the switch is greyed out: "Not available while the field is set automatically."
- **Not with the reference write-back.** A field that is one per section may not be the `syncTargetField` of a reference that can point at its type. See [node-reference](#node-reference).

**What a save refuses.** Each is a `400` naming the field; the configuration import answers `422` naming the item.

- `Field "x": uniqueInScope must be "section" (one per section)`
- `Field "x": only a yes/no field can be one per section`
- `Field "x": a field that is one per section cannot also be set automatically`
- `Field "group.sub": a field inside a group cannot be one per section (uniqueInScope)`
- `Field "x": the fields of a connection type cannot be one per section (uniqueInScope)`
- the write-back pair, listed under [node-reference](#node-reference)

#### How they fit together

- **A value rule also sets a field the create form hides.** With the category hidden on create, ticking **This is me** still makes the new entry Family.
- **A required field with no default stays on the create form,** whatever `showOnCreate` says (see above). A value rule doesn't change that: the field shows, read-only while its rule holds.
- **`setWhen` and `uniqueInScope` can't be on the same field,** because a rule could turn back on what one per section turns off. In the field editor, while **Only one per Matter** is on, the Conditions dialog says 'Set the value isn't offered: "Only one per Matter" is on.' While the field has rules, the switch is greyed out with the note "Not available while the field is set automatically."
- **Neither is allowed on a connection type's own fields,** whose writes apply neither. A field-group sub-field takes value rules (v1.89.21) but never one per section. A field template that a connection type attaches may still carry them, for the node types that use the template; on the connection type they do nothing.
- **They are dropped when the field changes.** In the field editor, changing a field's type takes off `uniqueInScope` once the field isn't `boolean` or `toggle`, and takes off `setWhen` once no rule can set the new type. Since v1.52.0 the value rules (`setWhen`, `setOtherwise` and `setSync` together) also go when one of their values can't set the new type: a fixed value on a field that becomes an address, say, or a value put together on one that becomes yes/no. Giving the field a merge template takes them off too. The server would refuse any left behind.
- **The AI tab's fields** offer none of the four.
- **Updating existing entries** does not run one per section. **Update entries** runs the value rules since v1.118.3: see [Updating existing entries](/modules/structure/configuration/#updating-existing-entries-v1450).

### The link as a condition (v1.50.0)

A [profile type](/modules/structure/usage/#profile-types-v1500) whose entries can be linked to user accounts offers the link to its conditions as a built-in yes/no target called `linked`: on while the entry is linked to a user account (anyone's), off while it isn't. It reads like a yes/no field: **Is on** is `{ "field": "linked", "equals": "true" }` and **Is off** is `{ "field": "linked", "notEquals": "true" }`.

```json
{
  "id": "person_category",
  "type": "select",
  "label": "Category",
  "options": [
    { "value": "family", "label": "Family" },
    { "value": "friend", "label": "Friend" }
  ],
  "setWhen": [
    { "when": { "field": "linked", "equals": "true" }, "value": "family" }
  ]
}
```

While the entry is linked, its category is Family. This does what a rule on an `is_me` toggle did, without the toggle.

- **Where it works.** `visibleWhen` and `requiredWhen` on the forms, a value rule's `when` (`setWhen`) on the forms and on the server, and a reference field's [`filter`](#only-some-entries-filter-v1420), where it tests the target entry's link.
- **In the admin field editor,** the Conditions dialog (for Visible when, Required when and **Set the value**) offers it first, named after the type's switch label (for example "This is me"), with **Is on** and **Is off**. **Only entries where…** on a reference field offers it as "Linked to a user account" when a type the field may point at has linking on.
- **On a form** it follows the "This is me" switch as it stands, before the save: on the create and edit forms, a reference field's **Create new** popup and a page layout's "Add a …". An entry linked to another user, which you leave as it is, counts as linked. The detail page reads the stored link.
- **On the server** it reads the stored link. Linking, unlinking, and a link that moves from one entry to another all re-apply the value rules of each entry they change, with the type's sync field, so a rule such as "Family while linked" follows the link. A create with `linkToMe` reads `linked` as true in its own rules.
- **Only a defined field wins.** A type with its own field called `linked` keeps it: conditions read that field, and the builders don't offer the link. Otherwise a `linked` key in an entry's data never counts, and the server drops one from the writes of a type whose entries can be linked, so a write can't fake the link.
- **A type whose entries can't be linked** (a Record, or linking turned off) has no link to read: `linked` is left out of its conditions, so **Is on** never holds and **Is off** always does.
- **Linked to anyone, not to me.** `linked` is the same for everyone who looks. Whether the entry is linked to *you* is `linkedToMe` (**Linked to me**, v1.54.0), which only a type's [badge](#the-badge-v1540) can test, because it changes with who is looking.
- **In templates and formulas (v1.57.0).** The same link is the built-in `{linked}`: "Yes" or "No" in a merge template, true or false in a formula, and "Yes" or "No" with `{linked|label}`. `{ref->linked}` reads it through a reference. Build it lists it in the **Built-in** group under the type's switch label, with the note "Linked to any account". A stored value that reads it is worked out again whenever the link changes. See [The user-account link](/modules/structure/merge-fields/#the-user-account-link-v1570).

---

## Shared field templates

*(Added in v1.29.0.)* A **field template** is a named set of field definitions you author once and attach to any number of node types — so a common bundle (contact details, audit metadata, a standard address block) lives in one place instead of being copy-pasted onto every type.

### Authoring

Create and edit field templates in the **Field Templates** tab of the **App Structure** admin page (`{ name, slug, description, fields }`, where `fields` is a JSON array of the same field definitions documented above). Writes are admin-only, exposed at `/api/v1/structure/field-templates`.

### Attaching

On a node type, open the **Fields** tab and use **Shared Field Templates › Add shared template** to attach one or more templates (stored as `fieldTemplateIds` on the type). Each attached template appears as a card you can expand to preview the fields it contributes, reorder with the up/down arrows, or detach — shown above the type's read-only inherited-field preview.

**Attachment order is precedence.** When two attached templates declare the same field `id`, the one listed first wins.

### A template used as a widget (v1.128.0)

A template's fields show in a section named after the template, or in the sections the template gives them (below). **Use as a widget**, on the Field Templates page, puts the template's form on the record's page of every type that uses the template. It is the builder a structure type has on its Pages tab:

- **Use as a widget.** The switch. Off keeps the other settings.
- **Title.** The heading over the form. `{name}` is the name of the record it sits on. Left empty, it is the section's own heading, or the template's name when the form holds several sections.
- **Fields.** Which of the template's fields the form shows. Left empty, all of them. A field left out is read in its section as before.
- **One page per section.** Each of the template's sections is a page of the form, with Back, Next and "2 of 3"; the last page has Save.
- **Save on pick.** For a form of one select (or a chained select picked one at a time): picking an option saves at once. Not with One page per section.
- **Keep a history.** Each save is kept as its own row, with who and when. The template's fields become one field group whose rows are added on the record's page, newest first (the group's **Add rows on the page** and **Record who and when**). The editor puts the fields in the group, and takes them out again when you switch it off. The settings above don't apply while it is on. A field group, a list, connections and an entries list can't be part of a row.

How it shows:

- **One form, once.** The form sits in the first section of the page that holds one of its fields, and holds the fields of all the template's sections under their headings. Those fields are not shown a second time in their sections, and a section left with nothing to show is put away.
- **The fields are the record's own.** A save changes the record: every lock, rule, check and permission applies, and a refusal shows in the form. What is being typed is kept if the record changes meanwhile.
- **Only for whoever may edit the record.** Everyone else reads the values in their sections. So does the page's own Edit.
- **The type needs sections.** A type with no sections of its own shows the template's fields under its name, without the form.
- **Choose Keep a history before records hold values.** Once a type that uses the template has records, a switch that moves fields in or out of the group is refused.

A type's own section can still be filled in on the page by itself: **Fill in on the page**, in the section's settings on the type.

Before v1.128.0 the template had a **Section** block (later **Its own section**) with a heading, a line under it and a choice of No, On the record and A new entry each time. A template saved then opens with **Use as a widget** on where it was, and its old heading is dropped at its next save.

### Several sections in a template (v1.127.0)

A template is not limited to one section. On the Field Templates page, under the fields, **Sections and field order** works as it does on a structure type: add a section, name it, order the sections and move fields into them. Each field's **Section** setting picks one of them.

- **On a type that uses the template**, each section shows as its own section, in the template's order. Fields in no section still show under the template's name.
- **A section's settings** are behind its cog: **Heading** and **Line under the heading**. They reach every type that uses the template, until that type saves its own for the section. (Until v1.128.0 the cog also had **Fill in on the record's page**; the template's **Use as a widget** above does that now.)
- **Stored on the fields.** A field's `section` is its section's name, and the fields' order is the sections' order. The template keeps each section's settings by name (`section.more`), so two sections can't share a name.
- **An empty section is not kept.** A section with no fields when you save is gone the next time you open the template.

A connection type's **Fields** tab has the same editors (v1.127.0). Its sections have no settings: each one's name shows above its first field in the **Add Connection** dialog.

### Duplicate a template (v1.127.0)

**Duplicate**, at the top of the editor, opens a copy as a new template named with "(copy)", with the same fields, sections and settings. Change what you need, then **Save**. The copy's fields keep the same ids, so give them new ids if one type will use both templates: when two attached templates share a field id, the one listed first wins.

### How template fields merge

Attached template fields become part of the type's **effective fields** — they render on forms and detail pages, and resolve in merge templates — but are **not** persisted into the type's own `fields`. Precedence, with same-`id` shadowing:

**own fields › template fields › inherited (superClass) fields**

A type's own field with the same `id` overrides a template's; a template's overrides an inherited one. Field templates are **attach-only** — they are not inherited through `superClasses`; a subtype that wants a bundle attaches it directly.

<Aside type="note">
Template fields are first-class for **security**: a `secret`-type field in an attached template is encrypted at rest, and an `editableBy: 'admin' | 'system'` template field is enforced on write — exactly like a field declared on the type itself. Since v1.38.0 an attached template's fields resolve for **every** user of the type, whichever admin owns the template, so this holds on app-level types too. Deleting a field template simply stops attaching types from receiving its fields (existing node data is untouched). Erasing the template owner's account is different: the template's fields are first copied into every surviving type that attaches it (app-level types and other admins' types), so those types keep them.
</Aside>
